# Level 2 finale: the tunnels and the TEK machine (second of the two level 2 bosses, end of 3.0)

Status: designed (2026-10-17), the boss fight reworked around a chase (2026-10-19); stage 1 built (2026-10-19, 3.0.63); the tunnels made giant with a living backdrop, the chase on a tiled floor under a still backdrop (2026-10-20); stage 2 built (2026-10-20, data, no version): screen 10 stands on a layered tunnel; stage 3 built (2026-10-20, 3.0.64): the Duplet; stage 4 built (2026-10-21, 3.0.65): the dust of screen 10; screen 11 designed (2026-10-21): the stone that wakes and builds its own way, closed doors as a common mechanic, the fang kit; stage 5a built (2026-10-21, 3.0.66): closed doors; stages 5b and 5c built (2026-10-21, data, no version): the art and the data of screen 11; stage 5d built (2026-10-21, 3.0.67): the stone wakes; stage 5e built (2026-10-21, 3.0.69): the rocks fall back; the fall retuned as a free fall, the music left alone in it and the checkpoint moved to the stone (2026-10-22, 3.0.71); screen 12 designed and its art made (2026-10-23): the far ledge with convoys and a searchlight, the sphere of 200%, two lines of the shuttle; stage 7a built (2026-10-24, 3.0.72): the life sphere, and the terminal keeps a line; stage 7c built (2026-10-24, data, no version): screen 12 stands, before 7b; stage 8 split into 8a (screen 13 as data and art) and 8b (the machine) (2026-10-25); stage 8a built (2026-10-25, 3.0.74): screen 13 stands, the boss track comes in after the tunnel music fades out, the city road on 12 and 13; the machine designed for 8b and its art packed (2026-10-26, `tek-machine.mset`, no version): 169 by 128 units, facing left for good, three heads on a telescopic mast, contact wounds and shoves; named TEK-R13, the Ram, and its entrance designed (2026-10-26); stage 8b built (2026-10-26, 3.0.75): the Ram drives in, stops and puts out its mast, the shield over its body, the contact rule, a first patrol after the lock, and the track of a screen plays on through a death; act 1 designed and stage 9 built (2026-10-27, 3.0.76): the hunter - its eye the only target, 100 lives a grade, bursts by the thirds of its lives with a red lens before each and a rest after - and the machine that presses a hero low on the road and hunts one on the pads; the dead head gives way to a silent sweeper; turret 2, its blast and the charge by heads designed (2026-10-28): the sweeper hit on its whole barrel pack and only from over the roof, its fire by thirds (one sweep, the fork, the ladder), the heads short out from half their lives, medkits on the thirds dropped by the shuttle, and a colourless shock wave from the sweeper's blast that throws the hero clear and sends the five pads off in burning spirals; stage 10a built (2026-10-28, 3.0.77): the sweeper rides to the hero's row, burns red and sweeps it, closes the ceiling over a hero on the road, forks and climbs a ladder by the thirds of its lives, is hit on its whole barrel pack and guarded from below by two honeycomb plates; stage 10b built (2026-10-28, 3.0.78): both heads short out from half their lives; the wave's pads and the start of the chase settled on a stand (2026-10-28): the wrecks fly slow and spin fast, and the chase waits for a hero left of the machine on new pads brought in over it; stage 10c-1 built (2026-10-28, 3.0.79): the sweeper overloads, bursts with a white flash and a low blow; stage 10c-2 built (2026-10-29, 3.0.80): the shock wave runs out over the screen, kicks up the road's dust and throws the hero clear with no wound; stage 10c-3 built (2026-10-29, 3.0.81): the wave knocks the five pads off, each a wreck flying slow and spinning fast in flames, and a death gives them back; the sweeper's end reworked and stage 10c-4 built (2026-10-30, 3.0.82): before it bursts the Ram backs off to the right wall with its barrels red and the alarm, so the wave throws the hero left and the chase starts with the machine at the right - the bridge of new pads dropped; and a hero thrown down inside the hull walks out of it unhurt, through the whole fight; the wrecks and the sweeper's blast retuned after the live run (2026-10-30, 3.0.83): each wreck kicked off hard by the blast, the harder the nearer, then slowed, each with a climb and a spin of its own, and the sweeper bursts with the tank's death over its own low drone; stage 10d built (2026-10-30, 3.0.84): the shuttle drops a medkit in a capsule as either head's lives pass into their next third - the hunter's onto the high pad farther from the hero, the sweeper's onto the road at the wall away from the Ram; stage 15a built (2026-10-30, 3.0.85): the charge by the first two heads - a hidden tank between the heads' wounds and the gauge, late and in uneven pulses, under the ceilings of 30% and 55%; the bunker's part (15b) comes with the bunker. the chase designed anew and the city behind it (2026-10-30): a quiet leash of napalm, pads in chunks, the bunker with its column, the backdrop from elements with a sun in code; stage 11 split into 11a (the belt) and 11b (the backdrop and the fangs), the chase's start and end tied to the bunker, and stage 11a built (2026-10-31, 3.0.86): the Ram drives off after the sweeper and the road slides under it, carrying the hero and the loot; the drifting pads designed (2026-10-31) and stage 13 built (2026-10-31, 3.0.87): lone pads and random climbs to the hatch's height ride in on the road and are let go past the left edge; stage 13b built (2026-10-31, 3.0.87): a medkit on the middle pad of one climb in three, riding its pad out; the fangs decided and stage 11b-2 built (2026-10-31, 3.0.89): spurs of basalt ride by at random behind the play layer from the start of the chase, mostly low, a few tall over the city, never two of those at once; the fight kept in motion to its end and the air phase reworked (2026-11-01): the road ends as the machine takes off, the hero flies on with it on long pads, a fall brings him back on a pad; the bunker redesigned in three phases on a stand (2026-11-01): its shell flies an arc and knocks off the pad it lands on, the machine falls back to the middle in the second third and hunts the hero from behind, stern first, in the last - the brake lurch dropped.; the henshin after the bunker, the throw of the pads at the Ram, the wheel and the takeoff designed (2026-11-04): the world stops for the eclipse and the rite on one heartbeat, restart points before the henshin and at the takeoff. Many small stages, each its own commit and live run. The screen numbers 10-13 are a guess (arena 2 is screen 9) - shift them if the layout differs.

## The idea in one line
A false ending: arena 2 is won, the victory track plays, the player thinks the level is over - and the arrow points right. Tunnels of lunar rock, a giant double-barrel, the moon stone, an energy gauge at 5%, and a TEK machine that cannot be hurt until the hero becomes the moon form - and that runs away from him in the middle of the fight.

## Rules of the finale
- No monsters on the tunnel screens. The emptiness is the threat.
- The boss fights alone - no other enemies help it. The napalm and the drifting pads of the chase are the machine and the room, not helpers.
- Death in the boss fight restarts the fight from its start (the boss screen), not from the tunnels. The battles are short - no checkpoints inside.
- The floor of the boss arena is flat.
- The boss screen is closed on both sides: a wall on the left, no way on to the right - it is the last screen of the level.
- TEK armour = the shared TEK shield of arena 2 (Game.Shield: the pale gold honeycomb, flare on a hit, no sparks). The player has learned it on the cannons: honeycomb means "do not waste bullets". The ricochet of the 2026-10-08 idea is dropped - one sign of invulnerability, not two.
- TEK fire is napalm: sticky orange and black, it burns where it lands. The fire rain and the orbs are the hero's friends and never come from TEK.

## Screens
### The false ending (screen 9, after the trial)
- win.ogg plays to its end; the anxious retro ambient, lunar.ogg ("Lunar Silence", made in Suno, 3 minutes - for the three tunnel screens before the boss), fades in under its last seconds; at its very end the go-right arrow. (The chewed-tape slowdown was turned down - cannot be done.) As built - see "Stage 1 as built".

### Screen 10 - the double-barrel
- A room of lunar rock, the double-barrel on a pedestal, as the super shotgun in Doom 2. No monsters.
- **The gun is big against the hero, not monstrous** (decided 2026-10-17, size settled 2026-10-20). Reason: no close combat with small monsters ahead, and a big boss is hit for sure. A short-range wide spread is exactly the weapon for a 2x2 target. On the pedestal it is 42 units long (three cells and then two were too much); in the hands about twice an ordinary weapon.
- **The door is shut until the gun is taken** (see "Closed doors"): taking it opens the door, and the arrow says so.
- The look: a modern, utilitarian combat double-barrel of a lunar expedition - black polymer and gunmetal, a perforated shroud over the barrels, no wood, no glow, nothing of TEK (the honeycomb is the enemy's language).

### The tunnels are giant
The hero walks along a ledge in a tunnel far bigger than the screen: the backdrop is far away - the opposite wall, the vault, the depth of the tunnel going off into the dark. The playfield (floor, ledges, the pedestal) is near and small against it. Scale is the point: a small hero in a huge empty place.

How the picture says it (learned on screen 10):
- no near ceiling and no near wings: the top of the frame is black, the vault too high to be seen; the far wall rises out of the bottom edge and fades into the dark at the top, its foot never shown - the hero stands high over the floor of the tube;
- the light has a story: sunlight through a small hole high in the vault falls on the floor of the tube far below, and the lit floor lights the wall from below, warm; in a vacuum the shadows are black and nothing glows in the air;
- balance, not symmetry: the light and the interest on one side, the near matter (the TEK casing) on the other;
- even flat light and an even rhythm make wallpaper, not a place.

### The living backdrop (tunnel screens 10-12) - a new mechanic
It is the backdrop itself that lives, not effects laid over the playfield. The screens do not scroll and the backdrop never moves as a whole - no parallax anywhere, the boss arena included.

**Layers for occlusion, not for motion.** The backdrop is two or three still layers - the far wall, the arches or pillars in front of it, the haze between. A layer is a stage wing: something alive can pass behind it. The art is cut into layers at generation time (a prompt per layer), not after; each layer within 2048 px (old cards).

**One event per screen, growing toward the boss** (decided 2026-10-20) - silence, one blow, then a busy world:
- **Screen 10 (the double-barrel) - haze only.** Long bands of levitating dust hanging in the depth, drifting very slowly, lit where a shaft of light crosses them (no wind on the Moon: the dust is charged and hovers - it drifts, it does not billow). Nothing else moves: the first screen after the false ending says "you are tiny and it is empty".
- **Screen 11 (the moon stone) - the stone wakes.** The event of this screen is the stone itself (decided 2026-10-21, in place of the distant rockfall): the dust of the screen stops dead and settles a little when the stone wakes - the stone stops even the air - and the rocks it raises are the motion. See "Screen 11".
- **Screen 12 (the sphere, the shuttle) - the far ledge is busy.** Tiny lights of TEK convoys crawl right along a far ledge, hiding behind the pillars; a searchlight from the city gate at the far end of the tunnel sweeps the far wall (a light texture with additive blend). The shuttle says the entrance is guarded - the backdrop shows it.

**Dropped** (2026-10-20): pulsing veins in the rock (glowing things are the hero's language - orbs, the stone); sunlight crawling through cracks (a lunar day is two weeks long, and the tunnels are dark); the near-plane dust specks, sand streams and periodic tremors of the first proposal (noise next to a living backdrop).

### Closed doors (decided 2026-10-21)
A common mechanic, not a feature of the tunnels: a screen whose way on is shut until something happens on it. The train level will use it too (no way on while a monster lives).
- **Shut** is a list of the level (`"shutDoors": [9, 10, 11]`). The door is invisible, as the 2008 breakthrough: the hero stops at the threshold, as at the door of the trial.
- **Open** is an action of the events (`openDoor`), and it raises the go-right arrow itself: open = the arrow, one action, never two. No sound, as the arrow of stage 1.
- **What opens it** is any condition of the events. A pickup taken is dead to the field, so `allDead` on the pickup's tag is the taking (the Duplet, the moon stone) - no word of its own was needed; `allDead` on the monsters is the train; `trialDone` is there too.
- A restart shuts the door again if the thing is not done (a pickup not taken comes back with the screen).

### Screen 11 - jumping and the moon stone
The stone builds its own way to the hero - and takes it back once it is in his hands. Seen as a sketch (2026-10-21) and accepted.

**The backdrop is screen 10's far wall, slid left** (decided 2026-10-21). A screen to the right, the far wall moves a third of a screen: the hole of the vault and its shaft now stand at the left (about x 160). The scale is sold by the step between two screens, with no parallax at run time. The new right third (outpainted in ChatGPT from the shifted wall; the original pixels kept on the left, the seam blended over 120 px) is darker and turns from basalt columns to horizontal strata - the way to screen 13 begins here. 1440x1080, as screen 10. The dust of screen 10 again, its shaft moved with the hole.

**The grid** (16x12, rows from the top, cells counted from 0):
- the way in: the ledge of screen 10 going on, cols 0-2 on row 11 (floor line 352) - cut out of `s10-room`, so the ledge runs on through the door with no seam;
- column A, cols 4-5, top on row 10; column B, col 7, top on row 8; column C, cols 12-13, top on row 6 - the stone on it; the way out, cols 14-15 on row 11, right against C, cut out of `s10-room` too; the right edge open from row 0 to 10;
- the columns are columnar basalt, as the pedestal of screen 10, rising out of the dark of the pit; a fall into the pit is the 2008 pit (a life, back to the respawn point at cell 2, 11);
- by the hero's own arc, not the table of `JumpReach`: in to A a gap of one up 1, A to B a gap of one up 2, C to the way out a step off its edge, a drop of 5 with no gap - all pass. No gap at the way out on purpose: once the stone is taken the rocks are gone, and a hero dropped into the pit there would come back at the way in with no way up to C and the door - a softlock. B to C is four cells (128 units) up 2, out of reach - the stone cannot be reached without the rocks. Nothing goes round C: it is a wall;
- **`JumpReach` is the safe reach, not the longest** (found on the first live run, 2026-10-21): it keeps the hero's middle a Bound inside both decks, so a gap it calls out of reach may be crossed from the very edge. The arc itself carries the middle 120 units up 0-1 rows, 102 up 2 and 72 up 3 (peak 100 units over the start). C stood three cells (96 units) from B and was jumped without the step; a gap that must not be crossed is checked against the arc.
- the stone stands high and in the dark at the right, away from the shaft: the black glow reads where there is no light.

**The stone wakes** (on the hero landing on B - a new condition, the hero on a cell):
1. half a second of nothing - no sound, the dust stops dead and settles a little;
2. the stone wakes: its rim brightens, the dark thickens round it (SDL_BLENDMODE_MOD), and a ring of darkness runs out over the room - seen where it crosses the lit wall;
3. grit rises out of the pit first - the warning;
4. the rocks rise out of the pit, each at its own delay and pace, to its own height: an ease-out with a slight overshoot, the tilt dying away, grit falling off their undersides. One rock is the step: it stops with its top on row 8 between B and C, 34 units from each (B to it at one level, it to C up 2). The others are decoration at different depths, slowly bobbing; one or two big blocks deep behind, so the rocks do not read as rubble against the giant tunnel. The step does not bob;
5. a low hum grows under it all; the crack of stone as the rocks come free.

The rocks are pads (Levels.Pads): a path from under the bottom edge to their place, one stop, no way back; the ease is the one new thing in their motion. Steps 1.5-2 cells across.

**The stone is taken:**
- the rocks lose their strength and fall back into the pit - the step too: there is no way back; the dust comes back to life;
- the energy gauge at 5% (stage 6);
- the door opens and the arrow comes (`allDead` on the stone's tag -> `openDoor`);
- the checkpoint of the screen moves to the stone's column (`respawn` in the same event): a hero who jumps back into the pit after the rocks have fallen comes back by the stone, not at the way in with no way up.
- **In reserve** (decided 2026-10-21): the eclipse - on taking, the stone drinks the light: the shaft of the vault goes out, the dust sinks into the dark, the backdrop goes black but for the rim of the stone and the gauge. The same tools as the waking at full strength; kept for the finale if the taking needs more.

**Dropped** (2026-10-21): the force field over the way out (a TEK field on natural rock is out of place - the closed door is invisible); the distant rockfall (the stone is the event); a fang shot down by the Duplet into a bridge, and a fang broken by a falling rock (a believable fall needs drawn animation; rigid motion, light and sound are what this game does well).

### The fangs (decided 2026-10-21)
The basalt spurs of `s10-depth` - the "fangs" - are kept and multiplied:
- a kit: one ChatGPT sheet of four or five fangs of different heights and outlines on black, cut by script into separate sprites, each within 2048 px (old cards); neutral rim light, tinted per screen;
- screen 11: two or three fangs at the depth of `s10-depth`, the rocks rise in front of them;
- the chase (stage 11): the fangs ride through from right to left at random, the "posts by the road" that sell the speed. A fang is a separate sprite, nothing to loop. Their speed is tuned live: slower than the floor (they stand deeper - discrete objects need no seamless backdrop) or with it.

### Screen 12 - the far ledge and the sphere (designed 2026-10-23)
A pause before the fight: flat floor, nothing to jump, the shuttle speaks, the far ledge comes alive, and the way on opens only with the sphere. The bumps of the first plan are dropped - a flat screen lets the player look at the tunnel while the shuttle talks.

**The backdrop is screen 11's far wall, slid left again** - the same step of a third (480 px of 1440), the new right third outpainted in ChatGPT: horizontal strata only, the warm light of the vault dies on the left, and at the right edge, at the far end of the tunnel, a small lit gate building on a natural stratum - the brightest point at (472, 217). The word "city" is not said on this level: the gate is "the end of the tunnel", what lies behind it is the next level's first frame. `level2-backdrops.mset`: `screen12`, 1440x1080 (the generation came 1448x1086 and was scaled).

**The far ledge** - the stratum of the backdrop itself, at 217 (611 px), running into the base of the gate; the convoys ride on it. What marks it is `s12-far-lip`, a hair of light on its rim over a soft shadow - the way matte painting shows a far shelf: not its detail, the light on its edge. Screen 12 only (decided 2026-10-24): a ledge sprite of its own, carried onto 10 and 11 as well, was tried and dropped - it read as a plank laid over the wall.

**The pillars** - a kit of four, one generation each from a silhouette, neutral light, tinted per place: `s12-pillar-needle` (A, 43 units tall), `-twin` (C, 38), `-block` (B, 30), `-stump` (D, 23), feet on the ledge. Screen 12 has five: needle at 50, twin at 153, block at 256, stump at 331, the needle mirrored at 395 - each about 20% darker than the wall behind it, so it reads as a silhouette; at the gate end they all but vanish until a convoy light goes out behind them or the searchlight passes. They are what the convoy lights hide behind.

**The convoys** (code, `Beacon` lights in a row): columns of 3-5 lights - a warm headlight in front, a red tail light at the back, an orange flasher now and then - crawling right along the ledge at 8 units a second, two lanes a little apart, the second 20% slower. Drawn under the pillars, over the wall. A column that reaches the gate fades out there. **The start:** on 10 and 11 the ledge is empty; on 12 the first column comes in from the left alone, on the shuttle's first line; then the gap between columns narrows from half the ledge to dense traffic over 45 seconds; the second lane joins from the second column. The clock starts when the hero comes onto the screen, and a death restarts it.

**The searchlight** (code, a new dynamic): a cone of light out of the gate (additive, over `gsBeam` like the go-right arrow) and a spot on the far wall that walks a figure of eight over the left two thirds of the wall in 11 seconds; a pillar under the spot lights up. It lights with the shuttle's first line. It never touches the playfield.

**The playfield:** row 11 is the floor, the floor line 352, flat from wall to wall; the rest is air. Cols 0-9 - lava crust (`s12-crust`, one object, joins `s11-way-out` with no seam, its broken lip 8 units over col 10); cols 10-15 - the made road of TEK: one square tile, `s12-road-tile`, 32 by 32 units, a self-contained plate with a frame on all four sides and a hazard stripe under its rim, repeated six times - a brick, not a seamless strip. The same tile is the floor of the boss arena. The respawn point at cell 1, 11. `"shutDoors": [12]`, the door opens with the sphere.

**The order of events:**
1. The hero comes onto the screen. Half a second later the shuttle's first line; on the same beat the first convoy comes in from the left and the searchlight lights.
2. The second line; the energy gauge blinks.
3. When the second line is typed out the sphere forms.
4. The sphere is taken - health to 10, the door opens, the go-right arrow lights.

**The shuttle** - two lines, header ЧЕЛНОК: "Мастер! В конце тоннеля что-то большое." and "Лёд не ответит. Бей и следи за зарядом камня." - the threat without a name (the machine is the answer to "something"), why henshin does nothing, and that the fight is what charges the gauge (not "hold out": the charge comes from hitting the turrets). The moon form is not named before it happens. The line "the stone fits the suit" of screen 11 is covered by the second line.

**The sphere** (`TLifeSphere`, drawn in code, no art) - the soulsphere of Doom: 200%, health to 10, the five past the divider green. 16 units across by default. A green body (a light spot upper left, dark at the rim) in a soft green halo, breathing (8%), three bands of light running round it inside, two sparks orbiting, a slow sway up and down. **Forming:** about 26 green sparks spiral in from 40-100 units over 1.6 seconds, the sphere grows out of them and flashes. **Taken:** it bursts, the sparks fly into the hero along curves, a flash on him, the pips fill to 10 (the green ones past the divider). The green is the HUD's bonus green. Not an orb: orbs are the hero's projectiles, the sphere is a pickup.

### Screen 13 - the boss arena

**Decided 2026-10-25 for stage 8a, over everything below where they disagree:**
- The backdrop is a generation of its own: screen 12's wall slid left a third was drawn on to the right in ChatGPT, which repainted the kept part too, squeezed across unevenly. It is taken whole, not stitched to the pixels of 12 - the screens change by a cut, and a warp to fit them was the crooked walls of an earlier screen again. The gate stands at 254, 213.7 units, not at the 291 of a slide of a third; the strata go on past it to the right, darker, the light of the gate left behind.
- The far ledge of 13 is its own: the lip, three pillars (block, stump, the mirrored needle - the others fall off the left edge), the convoys riding to the gate and stopping there, and a searchlight that sweeps the whole screen, left of the gate and right of it. They burn from the level's load, not from an event of 13: the traffic is dense the moment the hero arrives, and a death on 13 rewinds nothing of it.
- The floor is the city road, one tile wall to wall, on screen 12 too (cols 10-15): a brick of dark gunmetal with a hex tread, its hazard stripe closed at both ends like the deck of a pad, so it needs no seam. The city pads are its kin: the road's plate as the deck, an engine under it.
- Five city pads stand on screen 13 from act 1 (decided the same day, over a first "none until the chase"), clear of the convoys. The machine is 128 units tall (its top at 224) and 169 long (decided 2026-10-26, see "The machine, decided 2026-10-26"), over the hero's jump - he never jumps it. **It drives the whole floor behind the pads** (a rule for 8b: drawn behind them, and no pad stops it or is moved by it): a hero on a low pad is out of its way. The low pads by the walls: `s13-pad-1` at 16, 280 and `s13-pad-4` at 464, 280, 72 units over the floor. The high ones: `s13-pad-2` at 72, 188 (between pillars b and d, its foot 7 units over the convoys' line at 214), the long `s13-pad-3` at 352, 200 (right of the gate, where no convoy rides) and in the middle, a little higher, the long `s13-pad-5` at 200, 160 - over the machine wherever it stands, to strike it from above and from either side. From a low pad to its high one is 80-92 units up and 24-48 across - the arc carries about 87 across up 80; from either high pad to the middle one, 28-40 up and 88-96 across - about 120 at that height. The convoys' line on the left (y 211-215, x 0-254) is kept free of pads; the jet of `s13-pad-2` crosses it at one point. The convoys' line on the left (y 211-215, x 0-254) is kept free of pads; the jet of `s13-pad-2` crosses it at one point. All five let bullets by (`bullets: pass`): no cover from the turrets, and the hero shoots down through his own pad. The chase's drifting pads (stage 13) are another matter.

**Decided 2026-10-25, over the text below where they disagree:**
- The far ledge of screen 12 goes on onto 13: the lip, the pillars, the convoys and the searchlight, shifted with the backdrop. They stay as they are on screen 12 - a living backdrop through the fight; whether to cut them back is decided after the live run.
- In the chase the backdrop moves, slowly: screen 13's backdrop is a long strip drawn on to the right in ChatGPT with room to spare (not seamless, not looped), each piece within 2048 px, every piece drawn on from the edge of the one before. The far plane moves as one: the backdrop and the `sky` layer with it (lip, pillars, convoys, searchlight), or the pillars come away from their wall. Its speed is set live.
- The gate (the small lit building at the right horizon) stays where it is: the hero does not go there. As the backdrop moves left in the chase the gate drifts left and leaves the screen on the left - he passes it by, and the end of the fight is on another part of the tunnel. The new right part of the strip continues the way: the strata go on, the light of the gate stays behind.

The end of the tunnels and the way out to the city. The backdrop of screen 10 would not do for the chase: its basalt columns are verticals, and a vertical that does not ride past while the floor slides shows the belt for a treadmill. This one: horizontal strata only, no columns, cracks or stalactites running up and down; finer texture and lower contrast, farther than on screen 10; no bright spot in the middle - the light is a distant glow of the city along the horizon at the end of the tunnel. Screens 10-12 lead to it: each a little farther and more horizontal than the one before. Flat tiled floor - a made road, the machine drives on it - closed on both sides. The backdrop is still: no events of its own (the foreground is full - napalm, pads, turrets) and no motion in the chase (see "The belt"). Three acts in three tempos: the machine standing its ground (turrets 1 and 2), the chase (turret 3), then the evolution and the air phase on a still screen again.

## The boss: TEK-R13, the Ram
- Named 2026-10-26: TEK-R13 ("Таран" in the game's Russian), the Ram - a TEK index as the tank's TEK-J02 and the cordon's TEK-Q6; R for the ram blade it leads with, 13 for the screen it holds.
- A big heavy TEK vehicle on two wheels, 169 by 128 units (see below), under the TEK shield - the double-barrel does not get through it. Drawn as a hull (Monsters.Hull: layers, wheels turned by how far the body has rolled).
- Only the turrets can be hit. They come out on top on a mast (up to three cells high, swaying - the 2026-10-08 idea). Kill one, the next comes out and shoots differently.
- Three turrets. A fourth only if the chase proves too short live (see Open).

### The machine, decided 2026-10-26 (over the text below where they disagree)
- **Size.** The hull art came out about 4 cells long by 3 tall; at 4 cells long it would be 97 units tall, and the hero's arc peaks at 100 - he would jump it. It is scaled by its height instead: 128 units tall, 169 long (5.3 cells). Its top stays at 224, so the pads of screen 13 stand as they are; it has less floor to drive on.
- **It never turns round.** Too big for a turn to read as anything but a jump cut. It faces left for good and drives back and forth, backward when it goes right. The art is mirrored once when the set is packed; the game draws it as it is (no `mirrors`). The wheels turn the other way when it backs.
- **The chase goes right, so the machine backs through it**, its ram toward the hero who runs after it. The mortar on the stern fires over the hull and the napalm falls behind the hero, as planned.
- **Contact wounds and shoves** (a new rule; no enemy did it before): any touch of the hull or a wheel takes a wound and throws the hero away from the machine's middle, up and back, with a short loss of control and a moment of mercy. It is the "crush" of 2026-10-08 made a rule, and it holds everywhere - the ram driving into him, the brake lurch, a fall onto it. The roof is out of reach (128 over the floor against a peak of 100), and a hero who drops on it from a pad is thrown off by the same rule.
- **The eye is the telegraph.** The machine has a face that always looks toward the hero in the chase, so the warnings live there: the eye blinks fast for half a second before the brake lurch (the brake lights face away from the hero in the chase - they only burn on braking, for the look), and blinks while a new head rises.
- **The mast.** Three telescopic sections out of the well on the roof; a head on the top of the third. It sways a little (the 2026-10-08 idea): life, not aim. What stands over the well's middle line is drawn over the hull; the rest is inside it.
- **Three heads, one freedom each** - the player tells them apart by what moves:
  - **Hunter** (turret 1): the mast stands, the barrel turns on its sleeve to any angle and follows the hero; the lens flares before a burst of three. No blind spot - only moving saves.
  - **Sweeper** (turret 2): four level barrels; the head rides the mast up and down to the row of the hero and sweeps it. It does not tilt: a pad over it is no refuge, because the mast at full height (three cells) lifts the barrels to a hero on the middle pad. It comes to the hero's row, not a random one, so standing still does not work. The warning: the barrels redden once the head has stopped on its row (`sweeperHeat` tinted by code), then the sweep. It cannot come down under its own roof (224): a hero on the floor is out of its reach and in the ram's - the machine drives at him. Then the sweeper **closes the ceiling** (decided 2026-10-26): it sinks to the bottom of its mast and sweeps the row just over his jump, so he may stand on the floor but not jump up to a pad while the row burns - he waits for the gap between sweeps with the ram coming.
  - **Bunker** (turret 3, the chase): stands low on the mast; two short barrels fire a volley that drops to the floor and rolls along it; the hatch on its top **breathes** - shut for the volley, open about 1.5 s after it to vent; it is hit only through the open hatch, from above. The honeycomb closes its sides and front.
- **A head turns** to face the hero by squashing to nothing across its socket and opening mirrored, in about 0.3 s - all three do it. The turn is a warning of its own: the head faced him, a volley is coming.
- **The hunter turns too** (decided 2026-10-26, over "the hunter needs no turn"): its barrel's sleeve is at the left edge of the head, so a barrel swung to the right would lie across the head and its lens, and an instant flip of the head reads cheap. So the barrel aims within its own half - from straight up to a little below level on the side the head faces - and past the vertical the head turns as the others do. The head turns only when the hero has stayed past the vertical by about 15 degrees for about 0.3 s, or a hero right over it would flap it to and fro; never in the middle of a burst. Stage 9.
- **The change of heads.** The killed head blows up, the mast sinks into the well empty and smoking, the eye blinks, the next head rises - about 1.5 s of breath that shows the progress.
- **The demo stand** of the art and these rules (a page of its own, not in the repository) runs on screen 13 with its pads, floor and backdrop; the numbers in it are first guesses.

### The entrance, decided 2026-10-26
In this order, nothing overlapping:
1. **The hero comes onto screen 13.** The tunnel music fades out (the 2.5 s fade-out of `s13-music`), and no track follows: silence. The boss track no longer starts on arrival.
2. **The Ram drives in from beyond the right edge** in the silence, ram first (it faces left, so it drives forward), to a heavy rumble - a loop of its own, "дрдрдр", the engine and the cleats on the road. It is armoured (the TEK shield: hits flare the honeycomb and do nothing) and has no head yet: the mast is down in its well. Faster than its fighting pace - about 3 s from the edge to its stop. The contact rule holds already: a hero who runs into it is wounded and thrown.
3. **It stops clear of the pads on the right.** Its stop: the ram's tip at x 260. Its rear then stands at 429, 35 units short of `s13-pad-4` (464) - not behind it; and the mast (x 330) comes up 22 units left of the long `s13-pad-3` (352-416), so the head is not hidden behind that pad either. The stop is a brake: the brake lamps burn, the rumble cuts to a hiss, a small shake.
4. **It stops and puts out its gun.** A breath (about 0.5 s), the eye lights, the mast rises out of the well with the hunter on top (about 1.5 s), to a hydraulic whine.
5. **The boss track starts** - `boss3.ogg`, no fade-in, the moment the head locks at its height. The fight begins with it: the first burst about a second later.

- **A death replays the entrance shortened** (first guess, check live): the Ram stands at its stop, the mast rises, the track plays on (a track already playing is left alone). Watching the full drive-in on every try would test patience as arena 2's cannon scene does.
- **New sounds:** the rumble (a loop), the brake hiss, the mast's whine - synthesized in code under `tools/sounds/` as the others of the machines, or made in Suno; replaceable later by file.
- **For the code (8b)** (built otherwise, see "Stage 8b as built": the Ram's own action carries the silence and the track): `s13-music` becomes a fade to silence (the vocabulary may need a `music` action with no track, or a `silence` action with its `fadeOut`); the boss track starts on a cue of the Ram when its head locks, not on an event of the screen. The entrance is the Ram's own state, before its first act, so the restart can shorten it.

### Act 1 - standing ground (turrets 1 and 2)
- The machine drives back and forth across the arena, stops, shoots, drives again; driving into the hero wounds and shoves him (decided 2026-10-08, made the contact rule 2026-10-26).
- Turret 1, the hunter: aimed bursts at the hero - teaches to move.
- Turret 2, the sweeper: rides the mast to the hero's row and sweeps it, with the red warning on the barrels first - teaches to change height.

**Decided 2026-10-27 for turret 1, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **No safe place, no quiet harbour.** The first stage is the simplest of the fight, but not a shooting range: the player must feel the finale of 3.0 from it.
- **Two modes by the hero's height.** Low - where the ram can really crush him: the road, and a jump off it up to the height of the low pads, not standing on them - the machine presses: it thinks faster and drives all the way to the wall on his side, and the gun holds its fire (one threat at a time). Higher - on the pads, out of the ram's reach but not the gun's - it roams in short legs, the barrel looks at the hero and fires.
- **The mast does not hide for a press.** It goes down only for the change of heads: a gun that hid while the machine pressed would open the way up too easily.
- **Only the eye takes a hit.** The hunter's lens is the target; bullets pass the mast, the head and the barrel.
- **About 100 lives**, more on the harder grades (as the monsters': times 1.5 and 2) - tuned live.
- **Bursts with rests between them.** A hero must be hurt by his own mistake, not because there was no way out: every burst comes after the lens has burned red, and a rest follows it.
- **Harder by the thirds of its lives** (taken over a sniper's single pattern and a hose's): three shots where he stood; three where he will be if he keeps going; a hose of six that keeps following him, and a harder press.

**Decided 2026-10-28 for turret 2, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **The target is the whole barrel pack** - the four barrels, not an eye. Bullets pass the mast and the head.
- **Shot only from over the roof.** Two small plates of the TEK honeycomb under the barrels (`Game.Shield`) take every bullet that comes from under the roof line (224): a hit flares the cells and does nothing. A hero on the road cannot hurt it - he has to climb. With the closed ceiling it is one rule: the floor is the ram's and does no damage.
- **About 140 lives**, times 1.5 and 2 on the harder grades (the target is bigger than the hunter's eye) - tuned live.
- **Fire by the thirds of its lives**, as the hunter's: first third - one sweep of the hero's row; second - the fork: his row, then at once the row next to it, the one he is most likely to jump to; last third - the ladder: the head rides the mast while it fires, so the line of bullets climbs or sinks and has to be jumped through or let pass over. The red barrels before every sweep stay.

**Decided 2026-10-28 for both heads:**
- **They short out from half their lives.** Small bolts on the head (`TLightning`, as the tank's), none above half; under half, more often the fewer lives are left. The hunter's smoke at its last third stays. Stage 10b brings it to the hunter of 3.0.76 too.
- **Medkits on the thirds.** A medkit comes as a head's row of lives passes into its next third - two a head, four over turrets 1 and 2. None at the change of heads: the breath between heads is the reward. The hunter's go onto a high pad (`s13-pad-2` or `-3`), in its sights; the sweeper's onto the road at the wall away from the Ram, on the ram's ground under the closed ceiling - a reward inside the current threat both times. A death restarts the fight, and they with it.
- **The shuttle drops them.** A small capsule drawn in code (as the life sphere): it falls from the top of the screen with a light, opens on the ground or the pad and leaves the medkit. No line of the shuttle - the capsule says it.

**Decided 2026-10-28, the blast of the sweeper** (seen on a demo stand, accepted):
1. **Overload, about 0.6 s.** On its last life the head does not burst at once: it heats to white, the bolts thicken, the eye blinks.
2. **The blast.** A white flash for a frame, a heavier jolt than the hunter's (0.5 is too little), a low blow.
3. **The wave.** A ring from the head over the whole screen in about a second - the eclipse's `wave` again, colourless, as a shock wave in film: a dark band behind a thin white front. Dust kicked from the road under the front shows where it runs along the floor.
4. **The hero is thrown.** The wave shoves him as the contact rule does - thrown, a short loss of control, a moment of mercy - but takes no life. Away from the Ram's middle toward the side with more road, hard enough that he lands on the road clear of the hull: a hero on the middle pad would otherwise drop onto the roof.
5. **The pads are knocked off** where the front crosses them, the nearest first (the middle pad over the machine). Their engines do not die: the jet keeps burning, so the thrust turns with the pad and spins it harder - each goes off in a burning spiral, a torn-off firework, with an orange glow about it, sparks behind and small bolts over its deck. They pass through the hero without a wound - decoration - and all of them leave the screen past an edge; none hits the road. In about 2 s the screen is clear for the bunker.
6. **A breath.** About half a second of quiet, the empty mast smoking; then the bunker rises and the launcher opens - the chase.
- **For the code.** A pad does not turn ("a pad neither sheds nor turns", stage 5d), and it is not taught to: on the wave the pad goes dormant and a wreck takes its place - the pad's sprite drawn at an angle through `DrawSized` (the stone turns so), flown by its own thrust and the moon's pull. The five pads stand again on a death (every death restarts the whole fight) - the aftermath of the blast's scene puts them back.
- **The sound:** a blow and a low drone that sinks - the family of `stonewake.py`, going down as the stone's went up. No crackle.

**Decided 2026-10-28 on the second stand, over point 5 and "For the code" above where they disagree:**
- **The wrecks fly slow and spin fast.** The first stand flew them by their own thrust turning with them, as written: safe ones (never on the road) came out too high and too fast, and a real spin let the moon's pull drop them on the road. So the flight and the spin come apart: the wreck flies along a heading of its own - off at about 2 a tick away from the head, at least 40 degrees up, a thrust of 0.2 easing its heading to 30 degrees over the level, outward, a drag of 0.03 keeping it under about 6 a tick - and its sprite spins on its own, 18 degrees a tick and faster, up to 45. On the stand no pad touches the road from any stand of the Ram and any height of its mast (a margin of about 60 units); one is off the screen in about 1.2 s, the slowest in about 4 s. First guesses for the live run. *Replaced 2026-10-30 (3.0.83) - see "The wrecks and the blast retuned".*
- **The chase waits for the hero** (the trap seen on the stand: a hero who kills the sweeper from the long pad on the right, with the Ram standing left of x 183, is thrown further right, the wave has blown away every pad, and a machine backing to the right would pin him at the wall). The wave's throw stays as it is - away from the Ram, toward more road. After the blast, while the bunker rises, a pair of the chase's pads comes in from beyond the right edge - a low one and a high one, a bridge over the roof - and the belt does not start until the hero is left of the hull; a hero already left of it waits for nothing. This joins the two acts: the wave clears the old pads, TEK brings in the new, and the chase begins on them. Built with stages 11 and 13. *Dropped 2026-10-30 - the Ram backs off to the wall before the blast, see below.*

**Decided 2026-10-30, the sweeper's end, over the overload and "The chase waits for the hero" above where they disagree:**
- **The Ram backs off before it bursts.** The sweeper's last life: the gun falls silent and its barrels burn red, the eye blinks, the alarm sounds - the warning hum before an arena 1 rebuild. The machine brakes to a stand, holds a moment, then backs to the right wall (it faces left for good, so the right is its back). Only at the wall does the head overload - from red to white - and burst; the wave and the pads off as they were.
- **Why.** To drive the hero out of the right corner: the chase needs him on the left. With the hull at the right wall there is no road right of it, so the wave throws him left whatever he stood on - and the chase starts with the machine where it rides the whole chase, on the right. No bridge of new pads, no waiting for the hero.
- **No mercy on the way.** The ram has rammed through the whole fight: the warning is the red, the eye and the alarm. A hero who stays in its way is touched as ever - a wound and the throw (straight up at the wall, onto the low pad there). The medkits of the thirds pay for it.
- **A way out of the hull, through the whole fight.** A hero thrown - by a touch or by the wave - who comes down inside the hull walks out of it unhurt: one wound for one mistake, not a wound, a throw and another wound. But not for ever, or the hull would be a shelter: from the moment he lands, about 1.8 s; then a touch is a touch again.

### Act 2 - the chase (turret 3)
Turret 2 dies: turret 3 comes out, a napalm launcher opens on the stern, and the machine drives off to the right. It does not leave the screen - the screen moves under it.

**The belt.** The screen becomes a treadmill the hero does not steer:
- the floor slides left; the machine stays on the right part of the screen, its wheels turning;
- the backdrop stands still (decided 2026-10-20): a seamless backdrop is not going to be made, and it does not have to be - the far wall of a giant tunnel would barely move anyway, as distant mountains from a car window. The speed is shown by the floor, the things of the world riding it and the wheels;
- everything that belongs to the world - the hero, the napalm puddles, the pads, the medkits - is carried left at the belt's speed, in the air as on the floor (otherwise a jump would be a free way to stand still);
- an idle hero drifts back to the left wall, so the hero has to run to keep up. The machine is slow, the hero is faster: running, he gains on the belt at about the belt's own speed;
- the collision grid does not move: the floor is flat and the same along its length, so only the picture slides. The floor is a row of tiles - a classic square sprite, so sliding it is a tile offset that wraps, nothing to stitch;
- the fangs ride through from right to left at random - separate sprites, nothing to loop - "posts by the road" that sell the speed (decided 2026-10-21, see "The fangs");
- the belt comes up to speed from a standstill and runs down to one at the end - no jerk either way.

**The napalm launcher - why the hero runs.** An unkillable launcher on the machine's stern (part of the armoured hull: hits on it flare the honeycomb):
- fires almost straight up; the shell climbs off the top of the screen;
- it falls straight down behind the hero: it is aimed at where the hero was about a second ago - a running hero leaves it behind, a standing one gets it on his head;
- the shells land in the fire zone only - from the left wall to the fire's edge. The edge is unstable: it breathes, and now and then lurches a couple of cells toward the middle for a few seconds, narrowing the room. More often as turret 3 loses lives;
- every shell leaves a burning puddle that lives a few seconds and rides the belt to the wall. The wall of fire at the left is not an effect of its own - it is the puddles piling up there.
- The look, in four beats: the launch (a flash at the stern, a thick orange tracer with a smoke trail climbing out of sight, a dull thump); the wait (about 0.7 s: a shimmer or a red flicker on the floor where it will land, a whistle growing from above - the warning); the drop (a blob of napalm with a ragged dripping trail); the splash (tongues of fire to the sides, then the puddle, a loop of a few frames).
- Contact with the falling drop or a puddle wounds the hero.

**Turret 3, the bunker - hit from above only.** Shielded on its sides and front by the honeycomb, open on top: a hatch. The hero aims with the mouse in any direction, so from a pad above the machine he shoots down into the hatch. From the floor every shot meets the shield. Its fire (decided 2026-10-26): it turns to the hero and fires a volley that drops to the floor and rolls along it, so the floor is no shelter and the pads are the way; the hatch is shut for the volley and open about 1.5 s after it - the window to strike.

**The drifting pads.** Pads on the upper layer, standing still in the world, so on the screen they drift left with the floor:
- a pad carries whoever stands on it toward the fire, as the floor does: a pad is not a rest but a stepping stone - cross it to the next one;
- a ladder of heights: low pads reachable from the floor with the plain jump, higher ones, and the height of the hatch;
- the pattern is random (decided), but a stretch that leads up to the hatch comes at least every few seconds, so the player is never left with nothing to climb.

**Medkits on the upper layer.** A medkit rides the upper layer with the pads and drifts past: climbed up and took it - good; did not - it is gone off the left edge.

**The brake lurch.** Now and then the machine brakes hard and rolls back about a cell - in the chase it backs, so "back" is its ram, toward the hero. The warning: its eye blinks fast for about half a second (2026-10-26; the brake lights face away from the hero in the chase). Whoever it meets is wounded and shoved by the contact rule (decided 2026-10-19 as a crush). With the fire behind, jumping clear of the bumper may land in the napalm - check live that it is not too much.

**Decided 2026-10-30, the chase, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **A quiet danger on the left.** No wall of fire: a screen that burns for the whole act is noise. The mortar is a leash - it fires only while the hero is in the left third of the screen (the zone grows by the bunker's thirds, unseen); a hero who keeps up sees a quiet road. A puddle lives about 1.5 s - the splash, a short burn - then it is soot that smokes and rides the belt away. The left edge itself carries only a heat haze and a dark red vignette that deepens as the hero comes near it: no flames.
- **The backdrop goes from the tunnels to the city - the city comes almost at once.** A fast player must not miss it: the tunnel ends within the first seconds of the chase, then the city is long. The far plane starts fast (the machine speeds up) and slows along the city; the evolution and the air phase then play on a still screen in front of it, so everyone sees the city.
- **The city.** In a vast crater beyond the end of the tunnel: abandoned, intact, not ruined - everyone died or left. Retro-futuristic, synthwave: white and chrome spires, domed halls, stepped ziggurats, ring roads, a monorail on pylons; most windows dark, a few pink and cyan neon signs still lit, one may flicker. Under a gigantic dome that is only its frame - ribs and rings, no glass. Big: the next level goes through it. A main dome (the city) and a smaller satellite dome (industry: cooling towers, tanks, cranes, masts), the monorail between them.
- **The convoys** are autonomous TEK machinery going about some business of their own; what it is, is left for later. They stop coming where the strata end.
- **The sun** stands still on the screen - it is infinitely far - so it is not on the backdrop but a layer of its own drawn in code (disc, halo, glare), behind the backdrop. The backdrop's sky is transparent: the sun shows through it only where the rock has ended, so it comes out from behind the cliff by itself. Placed low, the far ridge cuts its lower part - a sunset with no code for it. The light is white and hard while nothing stands between it and the hero; where a dome stands in front of it the code tints it pink-orange (the dome's span along the strip is in the data) - the synthwave sunset is born on the move. Stars, if any, are code too.
- **The backdrop is built from elements, not drawn on in one strip.** ChatGPT redraws the whole picture when asked to continue one, and the kept half drifts - on the ribs of a dome a seam shows. So every object is one generation on a clear ground, whole, and nothing is ever seamed through it: the cliff (the end of the tunnel, joined to the wall of screen 13), the far ridge and the crater floor (uniform textures, made seamless by code), the main dome with its city, the satellite dome, the small things (a radio dish, a hangar, a landing ring, monorail spans). One style reference for every generation. The layers may move at speeds of their own - a parallax inside the far plane. The strip, its layers and speeds, the sun, the prompts and the tools: `chase-backdrop.md`.
- **The backdrop never runs out in sight.** Its speed is tied to what is left of the strip: within a screen of its end it brakes softly to almost nothing, while the floor runs on - a far wall that barely crawls reads as a far wall. If the strip ends anyway, the chase ends with it (a guard, not a plan).
- **The pads stand in the world** and ride left with the floor. The pattern is built from hand-made chunks of three or four pads, each passable by design, and a chunk leading to the hatch's height comes every few seconds. Some pads carry a medkit from the start - only on the chunks that lead up. The carousels are dropped.
- **Three speeds:** the backdrop slowest, the fangs faster, the road fastest. The fangs are drawn behind the play layer.
- **The bunker drives the hero up and keeps him off its top.** The volley: two short barrels lob shells that land and roll left along the road, faster than the belt - the floor is no shelter. Then the hatch opens: for its first 0.5 s a column of hot exhaust goes straight up 2-3 cells; then it stands open and cooling for about 1.2 s - the window. Straight over the hatch is the column, so the hatch is struck from a pad above and to the side, as the pad drifts past - dodge, climb, catch the window.
- **The bunker by thirds:** one shell and a low column; two shells and a higher column; the shells hop to the height of a low pad, so the low pads are no shelter either.
- **The brake lurch** only in the bunker's first two thirds; the last third has the hopping shells and the leash.
- **No new art for the weapons.** The bunker, its lid and the mortar are in `tek-machine.mset`; the napalm shell, the drop, the splash and the soot, the bunker's shells, the column and the haze are drawn in code. The chase's only new art is the backdrop strip.

**Decided 2026-10-31, the belt's start and end, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **The chase starts with the bunker.** The bunker rises and its row of lives lights; 0.5-1 s later the machine drives off and the belt starts. It runs until the bunker is killed.
- **The world rides the belt through `CarryX`, not `ShoveX`.** `ShoveX` moves in whole units and drops a hero off his deck; the belt carries in fractions of a unit, the wall asked at every one, and a hero standing on a deck is carried by the deck - the drifting pads of stage 13 will carry him at the belt's speed themselves.
- **The smoke rides it too.** What the exhausts and the empty mast leave in the air drifts off with the road: a machine backing at speed trails its own smoke over its hull.
- **The motion reads with the backdrop still** (the live run of 3.0.86): the road, the wheels and the smoke sell the speed on their own. The far plane (11b) adds the city, it is not needed for the chase to play.

**Decided 2026-10-31, the drifting pads (stage 13), over the text above where they disagree:**
- **Enough of them, higher and denser than on the arena**, or the bunker cannot be hit. The exact count is the live run's; the numbers below make it passable by design.
- **The firing height.** A shot at the hatch from the middle of the screen at the height of the highest pad of screen 13 (`s13-pad-5`, its deck at 160) is right: from there the hero's middle (x about 232, y 144) sees the hatch (x about 413, y about 205) at about 19 degrees down. So the hatch takes a bullet that falls at 15 degrees or steeper (a first guess for stage 14), and the chase's high pads stand higher than 160: their decks at 128. From 128 the same angle reaches the hatch from about x 170 to the column's edge (about 395) - 225 units of firing ground, about 7 s of belt.
- **A chunk that leads up** is three pads, a ladder rising toward the machine: low (deck 280, 72 over the road, the plain jump from the floor), middle (deck 200), high (deck 128, the long pad, 64 wide); 72-80 up a step, 24-40 across between edges - the arena's own steps (the arc carries about 87 across up 80). The belt carries the hero in the air as it carries the pads, so the climb is the climb of a still screen; only the screen's left edge and the machine are not of the world.
- **The timeline of a chunk.** The climb can begin once the low pad is left of the hull (its right edge under 343); three jumps and the walks between take about 4 s (a jump of 80 up is about 41 ticks), 130 units of belt. The hero comes onto the high pad with it at about x 320 and stands in reach of the hatch for about 4.5 s more - one or two of its windows (0.5 s of the column, 1.2 s open, a volley between).
- **Density.** A chunk that leads up enters every 180 units of belt, about every 5.5 s: the next one can be climbed as the last leaves the firing ground, so a hero who keeps up always has a way to the hatch on the screen. Between them, single low and middle pads - a step off the road from the rolling shells - no higher than 200, so the way to the hatch is always a climb.
- **Passing over the machine.** A chunk enters from beyond the right edge over the hull, its high pad through the column first - while the hero is still on the road or the low pad. A pad over the hull is in front of it, as on the arena.
- **Medkits** only on the chunks that lead up, as decided, on the middle pad.

**Decided 2026-10-31, the far plane (the live runs of 3.0.88), over the text above and `chase-backdrop.md` where they disagree:**
- **Speeds by depth.** The first build ran the tunnel's layer at 30 a second and the city at 12 to 7: the far wall with its tiny convoys - far off, as the whole arena told the player - rushed past, and the city with it. The wall and the city are far and ride slowly: the wall 6, the city 3, the ridge a share of the city. The near hills were in the tunnel's layer, their start hidden under the cliff, so a slow wall made them slow too: they are a layer of their own now, fast (14), in front of the wall.
- **The near hills stand from the arena's first frame.** A start fading in from the right read as ground out of nowhere - transparency does not work in this scene. Dark in the tunnel (30% of their colour, as the wall around them), they brighten as the tunnel leaves the screen, barely to be seen, to the full once the screen is all outside.
- **What matters is the city.** It shows from about 20 s; what lies beyond the main dome - the satellite dome, the outskirts - only if the fight drags on.
- **The city is mirrored**: the sun fell on it from the wrong side. Lit from one side it still reads as cardboard, and it lacks lights and life - a step of its own later, likely a unit of its own, to be thought through well.
- **No haze on the chase's screen for now.** The pad jets bent the still picture of screen 13 over a far plane that moves - a piece of the old wall in every jet. Off on that screen; brought back in a step of its own (the far plane drawn into a texture the haze bends).

**Decided 2026-10-31, the fangs (stage 11b-2), over the text above and "The fangs" where they disagree:**
- **From the start of the chase.** "Only once the cliff is gone" was decided while the tunnel ran at 30 and the cliff left in seconds; at 6 it leaves at about 106 s. At 20 the fangs stand between the near hills (14) and the road (33) - right by depth from the first frame, and in the tunnel they are its own basalt spurs, where the slow wall needs the speed most. The first waits at the right edge and comes in the moment the road runs.
- **The city stays in sight.** The near hills already hide everything under y 276, the domes' feet with it, so a fang whose top stands about there covers nothing that shows. Most are low (tops 250-310, 40-100 over the road); one in four may be tall (tops 140-200, over the city) - never two tall ones on the screen at once, and the crown, a low broad mass, never tall.
- **Their numbers live in the layout** (`chase-backdrop.json`, a section of its own beside the speed): the kit, the scale, the road between two, the corridors of the tops, the share of tall ones, the tint in the tunnel and outside. The game only - the stand does not play them, and may never need to.
- **Dark in the tunnel, lighter out of it**, as the near hills: 35% in the tunnel to 55% outside, each a few percent off. Low, so the warm rim light of the kit, lit from below, reads as a silhouette against the contre-jour of the far plane.

**Decided 2026-11-01, the city, over the text above and `chase-backdrop.md` where they disagree:**
- **Geometry is code, paint is the generation's.** The dome frames are drawn by code - geodesic half-ellipsoids in a 3D of their own, the far half behind the city, the near half over it, dark ribs with a rim of light on the side toward the sun, their shadows on the crater floor. A generation draws only what is paint: the buildings.
- **The buildings come from sheets**, many to a generation, one light and one scale for all, and are cut by code into a kit of pieces (42: landmarks, housing, outskirts, industry), placed in depth on the stand by hand. The domes' pictures (and their mirror), the outskirts' hangar, ring and dish and the first monorail are gone; the monorail is a sheet of parts too.
- **The style: the space age as the 1970s saw it** - a space programme's colony and Japanese Metabolism. No domes, spires or finials: Art Deco with them read as a Stalinist high-rise, a church and a mosque.
- **Depth by the feet.** One camera for the city: the lower a building's feet, the nearer it stands, the bigger it is and the faster it rides - the parallax inside the city layer.
- **The front row of the city may stand in a row**: at 3 a second it reads as a street.
- **The frames come first, then the life**: the frames on the stand and the eye's verdict, then in the game (3.0.90); the neon, the lamps and the train after. Whether a frame drawn by code lives beside the painted buildings is the stand's question; if not, a frame is generated without the city and the kit stays.

**Decided 2026-11-01, the bunker in three phases, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **Why.** The volley rolled along the road and drove the hero up - where he had to go anyway to strike the hatch. The threat and the goal pointed the same way, so nothing was contested: climb, wait for the window, shoot, ride off the left edge, again. A stand of the chase (an arc against the rolling volley, a bot climbing to the hatch) showed it: with the volley the hero stood on the high pad untouched. So each third of the bunker's lives is a game of its own, and the machine itself becomes a weapon.
- **The shell flies an arc.** The bunker lobs its shells up and back over the hull along a low arc seen whole - lower than the napalm, which climbs out of sight - at the hero. A shell that comes down on a pad bursts on its deck and **knocks the pad off**: a wreck as after the sweeper's wave (`TPadWrecks`), and whoever stood on it falls. A shell that misses every pad lands on the road and rolls left, faster than the belt. One weapon, two threats: the high pad, the only place to strike the hatch from, is the hottest place on the screen. A ring on the deck marks where a shell will land. The hatch keeps its breath: the volley, the column 0.5 s, open about 1.2 s - the window.
- **Phase 1 (from full lives to 2/3) - the hero chases it.** As designed: the machine at the right wall, the arc at the hero, the napalm's leash on the left.
- **Phase 2 (2/3 to 1/3) - it falls back.** Now and then the machine falls back to the middle of the screen (its ram at about x 256) and drives back to the right wall. Never to the left edge: the hero may have nowhere to jump. The warning is the brake lurch's: the eye blinks fast for about 0.5 s. A touch is the contact rule - a wound and the throw. The road behind its stern is forbidden while it falls back: a hero who comes down onto the road right of the hull is thrown back over it to the left, fast, with a wound. A hero on a pad there is not - the pad carries him over it. While it falls back the bunker holds its fire and the napalm pauses: one danger at a time, or the room between the leash (171) and a ram at the middle is a slot under two fires. As it falls back the firing ground at the hatch slides left with it: the best moment to strike is the moment it lunges.
- **The brake lurch is dropped.** Phase 2 is the lurch, bigger and fair; two of the same trick in one fight argue with each other.
- **Phase 3 (the last third) - it chases the hero.** The change: the fire stops, the eye and the alarm; three rows of dense pads ride in; then the machine falls back to the left edge through the hero, firing nothing while it moves. A hero up on the rows lets it pass under him. One left on the road is pinned at the left edge and thrown up over the hull to the right with a wound - the contact rule's throw at a wall. Either way he ends right of it, and the jump only saves a life. Then it hunts from behind: it keeps under the hero's x; the belt carries the pads toward it, so the hero runs right along the rows against the belt; a fall to the road is a ram - thrown forward to the right, a wound. The hatch is under the rows: he strikes down as the machine passes beneath. The shells knock holes in the rows ahead of him and under him, down toward it.
- **Stern first.** The machine faces left for good, so in phase 3 it drives at the hero stern first, as a truck reversing: the alarm for its warning, the exhausts in his face, the mortar on the stern looking at him. No mirror, no new art.
- **The napalm** is phases 1 and 2 only (paused while the machine falls back). As phase 3 begins the launcher folds into the hull: the machine behind is the leash.
- **The shells by phase** (first guesses): phase 1 one arc at the hero; phase 2 two - at the hero and at the pad he will climb to next; phase 3 one or two at the rows ahead of and under him. Every phase knocks pads off.
- **The phases change on the thirds**, when the shuttle drops its medkits (stage 10d): a medkit pays for every change.
- **The bunker's death** ends phase 3; the belt runs on ("Decided 2026-11-01, the fight in motion and the air phase").

**Decided 2026-11-02, the bunker's top, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **Why.** The first build hit the bunker only through the open hatch, in the window after the column - 1.2 s of every 3.2. On the live run it read as a machine that could not be killed: the honeycomb showed over the whole dome, and nothing told where or when to shoot. A player who does not get it in a minute leaves the game here. This is a game to spend time with, not a hard one.
- **Its top is open to a bullet at any time**, the lid shut or open: a bullet that comes down onto the top of the dome from above or aslant - from a pad - takes a life. A shallow one, or one from the side or the front, only flares the honeycomb.
- **The honeycomb is on the sides and the front only**, under the top of the dome: a shield seen over the top would say "armoured" where it is not.
- **The top says it.** A pale band of light lies on it and breathes, as the hunter's lens winks: this is what has lives. It burns white while the row comes up and when it is struck.
- **The breath stays**, as life and a threat, not as a lock: the lid is thrown open by the column, which wounds whoever stands over the hatch - so the place to shoot from is a pad above and to the side.

**Decided 2026-11-03, phase 2 (stage 14b), over the text above where they disagree:**
- **Phase 1 stays as it is for now.** Its difficulty is not touched before phase 2; the three phases are tuned together once they are all in.
- **The throw over the hull is a real throw**, not a scripted flight: a jump of its own, higher and farther than a touch's, with the plain physics after it - a pad may catch him on the way down, and that is fair. Phase 3's throw at the left edge will be the same.
- **The numbers are first guesses**, for the live run: a fall back every 6-8 s, the first about 2 s into the phase; a second's stand at the middle; the way back slower than the lunge.
- **The bunker is silent from the warning till it stands at the middle and sets off back**; on its way back it aims again.
- **Until phase 3 is built, both later thirds play phase 2.**
- **The second shell lets a pad in, then strikes it** (the first live run of 3.0.96): aimed at the next deck it knocked every pad off as it came in, and there was no way up at all; aimed at the road only, the way up would always stand and the phase would be as easy as phase 1. So: a hero on a pad - at that pad (and the first at him); a hero off the pads - at the nearest pad a plain jump up from his feet that has ridden past the middle of the screen; with none, onto the road ahead of him, its roll coming back at him. The way up is caught on the right half, where the pads come in fresh - the left is no place to sit out. Two shells bursting on one pad in one tick knock it off once: one wreck, not two flying apart.
- **The shells wound** (the second live run of 3.0.96, over the 2026-11-02 "falls with the pad, no wound"): the bunker was hard to kill but never hit him - only a rolling shell did. A shell is caught in the air too: it bursts on the hero it touches, a wound. And every burst - on a deck or on him - is a blast as a tank's: it wounds whoever is near, standing on the struck pad included.

**Decided 2026-11-03, phase 3 (stage 14c), over the text above where they disagree:**
- **The rows fly in.** Ridden in at the road's pace they would take 15 s to cover the screen, and the pass must wait for them. So the first stretch of the three rows flies in from beyond the right edge at once, its jets hard, about 3.5 s, and comes to the road's pace; from then on the rows are sent at the edge as the road runs. It cannot be explained, but it is a sight. A pad already riding keeps its slot in them.
- **The hunt keeps its hatch a step behind him**, a little faster than he runs along a row against the road: a runner stays ahead of it, a hero who stops has it under him. Worked through for the strike: from the top row (deck 120) the hatch takes a bullet from over 300 units across, from the middle one (192) from about 100, so with the hatch two dozen units behind him every shot down goes in - the attack is open, the danger is the rows knocked from under him and the ram. The column stays: a step behind, it misses a hero who runs or stands, and gets one who walks back over the hatch.
- **The pass shoves him.** As the plan says: a hero on the road is shoved before the bow to the left edge, and thrown over the hull to the right only when pinned there - while he is shoved, a jump to the low row saves the life.

**Decided 2026-11-04, phase 3 reworked (stage 14c, 3.0.98), over the text above where they disagree:** 3.0.97 was built on a misread of the plan; on the live run it was the wrong game.
- **The wave is only for the pass.** The crowd of pads is there while the machine changes from the right wall to the left, so the hero can jump it; then the wave rides off as any pad does, and the road goes back to the groups - a little denser than before (the guard 4 s in place of 6, climbs 70% of the groups in place of 60%). No rows sent after the wave.
- **Through and off the screen.** The pass goes on past the left edge; out there the machine turns round and comes back bow first - the hull mirrored - chasing the hero, not stern first. The turn costs about a second and a half; for its last half second the eye's red is thrown on the left edge, so the player knows where it comes from. Silent from the change till it is in again.
- **On the road in the pass: the throw**, not a shove and not the plain touch. Thrown over the hull to the right with a wound, he comes down behind it as it drives off. The left edge pins no one now.
- **The right is the hero's, the left is the machine's.** Its bow keeps a step behind him, but within the left half: past the middle of the screen it does not follow. Now and then it surges toward nearly the right edge and presses him there - very briefly, a couple of seconds, then back as fast: a long press at the right wall is a trap (the first run of 3.0.98: it pushed him to the right edge and kept him there). It backs only into a hero who has come down behind its stern - on the road or in the air - and throws him to the right over the hull; a hero up on a pad there it lets the road carry back over its hatch, the place to strike it from. Any touch on the road is a ram to the right.
- **The wave at the heights of the usual pads, from three sides** (the first run of 3.0.98: the rows stood at other heights than the pads that ride in). Its rows stand at the decks of a climb - the foot, a step up, the deck where a shot down reaches the hatch - and each flies in from its own side: the foot from beyond the right edge, the middle from beyond the left one, the top from above.
- **No throw back to the left at the right wall**: its bow stays a step behind him, so it never reaches the wall, and there is always room to the right.
- **No new art**: the hull, its well, mast and chassis are drawn mirrored; the exhausts get a pair of smokes thrown the other way.

**Decided 2026-11-04, after the run of 3.0.98, over the text above where they disagree:**
- **A shell's blast is half as wide.** A shell bursting on a pad wounded from too far: the game was too hard. `ShellBlast` 40 in place of the tank's 80. The rest of the difficulty stays.
- **The machine outlives the bunker.** Its third head blown up, it does not go back to the wall and stand: it runs on - the hunt with no head, bow first, invulnerable, nothing left on it to hit, ramming the hero on the road - until his henshin (stage 16), which ends it.
- **The chase is eased** (the next run: too hard while it hunts from behind). In the hunt the bunker lobs one shell, not a pair, a little more often (the aim 35 ticks in place of 50). Pads are still knocked off - but it aims at a pad less often: at the pad he stands on, always; in the air or on the road, at the road near him where no pad is in the shell's way. (A build in between left every pad standing: wrong, the pads are to be knocked off.)
- **A row of honeycomb off the top of the bunker's dome.** The open top reaches a row of cells (5.1) lower: a bullet from a pad a little lower or farther off, at a shallower angle, still takes a life. The steepness (15 degrees) and the width stay.
- **A shell rolls the way it was lobbed**: lobbed right by the hull turned round, it rolls right along the road; lobbed left, left as before.
- **The bunker's blast at 97-98% of the charge.** Its lives bring the gauge to about 97; its death pours up to 97 as any head's does, and the last three percent come after it, a percent at a time - full 10 to 15 s after the blast. The full gauge is the henshin, the next stage, designed in a chat of its own.

### End of the chase
Turret 3 dies: the launcher folds into the hull, the belt runs down to a standstill, the machine stops. The energy reaches 100% - the evolution. The rest of the fight is on a still screen. *Replaced 2026-11-01: the belt does not run down, the fight goes on in motion - see "Decided 2026-11-01, the fight in motion and the air phase".*

## Energy (the form charge)
How it looks and where it stands - "Stage 6 as built".

**Decided 2026-10-28, over the text below where they disagree:** the charge goes by heads - the hunter brings a quarter, the sweeper a quarter, the bunker the half that is left. From the stone's 5%: a ceiling of 30% while the hunter lives, 55% while the sweeper lives, 95% while the bunker lives, 100% on its death. Not tied to the lives exactly and late - the tank keeps pouring a couple of seconds after the hit, film magic. The trickle is cut down, or it would jump the quarters by itself.

```pascal
// a head dies -> the ceiling moves; the tank keeps pouring with a delay
case FHead of
  rhHunter: Cap := 30;
  rhSweeper: Cap := 55;
  rhBunker: Cap := 95; // 100 only on its death
end;
```

The charge is tied to the lives of the three turrets, but with a delay, so the dependence is not obvious:
- **Hidden tank.** Damage to a turret goes into a tank, not into the gauge. The gauge drinks from the tank at its own pace - it keeps growing a couple of seconds after the hit.
- **Trickle.** The stone charges by itself, about 0.5% a second - the gauge lives even with no hits.
- **Ceiling.** While the third turret lives, the gauge stops at 95%. 100% comes only with the death of the last turret; the evolution follows at once. *Replaced 2026-11-04: 97 while it lives, the last 3% in 10-15 s after its death - see "Decided 2026-11-04, after the run of 3.0.98".*

```pascal
// turret damage - into the tank, not the gauge
Pool := Pool + Damage * K;

// every tick: the gauge drinks from the tank
Take := Min(Pool, DrainPerSec * Dt);
Pool := Pool - Take;
Energy := Min(Energy + Take + TricklePerSec * Dt, CapBeforeLast);

// third turret dies
CapBeforeLast := 100;  // the gauge runs to 100 -> Evolution
```

K is tuned so the turrets bring about 90-95% and the trickle the rest - a slow player never sticks at 94%.

## Evolution and the moon form
- Evolution: black orbs fly to the hero - the henshin rite (docs/mechanics/henshin.md) with black orbs and a third pattern, "eclipse": a black ring closing in (snowflake for arena 1, vortex for the gravels, eclipse here). Healing as the rite heals.
- The moon form: an orb gun - black orbs that pierce the TEK armour; jump +25%, as the ice form; the fire rain and the protective aura, both of black orbs.
- The look of the moon form comes in the next version; in 3.0 it looks like the ice form. It must be felt through the weapon: the orb gun fires slower but heavier, with visible recoil and sound - a sledgehammer after a pistol.
- Death after the evolution: the fight restarts from its start (as every death here).

## Air phase
- No turrets left: the machine takes off, nozzles in place of wheels (the 2026-10-08 idea).
- Drops grenades on the hero and shoots its guns.
- The hero shoots the machine itself - the orbs pierce the armour.

**Decided 2026-11-01, the fight in motion and the air phase, over the text above and "End of the chase" where they disagree:**
- **The fight does not stop.** The chase is long enough to carry the rest of the fight (a live run rode it for minutes with the city still going by): after the bunker the belt runs on, and the evolution and the air phase play in motion. Each act a game of its own, not the same game with bigger numbers: standing ground, the belt, the air.
