- **The road ends.** As the machine takes off - the wheels into the hull, the nozzles out - the road runs out under the hero: its edge rides in from the right, and past it nothing, no floor below. The machine needs the road no more, and the road ends: one reason for both.
- **Long pads fly on with the hero.** Over the edge the hero jumps onto long pads that fly right with him and the machine, their nozzles turned to the left. On the screen they keep up rather than drift: the belt carries them and the hero no more, and the speed is the far plane's and the fangs'. The pads move among themselves - not a still floor in the sky; how (falling behind, overtaking, coming in) is the stage's design.
- **A fall is the pit.** Under the pads is the abyss; a fall is a pit, as everywhere in the game, and brings the hero back on a pad - the checkpoint rides with the pads. A death still restarts the fight.
- **The moon form is made for it**: the jump of +25% is the hop between pads over the abyss.

## The henshin, the throw and the takeoff (designed 2026-11-04)
Over "Evolution and the moon form", "Air phase" and "Decided 2026-11-01, the fight in motion" where they disagree: the world stops for the henshin and the throw, and the fight goes on in motion again from the takeoff. Three parts: the last of the charge, the rite, the scene with the Ram.

### The heartbeat
- One beat runs from 97% to the throw. The gauge pulses with it, the heart monitor on the HUD beats in step, the orbs of the rite come in on it, the ring of the rite beats on it, the burst is its strongest beat, the pads spin up as it quickens. Silence after the throw.
- A double "tuk-tuk", low - synthesized in code under `tools/sounds/` as the stone's drone (low sounds work in code, clicks and crackles do not); replaceable by a file.
- First guesses: 60 beats a minute at 97%, 90 at 100%, 140 at the burst; 70 to 150 through the spin of the pads.

### The last 3%
- The bunker dead, the Ram hunts on as built (3.0.98), and the last percents come as built - quick: nothing happens and it is easy to dodge, a breather.
- The gauge leaves its slot on the heart monitor's panel and rides to the top centre, where the level's title appears, growing on the way (first guess: three times its size in the slot). It beats with the heartbeat; the number in it keeps counting. The slot stays empty: the gauge is gone for good after the eclipse.

### 100%: the brake and the eclipse
- **The world stops before the rite.** The Ram drives the screen's motion, so at 100 it brakes - the hiss and the jolt - and the belt runs down with it (the run-down of 11a): the far plane, the fangs and the pads come to rest. Nothing moves to pull the eye off the scene. A hero inside the hull's zone at that moment is set out of it with no wound. From the brake to the wheel nothing wounds him.
- **The order, decided 2026-11-05** (over the eclipse of the disc, its corona, the diamond ring and the beam out of the diamond, built in 3.0.101 and taken out in 3.0.102: a beam out of a point in the HUD reads as a laser, not as light from above). The world at rest, the orbs come and lift him and circle him on the heartbeat; then the light goes; then, in the dark, the beam falls from above - the column of light of the Tron Legacy poster.
- **The control is taken** as the world comes to rest, and given back at the wheel. The orbs catch the hero where he is - on a pad, on the road, in the air - and lift him a cell; at an edge of the screen or by the hull he is drawn clear first. He does not touch the ground till the wheel: the rite, the beam, the burst and the throw are one levitation. *Replaced 2026-11-06: the hero runs, jumps and shoots through the rite, the beam and the burst - a paralysis from the brake to the wheel is too long; the catch and the lift move to the throw (18a), and the control comes back at the wheel as before. See "Stage 16d reworked".*
- **The light goes** once he is up: one overlay darkens the far plane and the play layer, the backdrop's sun with the rest; over it stay the beam, the hero, the orbs and the Ram's red eye. The gauge at the top centre goes out with the light, for good. The darkness is the rite's: in 16d it deepens as the orbs gather.
- **The beam falls from above**: a vertical column from the top edge of the screen straight down onto him, wherever he hangs - a white core about his width in a wide moonlit glow, soft edges, the light running out below his feet; a flash as it lands, a halo round him, and it swells and brightens on each heartbeat.
- **The boss track** fades out to silence as the light goes - paused, not stopped; the heartbeat alone.

### The rite
- Black orbs, one at a time, fast, from all sides: from the road, the pads, the fangs, the hull, and part of them from the far city - a point deep in the far plane that grows as it comes, out of the depth into the pattern. The gaps shorten with the beat (first guess: 30 orbs, 0.35 s down to 0.06 s, about 4 s).
- **The pattern "eclipse"**: a ring round the hero that beats. On each double beat it draws in and breathes out, and a faint ripple of light runs from it over the dark world. Not a heart shape - the heart is the beat, not the figure. A third pattern beside the snowflake and the vortex (`Orbs.Patterns`).
- The orbs wound nothing: there is no enemy but the Ram, and it takes no damage yet. The rite's healing: +3 at its end.
- The collapse over the whole body, as the rite does it; the body fills with light. The form becomes the moon form (`hfMoon`): in 3.0 it looks like the ice form and jumps +25% as it does - the wheel needs that jump.

### The burst
- Stronger than in arenas 1 and 2. The last, strongest beat, the inhale (half a second of silence), the strike: more rays, longer, across the screen and off it, through the far plane; a white core with the black tail and light halo of the moon look. The screen flashes white for two frames; the darkness is torn off in one frame - the light is back; the shake.
- The Ram is thrown back half a cell and stunned: its eye flickers out of step, its lamps go out, smoke from the wells. The rays wound nothing.

### The throw
- The hero hangs on his orbs. From the points of his body threads of orbs go out to the pads on the screen - whatever pads stand there, his own included; too few or too many is tuned after the live run.
- The pads' jets die - the orbs hold them now. They rise a little and spin about their own axis, slowly at first, faster with the beat, under a rising hum. The Ram's honeycomb lights up in alarm.
- Spun up, the hint appears over the hero: the mouse-button glyph of the bonus hint (3.0.5), pulsing with the beat. A press throws them; with no press they go by themselves in 3 s.
- Almost at once: each pad lets go at its own small delay (first guess 0 to 0.2 s) and flies, spinning, engines burning (the wrecks' flight of 10c-3), at the honeycomb. Each hit a blast and a piece of the honeycomb shattered, the last a big blast; then the rest of the honeycomb blinks and goes out cell by cell from the hits outward.
- **Decided 2026-11-06, after the live run of the first build of 18a-1** (over the points above where they disagree): threads of orbs from the hero to the pads read as a spider's web. The orbs come out of his body and fly off him to the pads and cling to their beams, and so they spin them. The pad spins about the middle of its beam, not of its picture (the engines hang under the beam). The Ram drives off at once after the burst, to the left third of the screen. The hero is carried out of the picture - a little over the road by the right edge. The gripped pads glide slowly to places on an arc round the Ram's right side, all as far from it, evenly apart - from the arc the volley reaches it all at once. The orbs are too big against the ice form's - left so for now, a step of its own later. *After the second run, the same day*: the Ram drives off farther, leaving a cell of road behind it; the arc wider, its top just under the top of the screen - room for the pads to gather speed at the volley. *After the third run*: the honeycomb does not flash in alarm - the Ram is not afraid; its lamps and its eye blink.
- **The music after the honeycomb (decided 2026-11-06).** The silence lasts from the volley to the last hit; with the big blast the music starts - from its beginning, fading in, not where it paused. For now the boss track; a new track of its own is planned for this moment - the fight after the armour, from the wheel on - and replaces it once it is made. *Made (2026-11-07), `flight.ogg`, in since 3.0.115 - see "The flight's track as built".*

### The wheel
- The armour gone, the front wheel - the one toward the hero, the hull facing right since the hunt - breaks off. The hull drops on that side and scrapes the road: sparks, a scrape. The wheel bounces once and rolls right, at the hero.
- The hero comes down to the road as the wheel is let go, and the control is his again. The wheel wounds as the hull's touch does and is to be jumped - sized for the moon form's jump. It rolls off the right edge.
- **Decided 2026-11-06, before the build of 18b** (over the points above where they disagree): the wheel keeps its own size, 41 - the very wheel off the axle; any jump clears it (the plain jump climbs 100, the moon form's 155), and the moon form's jump waits for the pads over the abyss of 18d. From the wheel on, the hull's touch wounds and throws again, as before the henshin: the control back, the fight back. The bow's plough stands on the road already, so the hull has nowhere to drop: it leans a few degrees about its rear wheel's touch and the point of the plough digs under the road line - the nose dug in.

### The takeoff
- The hull rocks; the second wheel is thrown to the left, off the screen, a clank out there. Nozzles in the wheel wells, no new art for now: jets drawn in code as the pads' (the blue of a jet), dust blown off the road. If they read poorly, art later - the fight's mechanics are to change anyway.
- The hull rises level, the lean gone, and hangs.
- A long health bar over it from the moment its armour is off - its length and its lives are the air phase's.
- The boss track comes back where it paused, fading in. *Replaced 2026-11-06: the music starts again at the fall of the honeycomb (18a-2), so the takeoff plays on under it - see "The throw".*
- What follows is the air phase (stage 18d), not designed yet: the road's edge, the long pads, how the machine fights. Till then it hangs.

**Decided 2026-11-07, the takeoff rewritten** (over the points above where they disagree):
- **The fire out of the front well.** Where the first wheel was, a jet of fire bursts down out of the empty well: the bow lifts off the road, the plough out of it, the hull rocking up about its rear wheel - the lean of 18b turned the other way.
- **The second wheel spat out.** A jet out of the rear well too, and the second wheel is spat out of it and rolls at the hero as the first did (`TLooseWheel`, from the other axle): a second jump, a second dodge. Not thrown off the screen to the left with a clank out there - a wheel the player never sees is a shot fired at nothing.
- **Level in the air.** On its two jets the hull rights itself as it rises: it swings past level, back, and settles, a spring dying out - then it is steady.
- **Over the hero, and it hangs.** It rises to stand over him and hangs there; how it keeps to him (over him or over a fixed point), and whether its hull wounds by touch in the air, are the stage's design.
- **The jets** are drawn in code as the pads' are, the blue of a jet or the fire of a burst well - no new art; if they read poorly, art later.
- **The hero watches.** Through the takeoff he has the controls and his old gun; the gun does nothing to the hull (it ricochets, 3.0.110). He has only the second wheel to mind.
- **The orb gun and the health bar come together, after the takeoff**, once the hull hangs: a bar without a weapon that strikes it would be a promise with no button.

**Decided 2026-11-07, before the build of 18c** (over the points above where they disagree):
- **The order.** The first wheel rolls off the right edge, a breath, then the fire out of the front well: the two wheels never roll at him at once.
- **Over the hero, with a lag.** Hanging, it follows him across, slower than he runs - its shadow keeps to his heels, and he shoots nearly straight up (18c-2). Not over a fixed point: a target in a shooting gallery.
- **Its height.** The bottom of the hull hangs just over the top of the moon form's jump (the jump climbs 155: his head at the top is about 165 from the top of the screen, the hull's bottom about 150); its top comes about 40 under the top of the screen. Its touch wounds as since the wheel, and hanging it is out of his reach - the question of a touch in the air answers itself.
- **The bar along the top of the screen**, between the HUD's panels, not over the hull: hanging, the hull leaves too little room over it.
- **Point B**: the hull hanging, the orb gun in his hands, the bar full, 5 health; the takeoff and the second wheel are not played again.

**The orb gun (decided 2026-11-07)**: the moon form's weapon, the one thing that wounds the Ram's body.
- **Like any weapon it replaces the one he holds** (the Duplet, the chain, whatever it is); its ammunition is endless.
- **Its fire is the chain's of level 2** (`weapon3`: a cooldown of 5, bullets at 10, three bullets a shot a unit apart) with one orb a shot instead of three, a little faster - a cooldown of 4 as a first guess. A sound of its own. One orb, one life.
- **The look of the shot**: a black orb with the moon look (the white heart, the light halo of `MoonBurstLook`), not a bullet; "a sledgehammer after a pistol" from "Evolution and the moon form" gives way to the fire just decided - heavier in look and sound, not slower.
- **What it strikes**: the Ram's bare hull, which loses a life to each orb; the loose wheels and anything else of it throw an orb off as they throw a bullet off. Its old bullets ricochet on, as since 3.0.110.
- **The Ram's lives**: 150 on the first grade, scaled by the grade as the heads' are (`HunterLives` and the rest); its long bar over it shows them.
- *How the gun comes to him was open; decided the same day, below.*
- **The art** (decided 2026-11-07): a white blaster in the spirit of tokusatsu hero gear, generated - pearl-white plates with panel lines, a black grip and a black ring at the muzzle, a crescent-moon fin along its top from the stern past the muzzle, a crystal window over the grip with a black orb in it with a white heart and a pale halo (the moon look of the shot, held in the gun). A horn was tried first and read as a horn, not a weapon. Built as the Duplet is (`tools/duplet/build_duplet.py`), `held` only - it lies on no pedestal: `orbgun.mset`, the grip in the fist of the 2008 arm frame, 32 units long in a square of 48. Its vents and trim lines vanish at 6 px a unit; they are for 4K.
- **How it comes** (decided 2026-11-07): the column of moonlight of 16c falls on him once more; black orbs pour down it, spiral round his hand and collapse into it; a white flash, and the gun is in his fist - white-hot first, cooling to its own look over half a second, drawn in code over the art. The long bar comes up with it.

**Decided 2026-11-08, before the build of 17** (over the points above where they disagree):
- **The world waits for the gun.** Till the gun is in his hands the fight does not go on: the hull hangs over him with its lag, the world stands as the eclipse left it (`BeginEclipse`: the Ram braked, the belt stopped), the hull takes nothing - bullets ricochet off it (3.0.110).
- **The gift comes by itself**: the hull settled (`tkHanging`), a breath, and the column falls. Not on a press of his: the press would decide nothing - the throw's press was its climax, this is a gift.
- **With the gun**: the hull's lives (150 on the first grade, by the grade as the heads'; a first guess, a constant, tuned live); the long bar comes up along the top of the screen between the HUD's panels; the orb gun fires and wounds the bare hull; the world moves off again - the road, the far plane, the fangs, the pads - smoothly, not with a jolt: the chase goes on.
- **The hanging hull, till 18d is designed**: it keeps over him with its lag, as since 18c-2, and does not strike. Till then the fight after the gun is a shooting gallery with nothing against him - about 18 s of a held button at the first grade's 150 lives - a stopgap, not a fight.
- **His old gun** goes as the spec says: the orb gun replaces whatever he holds. It stays his till the end of the fight; at point B it is in his hands again; no pickup takes its place.
- **Two steps, cut along another line than "the gun comes, then it fires"**: a bar with no gun that strikes it would be a promise with no button. 17a - the gun whole on a standing world: the scene, the art, the gun in his hands, its fire, its wound to the hull, the hull's lives and their bar; live, a shooting gallery that tries the weapon alone. 17b - the chase goes on: the world's smooth start, point B, the End key's 4th press.
- **Noticed live, for later**: from the road the hero can jump high enough to touch the hanging hull. To think about, not now.

**Decided 2026-11-08, after the first build of 17a was seen** (over "How it comes" and the points above where they disagree):
- **The gun in the hands is half the size**: 16 units long, not 32 - the 32 was out of all measure with the hero.
- **No column, no orbs down it**: simpler. The gun appears as the respawn does - the shroud of `ReviveLook` - in the middle of the screen, on the road; when the hero stands there, on the next cell, not on his. No flash at the hand, no cooling from white.
- **He takes it, and then the fight begins**: the gun is a pickup like any (the Duplet's take), and the hull's lives and the bar's cells come with the take.
- **The shroud is no more the hero's alone**: its painter is handed a figure - the frames and where it stands now - so the gun wears one too. A clean cut in architecture, as befits a showcase project.

### Restart points
Over "a death restarts the fight from its start" for everything after the bunker:
- **Point A, the bunker's death.** A death from there till the takeoff brings the hero back right before the henshin with 5 health: the bunker dead, the Ram hunting empty-masted from the left, the charge at 97 and the last 3% played again. The far plane goes on from where it stands.
- **Point B, the takeoff.** A death in the air phase brings him back there with 5 health - the rite and the throw are not replayed. Comes with 17b; till then a death after the takeoff goes to point A, and the restart gives his old gun back.

### The End debug key
The 1st press: the end of arena 2, as now. The 2nd: the start of the arena 3 fight (screen 13, the entrance). The 3rd: point A. A 4th does nothing. Point A from the key and from a death is one setup with two callers.

## The end
- The blast. The form comes off (black orbs into the aura ring, as the ice form). No victory track (decided 2026-10-22: win.ogg already lied once, at the end of arena 2); the flight's track (`flight.ogg`) falls silent, and the titles close the level - and version 3.0 with it. See docs/mechanics/finale.md.

## Stage 1 as built (3.0.63)
All of it is data on screen 9 plus a few words of the event vocabulary; no code knows the false ending.

- **The victory track of the trial is a sound, not music** (TSoundBank.PlayTrackHeld: win.ogg decoded whole at startup, played on a sound channel and held). The music channel is free, so the next track fades in over the end of the victory with no conflict. The trial's own track stops at the victory. The game counts down what is left of it (from the decoded length, TSoundBank.SecondsOf); the menu pauses the track and the count with it, a restart and a new level stop it. The victory at the boss of level 1 is still music - it ends the level.
- **Condition `victoryEnds`** (ecVictoryEnds), JSON "lead" in seconds (0 by default): holds once the victory track has "lead" seconds left or fewer, and after its end. A missing win.ogg is a track of 0 seconds - the events fire at once.
- **`music` takes "fade"** (seconds, 0 by default): the track comes in from silence (Mix_FadeInMusic).
- **Action `goRight`** (eaGoRight): the go-right arrow (Hud.GoRight) in the middle of the screen - a hollow arrow of neon tube, three strips of the band glow along its outline (halo, tube, white core), mitered at the corners, so it is sharp at any window size. Four slow blinks, 60 ticks each, every one a raised cosine from dark to full and back, then it is gone - about seven seconds. No sound: the arrow is big enough to be seen (a beep and a slide on each flash were tried and dropped). It also goes out when the hero leaves the screen, on a restart and with the level. The beat (TGoRightBeat) is apart from the picture. Reusable: any event can raise it.
- **The events**: s09-tunnel-music (`victoryEnds`, lead 5: lunar.ogg with fade 5 - remembered as the track a restart plays) and s09-go-on (`victoryEnds` at the very end: `openDoor`). Screen 9 is a shut door too: the trial over, the hero still waits at the threshold until the victory track ends and the arrow lights - the way on opens with the arrow, never before it.
- **The door is still a wall**: screen 9 is the last screen of level 2 until stage 2 adds screen 10. The arrow points at a closed door until then.
- lunar.ogg is put in `bin/music/` by hand; without it the screen stays silent and the arrow shows anyway.

## Stage 2 as built (data, no version)
Screen 10 of level 2 is all data: `bin/level2.json` and the two level sets. The art is five ChatGPT generations; what was done to each is in its description in the set.

- **The grid** (16x12, rows from the top): col 0 rows 0-9 the TEK casing; row 10 the slot out of screen 9 (open at col 0), under the casing column of screen 9's right edge; row 11 the ledge, floor line 352 as the shelves of screen 9, broken at cols 4-5 (two cells, jumped at one level), its left lip drawn 8 units into col 4 and held by a pad with no picture (`s10-pit-lip`, 128, deck on 352, 8 wide, lets bullets by), so the hero walks to the drawn edge before he falls; the pedestal - a step at (8, 10), the top at row 9 cols 9-11, a step at (12, 10); the right edge open from row 0 to 10.
- **The layers, back to front:** `backgrounds` from screen 10, `screen10` (the far wall, 1440x1080, tint 75/75/80); `s10-depth` (two basalt spurs in the middle distance, tint 60/60/64, the space over the pedestal kept clear); `s10-pedestal` (columnar basalt, re-cut onto its matter: 160 units wide at 256, 287 - the steps 32 units each, the middle 96, its top on 288 and the steps on 320; set into the ledge so the ledge drawn over it hides the joint); `s10-room` (the ledge of solidified lava crust, the slot with a steel sill flush with the floor, the violet bar); `s10-casing` (the concrete casing cut out of the room's own pixels, tint 45/38/33, so it sits with the rock).
- No 4K yet: the room is at backdrop density; a 4K room comes as four objects of 256 units once it is upscaled outside the session.
- **The gun:** a placeholder until stage 3 - the 2008 `weaponShotgun` at cell 11, 9 (feet on 288, the top of the pedestal). Its picture waits for stage 3 at 42 units, 6 px a unit.
- **The respawn point:** cell 2, 11 (as every placement here, counted from 1).
- **The door from screen 9** needed no code: with a tenth screen the right door of screen 9 leads on, closed while the trial runs. The ending titles moved with it: the right edge of screen 10 is now the last door of the campaign until screen 11 comes.
- `lunar.ogg` plays on from screen 9; a restart on screen 10 plays it too.
- **The End debug key** goes straight to this screen from anywhere, with the music a walk there would have brought (`DebugJumpToWork`, `TrackBefore`). Move its screen number on with the work.
- Nothing moved on the screen at this stage; the haze came with stage 4. No lamp-and-beacon of REPAINT here - on screen 10 only the dust moves.

## Stage 3 as built (3.0.64)
The look and the shot went in together, as one commit: 3a and 3b were one reason - the new gun.

- **The name: the Duplet** ("Дуплет", en "Double Tap") - a hunter's word for a shot from both barrels at once: the name says what the gun does. Pickup `weaponDuplet` in `bin/monsters.json`, weapon type 5 (`DupletWeapon` in Hero.pas; 0..4 are the 2008 guns).
- **The art:** `bin/sprites/duplet.mset`, made by `tools/duplet/build_duplet.py` from one generation (the source is not kept): `lying` - the pickup, 42 x 12 units at 6 px a unit, the gun 11.2 units tall on the bottom edge (so it sits on the stone, drawn a unit under the matter); `held` - the gun in the hands, 32 units long (twice a 2008 gun) in a square of 48, the grip in the fist of the 2008 arm frame, so the gun sticks out ahead of the hero (decided: the shot leaves far from him - his target is a big TEK machine); `gone` - a clear frame.
- **A picture wider than its cell:** `"picture": {"width", "height"}` in a monster's def (`TPictureDef`). `TMonster.PictureBox` - the frame's box: the cell, or the picture standing on the feet line, centered on the cell. The monster draws its frame there, and the uptake takes its corner, its size, its silhouette and its halo from it (`TUptakePainter.PictureOf` takes the monster now). The pickup's hit box and its contact stay the cell.
- **In the hands:** `TSpriteRenderer.DrawRotated` takes the square's side (the 2008 32 by default); the held frame is dense art in a cache of its own (no color key, linear filter).
- **The shot:** 12 pellets, six out of each barrel (bores 2 units apart across the shot), born at the muzzle, 32 units ahead of the hand and 2 over its line, each barrel's shot from a square 6 units across, all at the aim angle - the shotgun's pattern, doubled. Cooldown 34 ticks against the shotgun's 20, speed 8, gravity 1: 2.4 times the shotgun a shot, 1.7 times slower; 1.4 times the shotgun a second.
- **Red shot:** `TBulletLook` (blPlain, blRed) on a bullet; a red one tints the shared texture for its own draw (100/28/22 percent) and puts it back - the flight and the burst frames alike.
- **The recoil** (removed 2026-10-28, 3.0.77: it threw the hero off his pads and was awkward in the fight; the shake stays): 6 units along X against the shot (the cosine of the aim: a shot straight down barely moves him), paid in halves a tick at a time through `ShoveX` - a wall stops it, a deck's edge drops him. Kill and Revive drop what is left. Plus a light shake, trauma 0.2 a shot.
- **The sound:** `bin/sounds/duplet.wav` by `tools/sounds/duplet.py` - the 2008 shotgun slowed to 0.8, fired twice 14 ms apart, over a thump sliding from 110 to 42 Hz, soft-clipped; 0.75 s.
- First guesses for the live run: the muzzle reach, the recoil, the trauma, how red the shot reads.

## Stage 4 as built (3.0.65)
A new kind of the dynamics, `dust` (`TDust` in Levels.Dynamics), and one field of it on screen 10. No render code changed: the kind lives on the `sky` layer by default, drawn after the backdrop and before the static objects, so the spurs of `s10-depth`, the pedestal and the room stand in front of the dust by the order the game already draws in. The layer stands still while the world shakes, as the far wall does.

- **Seen only where it is lit.** A field has one light (`TDustLight`): a shaft out of a hole - brightest on its axis, wider the farther it falls, dark above its source -, a glow off the lit floor (an oval fading from its middle) and an ambient. The light is reckoned at the corners of a mesh laid over each band (a corner every 4 units across at most, 8 rows down; `SDL_RenderGeometry`), its tint is the color of the light, and the band is added over what is behind it (`SdlBlendModeAdd`). There is no texture of a beam: in a vacuum the shaft is seen only where dust crosses it, so the dust draws it. The light stands still while the dust moves through it.
- **The bands** (`TDustBand`) roll across at a steady speed and sway up and down, slow; each is stretched to its length (one turn of the art) and height. A band is drawn in runs, one for each turn of the art the field's width crosses, so no texture wrap is asked of the renderer. The sway of every band starts at a point of its own (`SlotRoll` of its index). Time counts from the level's load and a restart does not rewind it: the dust does not jump on a death.
- **The art:** `bin/sprites/dust.mset`, three strips drawn by `tools/dust/build_dust.py` from wrapping value noise (fixed seeds, the same set every run): white, the dust in the alpha, seamless across, 1536 px wide - `dust-band-1` a thin torn thread, `dust-band-2` the wide even body, `dust-band-3` dense with gaps. `dust` joined the `objectSets` of level 2.
- **Screen 10:** tag `s10-dust`, the light warm (tint 100/80/60); the shaft from the hole of the vault (341, 16 - measured on `screen10`) to (322, 470) under the bottom edge, 3 units wide at the hole and 48 below; the glow at (318, 430), reach 205 by 240, 75%; ambient 5%. Bands, top to bottom: `dust-band-1` at y 128, 40 high, 720 long, 12 units a second, 60%; `dust-band-2` at 205, 90 high, 900 long, 8 a second, 100%; `dust-band-3` at 282, 70 high, 800 long, 5 a second, 90%. All drift right, toward the city and the boss - the arrow has gone out, the dust keeps pointing. Sways 3, 4 and 2 units over 25, 35 and 45 seconds. Doubled after the first live run (2026-10-21): at 6, 4 and 2.5 units a second over 50-90 seconds the dust was all but invisible.
- **Old cards:** three textures of 1536 px at most, six draw calls a frame at most, a few thousand corners.
- First guesses for the live run: the densities, the width of the shaft, how bright the glow lifts the low band.

## Stage 5a as built (3.0.66)
Closed doors, a mechanic of any level; on level 2 it shuts screen 10 until the Duplet is taken.

- **The level** (Levels.Defs): `"shutDoors": [10]` in the root, a list of screens (`TLevel.DoorShutOn`). The load turns down a door that no event of its screen opens (`SLevelDoorNeverOpens` - a softlock caught at load), an opening on a screen whose door is not shut (`SLevelDoorNotShut` - a screen number gone astray), a screen off the level (`SLevelDoorBadScreen`) and one named twice (`SLevelDoorTwice`).
- **The action** `openDoor` (`eaOpenDoor`, Levels.Events), JSON nothing more; the director calls the cue `OpenDoor` (`TOpenDoorCue`, Events.Director).
- **The game** (Moon2D.dpr): `FDoorOpened`, raised by the cue, which shows the go-right arrow in the same breath - open = the arrow, one action. `HandleScreenTransitions` lets the hero through the right door only when `not (FLevel.DoorShutOn(screen) and not FDoorOpened)`, as it does while the trial runs: a shut door is the wall the trial's door already was. `FDoorOpened` drops with every arrival on a screen, every restart (the pickup grows back with the field, and the door waits for it again) and every new level.
- **Screen 10:** the Duplet carries the tag `s10-duplet`; the event `s10-door` waits for `allDead` on it and opens the door. Until a screen 11 comes, the door it opens is the last door of the campaign (the ending titles).
- **Tests:** `Tests.Levels.Doors` - the list read, the action read, the four refusals at load.

## Stages 5b and 5c as built (data, no version)
Screen 11 of level 2 is all data, the rocks standing in their places; the stone's waking and its taking are 5d and 5e.

- **The art: one ChatGPT generation an element**, each on a clear ground (real alpha) with a silhouette of its geometry and the screen 10 composite for its style; what was done to each is in its description in the set. In `level2-objects.mset`: four fangs (`s11-fang-needle`, `-crown`, `-double`, `-leaning` - the last kept for the chase), three columns (`s11-column-a`, `-b`, `-c`, drawn 2 units past each edge of their cells), four rocks (`s11-rock-step`, `-block`, `-chunk`, `-shard`), the ledges in and out (`s11-way-in`, `s11-way-out`, cut pixel for pixel from `s10-room`). Near objects at 6 px a unit for the width they are drawn at, never above the generation's own pixels; every texture within 2048 px. In `level2-backdrops.mset`: `screen11`, the outpaint.
- **The moon stone is a broken lump, not an oval** (decided 2026-10-21): the first generation, an upright oval with a rim, read as a portal - its own file was named "black portal". The lump, 24 units wide (smaller than the hero, decided on the first look), in `moonstone.mset`: `lying` is a picture 56 units wide with the stone's middle 44 units from its left, so that the picture, centred on the cell of column 12, puts the stone on the middle of column C; `gone` is clear. The pickup `moonStone` (`bin/monsters.json`): no effect yet, no sound; taking it is what counts.
- **The level** (`bin/level2.json`): the grid of "Screen 11"; the backdrop from screen 11, tint 75/75/80; the objects back to front - the fangs tinted to their depth (needle 55/52/55, crown 45/43/46, double 40/38/42), the rocks of decoration (55/55/62, 70/70/78, 60/60/68), the columns, the ledges; the step as a still pad (`s11-step`, 290, deck on 256, 60 wide, blocks bullets); the dust of screen 10 with the shaft moved with the hole (from 161, 16 to 142, 470, the glow at 138, 430); the respawn point at cell 2, 11; `"shutDoors": [10, 11]` and the event `s11-door` (`allDead` on `s11-stone` -> `openDoor`).
- **The End debug key** stays on screen 10: the whole way from the Duplet on is seen in one walk.
- Until 5d the rocks stand risen from the start, and the step is a plain floor. Until a screen 12 comes, the door of screen 11 is the last door of the campaign.

## Stage 5d as built (3.0.67, tuned in 3.0.68)
The waking is a scene (Levels.Scenes) - `s11-wake`, once a level - started by an event the hero sets off by landing on column B. All but four pieces of it the scenes already had: the sound, the music, the shake, waking pads and flying them.

- **The condition `heroStands`** (`ecHeroStands`, Levels.Events): `"x"`, `"y"` from 1, as a placement's cell. The director asks the game (`THeroStandsCue`, `HeroStandsIn` of Moon2D.dpr): the hero's middle over the column, his feet on the bottom line of the row (to a unit), not dead, not in a jump or a fall. The load turns down a cell off the grid. Screen 11: `s11-wake`, x 8, y 8 - on B.
- **The back ease** (`meBack`, `"ease": "back"`, Levels.Dynamics `EasedShare`, now in the interface): an ease-out that runs about 4% of the way past its mark at two thirds of its time and settles onto it. A fly of a scene may take an ease now (`Eased` on the cue; `EasedFlight` of Pads.Flights, `TPad.FlyEased`); a fly without one brakes as a pad does, as before - the cordon's lift unchanged.
- **The pace** of a dynamic object (Levels.Dynamics): its clock's speed, 1 as the level file has it, faded as the intensity is (`PaceTo`, `TDynamicObjects.PaceTagged`), back to 1 on a rewind. A scene sets it with the cue `pace` (`ckPace`; a target and a value, a percentage). Only the dust minds it: its bands roll and sway by a clock that runs at its pace. The dust of screen 11 is stilled over 30 ticks as the stone wakes.
- **The eclipse** (`TEclipse`, kind `eclipse`, Levels.Dynamics; back layer by default - over the static objects and the pads, under the monsters, so the stone stays clear of its own dark): look `void` - the world darkened toward black in a soft disc of `reach` units, `depth` deep at full intensity, and a soft cold halo `rim` units across behind the heart; look `wave` - a ring of dark running out to `reach` as the intensity runs from 0 to 1, fading as it goes, gone at either end. The dark is drawn black at the alpha of a glow made at startup (`CreateGlowFromImage`, blend mode set to blend); the halo is added. A round ring for the rim was tried on the preview and dropped: round against the broken stone it read as a portal again.
- **The stone turns** (Monsters.Defs `TPictureDef`: `"turn"`, seconds a turn, `"hover"`, units; Monsters `DrawPicture` through `DrawSized`, which takes an angle now): the moon stone turns once in 20 seconds and hovers 1.5 units over 4. Its picture is the stone alone with 4 clear units over and under it (24 x 37) so it turns about its own middle and never touches its column; the placement's `"shiftX": 16` (Levels.Defs `TEntityPlacement.ShiftX`, taken by `TMonster.Create`) stands it on the middle of column C, which is two cells wide.
- **The rocks are pads** asleep under the bottom edge (y 384, `"dormant": true`): the step `s11-step` (still, blocks bullets) and three of decoration (`s11-rock-block`, `-chunk`, `-shard`; let bullets by, bob a unit). The decoration can be stood on - nothing up there leads anywhere. The chunk stands over the gap between A and B: over B it rose into the hero's head. The shard rises over the way out, to y 70, out of the hero's reach from C: over C it passed under the stone, and the stone - a monster, riding the decks as monsters do - went up with it.
- **The jolt** (`ckJolt`, cue `jolt`, 3.0.68): one blow to the screen, `value` a percentage of a trauma of 1 (the boss's blast). The director gathers the jolts of a tick and the game takes them once (`TSceneDirector.TakeJolt`); a scene cut short or put as an aftermath deals none. The rumble of `shake` alone is a tremble of about a unit at full: under a third it was not felt at all.
- **The scene** (about twice as slow in 3.0.68 - the first cut was over before it could be watched): the music ducks to 15% over 40 ticks and comes back over 99 from tick 340; `stonewake.wav` plays from tick 0; the dust is stilled over 30 from 0; the void fades in over 70 from 25, with a jolt of 20; the wave runs over 90 from 35; the rumble rises to 40% from 30 and to 80% from 60, gone from 260 over 80; the rocks wake and fly by `back`, each with a jolt as it comes free - the block at 60 (140 ticks, jolt 45), the step at 85 (120, 40), the chunk at 105 (130, 30), the shard at 125 (150, 30). The aftermath (a death after the scene): the dust stilled, the void and the wave at full, every rock awake in its place.
- **The sound:** `bin/sounds/stonewake.wav` by `tools/sounds/stonewake.py` - a drone made as the cordon's hum is (`cordon.py`): two stacks of 12 overtones at 41 Hz, half a hertz apart, the pitch settling from 6% above over the first 4 seconds and drifting a little, soft-clipped, then low-passed at 260 Hz; under it noise low-passed at 70 Hz. Half a second of nothing, awake by 4 seconds, held to 8.5, gone by 12.5 as the music comes back; 13 seconds. No cracks: clicks and a bright hum over noise sounded like a vacuum cleaner, not a ship's bulk.
- **The stone is anchored** (`"anchored": true` in monsters.json, Monsters.Defs `Anchored`, 3.0.68): no blow, no blast's throw and no deck moves it. A pickup of 2008 is knocked about by shots; the stone was shot off its column and left its halo hanging where it had lain. It is invulnerable too (`"invulnerable": true`, `Invulnerable`): a shot bursts on it and takes nothing - shot down, it would have opened the door without being taken.
- **The halo goes with the stone:** taking it (event `s11-door`) fades the void to nothing over 20 ticks. The wave is left alone - at full it is not seen, and faded back it would run in.
- **The shuttle** speaks as the hero comes onto the screen (event `s11-shuttle`, `enterScreen`): a hint at the top, header ЧЕЛНОК - "Мастер, это лунный камень. От него идут волны энергии." That the stone fits the suit is said later.
- **Left out:** the grit falling off the rocks' undersides and the rocks' tilt dying away - a pad neither sheds nor turns; worth it only if the live run asks.
- First guesses for the live run: the depth of the dark (in the dark corner of the stone it shows little; the wave shows over the lit wall), the halo's size and color, the overshoot, the music's dip, how loud the hum is.

## Stage 5e as built (3.0.69)
Taking the stone starts a second scene, `s11-fall`: the event `s11-door` (`allDead` on `s11-stone`) opens the door and starts it.

- **A scene takes over** (Scenes.Director): a scene started while another plays used to be dropped, and the waking runs about 13 seconds - longer than the way from B over the step to the stone. Now the new one takes over on the next tick, as a cut: the old one's things land (`TakeOver`), its world is put as its aftermath says - a rock still rising snaps to its place, to fall from there -, its sounds play on, still held, and the music, the shade and the rumble go on from where they are; the new scene's cues lead them on.
- **The rewinds before the aftermaths** (`ReArm`): a death rewinds the scenes it rewinds first, then puts the aftermath of every scene once a level that has played. Before, they went in the order of the file, and the fall - a scene of a life after the waking - took the dust back to the level file over the waking's aftermath, which stills it.
- **Once a life:** the field is reborn on a death, and the stone with it - the rocks must stand again for it to be taken. A death on screen 11 rewinds the fall, the waking's aftermath raises the rocks, the event waits for the stone again. A death elsewhere puts the waking's aftermath and leaves the fall alone: the stone is back on its column, the rocks are up under it.
- **The halo** fades in the fall (the void to 0 over 20), not in the event: the takeover lands the waking's own cues, the void at full among them, a tick after the event, and would have lit it again.
- **The fall** (33 ticks a second): the rocks are let go - no sag, no wind-up - and fall by the pull the hero falls by (`Gravity` of Hero.pas: 1/7 of a unit a tick, every tick), the moon's pull of the game. The ease is `fall` (`meFall`, Levels.Dynamics): the way grows as the square of the time, so a rock sets off at once, and the ticks of a fall are `sqrt(2 d / g)` for the distance d - a heavier or lighter pull is four numbers in the scene. Each rock drops to y 520, under the pit line (450) with its deck, so a hero falling into the pit never lands on it: the step from tick 10 (264 units, 61 ticks), the shard from 20 (450, 79), the chunk from 30 (305, 65), the block from 40 (370, 72) - nearest to the stone first. The music is not ducked again: the dip of the waking is the only one, and the fall gives the volume back over 99 from 150 for a stone taken before the waking has done so. The rumble to 30% over 25, gone from 110 over 60; `stonefall.wav` from 0. A jolt of 25 at 100 as they come down far below; the dust back to its pace over 90 from 60. The aftermath: every rock at y 520, the dust at its pace.
- **The sound:** `bin/sounds/stonefall.wav` by `tools/sounds/stonefall.py` - the drone of the waking powering down: the same two stacks at 41 Hz, the pitch sinking an octave over 3 seconds as the level dies by 3.2; a dull blow at 3 seconds (noise and a 30 Hz thump under a low-pass at 90 Hz, a 30 ms attack, 0.8 s decay); 7 seconds. No clicks.
- **A hero on the step** when it drops rides it down into the pit, as a pad carries him: the step goes first but 10 ticks after the stone is taken, by when he stands on C.
- **Tests:** two in `Tests.Scenes.Director` - the takeover, and the rewind of a scene of a life under the aftermath of a scene once a level.
- **The checkpoint** (3.0.71): the event `s11-door` moves the screen's checkpoint to the stone's column, cell 13, 6. The action `respawn` (`eaRespawn`, Levels.Events; `"x"`, `"y"` from 1 as a respawn point's, held at load to the rules of one - on the grid, out of a wall, over a floor), the cue `Respawn` (`TRespawnCue`, Events.Director), `MoveCheckpoint` of Moon2D.dpr. The pit is not a death: it takes a life and sends the hero back to the checkpoint with the field untouched, so a hero who took the stone and jumped back into the pit came back at the way in, the rocks gone and the stone taken, with no way up to C - a softlock. Now he comes back on C, a step from the way out. A death restarts the screen whole - the stone grows back, the rocks stand risen - and puts him on the same cell, by the stone; the screen's own respawn point, the way in, is pinned again after it.

## Stage 6 as built (3.0.70)
Not a bar: a small eclipse. Three bars were tried on demo stands (2026-10-22) - hollow, filled with a light edge, filled light - and dropped: a bar is the language of a loading screen, and the HUD already has two rectangles. The charge is the moon stone's, so it looks like the stone.

- **The gauge** (Hud.Energy): a black disc 20 units across in a pale halo, in a slot the heart monitor's panel grows on its right, 32 units wide - the twin of the reward slot on the left of the charge panel, so the two corner panels mirror each other. The charge runs round the rim as an arc of light from the top, clockwise, over a faint ring of the whole rim; a white point at its head. The number stands in the middle of the disc with a percent sign - "5" alone did not say five of what: the 3x5 digits at a pixel of 1.4 units (1.2 for "100", or its corners touch the rim), the sign a glyph of the same kind at 1.0, standing on the line. A rise eases the arc in and flashes the halo and the number; a fall (a death) is shown at once. Full, the ring breathes faster. No slot until the first of the charge comes; with the charge gone, it folds back over 8 ticks. Centered was tried and looked lost between the corners; the right panel was tried and crowded the reward slot.
- **The panel's wing** (Hud.Vitals `Wing`): the monitor draws its fill and frame that many units wider, with a divider where its own width ends; what is in the wing is not its business. The game passes the gauge's `WingWidth` every tick.
- **The arc** is drawn as strips of light along the circle (the band glow of the go-right arrow, `SDL_RenderGeometry` over `gsBeam`): round at any size of the window. The disc is a new glow shape, `gsDisc` (Render.Glow) - a disc with a crisp edge, two texels of a 64-pixel texture - drawn black through `CreateShadeShape` (the same shape blended, not added).
- **The orbs** (Orbs.Feed): taking the stone sends the charge out of the hero as black orbs - five at most, the charge split among them, so the stone's 5 is five orbs of 1 and the number counts up as they land. A beat after the taking (15 ticks) they come out of him one after another, 9 ticks apart, growing out of a point over 30 ticks to spots over his head (an arc of 70% of a half-circle, 22-30 units out), hang there 60-85 ticks swaying and keeping to him as he walks, then fly to the middle of the disc in 40 ticks, each on its own bowed curve, gathering speed (cubic), and go into it a little smaller. The first lands about 4.8 seconds after the taking, the last about 6. They strike nothing, and are drawn over the HUD with no shake, so they go into the gauge.
- **The dark orb** (Orbs.Flock, `TOrbTint.Dark`, `MoonOrbTint` - the colors of the moon burst): two units bigger than an ice orb - the halo 15 across, its thick part 10, a white rim 9.2 and a black body 7.6 over it, drawn last; an implosion flashes in the glow color, the black core would add nothing. The canvas of the dynamics has the disc twice now: `DiscGlow` added, `DiscShade` blended. The moon form will fly the same orbs.
- **The event:** the action `energy` (`eaEnergy`, Levels.Events), `"value"` 1..100; the director asks the game (`TEnergyCue`). Screen 11: `s11-door` gives 5 with the door and the fall. The game caps what it starts at what the gauge has room for.
- **A death gives back the charge the hero had on coming onto his screen** (`FEnergyAtEntry`): what a screen gave regrows with its field. Taken on screen 11 and died there - the stone is back, the gauge folds, the stone gives again. The orbs still on their way when he goes through the door count as the charge's already. The dead hero's orbs draw into their points and bring nothing. A new level starts at 0.
- **Not yet:** the tank, the trickle and the ceiling of the charge (stage 15) - the gauge only shows what it is given; the shuttle's line that the stone fits the suit.

## Stage 7a as built (3.0.72)
The life sphere is code alone; screen 12 gets it with its data (7c).

- **Game.Sphere**, two classes as the uptake: `TLifeSphere` counts (the phase, the sparks, the clock of the fill; no SDL), `TLifeSpherePainter` draws with the shapes of the dynamics' canvas - `DiscShade` for the body, `PointGlow` for the halo, the core, the glint, the bands and the sparks, `FlareGlow` for the flash it comes whole in. No texture of its own.
- **Not a monster**: no def, no art, no place in the field - so `allDead` does not see it (and on a tag no body carries it would hold at once). It stays on the screen it formed on until taken: a hero who walks back to screen 11 and returns finds it there.
- **The phases:** forming 53 ticks (26 sparks set off within 12 ticks from 40-100 units out, spiral in 1.25 turns by an ease-out, come in from tick 30 on; the sphere grows from tick 30 and flashes 8 ticks as it comes whole) - whole (breathes 8% over 50 ticks, sways 1.5 units over 132; three bands of 10 points turn inside it over 40, 55 and 70 ticks; two sparks orbit it over 45) - burst (a white swell over 5 ticks; 26 sparks fly out within 4 ticks along curves bent 18-40 units out and 12 up, 16-24 ticks each, slow out and fast in, into points of his body 12 by 20 units round his middle, the end taken from where he stands every tick) - done (a flash of 48 units on him for 8 ticks once all are in).
- **Only a whole sphere is touched.** The box is the hero's `HeroBody` against the sphere's square.
- **The light on his body** is the uptake's channel (`THeroShroud.Feed`): every spark in gives `2 * 0.9 / 26`, gone in 20 ticks. The ice suit's glow is not cut.
- **Health to 10 a pip at a time:** a pip every 4 ticks, the first in the tick of the touch; the game caps at `SphereHealth` (10). A hit while it fills does not stop it. The HUD needs nothing: `Hud.Vitals` and `Hud.Marks` flash every grown cell themselves.
- **Door, death, restart:** a door (`Settle`) gives the light of the sparks still on their way at once and the pips still due (health to 10); a death (`Drop`) stops the fill and the sparks fade where they are over 10 ticks; a restart and a new level clear it - the events re-arm, the line is typed again and the sphere forms again.
- **The sounds** (`tools/sounds/sphere.py`): `sphereform.wav`, 2.4 s - partials gliding from 330 to 990 Hz with a flutter each, ticks of light coming thicker, a chime C6 E6 G6 at 1.6 s as it comes whole; `spheretake.wav`, 1.6 s - a hiss and a thump of the burst, the glide again fast, 26 ticks as the sparks come in, a chime E6 G#6 B6 D#7.
- **The events:** the condition `hintTyped` (`"event"`: the id of an event of the screen with a hint) holds once that hint has been typed to its end, and stays true; the action `sphere` (`"x"`, `"y"` from 1, the middle of the sphere on the middle of the cell; `"size"`, 16 by default); the condition `sphereTaken`. The load turns down a sphere off the grid, a `sphereTaken` on a screen no event forms a sphere on, and a `hintTyped` naming no event of its screen with a hint. The director keeps the terminal's ticket of every hint it types (cues `Sphere`, `SphereTaken`).
- **The terminal keeps a line** (Hud.Terminal): a text started while another is typed or read waits, and takes the box when the one before has stood its reading time, in place of its fade. Before, a newcomer replaced the text mid-way. `Start` gives a ticket, `Typed` says whether the text of a ticket is out; a clear (a death) drops the line and forgets the tickets. A hint of the screen left behind still finishes on the next one.
- First guesses for the live run: the forming time and spiral, the size and brightness of the body, the bands' speed, the pace of the pips, the loudness of the two sounds.

## Stage 7c as built (data, no version)
Screen 12 of level 2 is all data, built before 7b: the far ledge stands still and empty - the convoys and the searchlight come with 7b.

- **The art** (`level2-objects.mset`, `level2-backdrops.mset`; what was done to each is in its description in the set): `screen12`, the generation resampled to 1440x1080; `s12-far-lip`, drawn by `tools/screen12/build_far_lip.py` (1440x14, drawn 512 wide); the pillars cut to their boxes at 6 px a unit for their heights (needle 6 by 43, twin 16 by 38, block 17 by 30, stump 16 by 23) and `s12-pillar-needle-mirrored` for the fifth - a static object has no mirror; `s12-crust` cut from its walking line, not resampled (4.66 px a unit); `s12-road-tile` made square, 192x192, opaque.
- **The far ledge: a lip, not a plank, and on screen 12 only.** The first cut was the generated ledge (`s12-far-ledge`), carried onto screens 10 and 11 at the slides of their backdrops - measured: 12 is 11 slid 480 px, 11 is 10 slid 506 px (180 units, not a third), so a thing at x on 12 stands at x + 171 on 11 and x + 351 on 10 (the plan's -341 and -171 had the sign wrong). On the first live run the plank spoiled the picture: it is gone from 10 and 11, and on 12 it gave way to the lip. Of four ways of a delicate line tried on a stand - a lit lip, a dotted road of lamps, a seam in the strata, a hairline rail with posts - the lit lip was taken: the lamps would argue with the convoys, the rail is TEK's language on natural rock.
- **The lip and the pillars stand still with the backdrop:** as static objects they shook with the world and came away from the wall on every jolt (seen on the first live run). They are crafts with a body and nothing else, on the `sky` layer, which stands while the world shakes, as the dust does; no scene moves them. The lip at 0, 215.2 (its rim on 217, the base of the gate; the gate's brightest point measured at 462, 216), untinted - its light is drawn in; the pillars centered on 50, 153, 256, 331, 395, feet a unit under the rim, tint 60/60/64 against the wall's 75/75/80.
- **The near matter of screen 12, back to front:** six road tiles at 320 + 32 k, 352; the crust at 0, 353 over them (its lip over col 10), as `s11-way-out` stands at 353.
- **The grid:** row 11 solid wall to wall, the rest air. The respawn point at cell 1, 11. `"shutDoors": [9, 10, 11, 12]`. The right door of screen 12 is the last door of the campaign until screen 13 comes.
- **The events:** `s12-shuttle-1` (`enterScreen`, delay 16 - half a second), `s12-shuttle-2` (`hintTyped` on the first), `s12-sphere` (`hintTyped` on the second: the sphere at cell 12, 10 - a cell over the hero's middle, over the road), `s12-door` (`sphereTaken`: `openDoor`).
- **The End debug key** stays on screen 10: the whole way from the Duplet to the sphere is seen in one walk.
- **Not yet:** the gauge blinking on the second line (no action does it); the convoys and the searchlight (7b). No dust on screen 12: the shaft of the vault is off its left edge.
- First guesses for the live run: the road tile against the dark (untinted, it may read too bright), the pillars' tint, the place of the sphere.

## Stage 7b as built (3.0.73)
Two new kinds of the dynamics (Levels.Dynamics), both on the `sky` layer by default: they stand while the world shakes, as the lip and the pillars do. No code of the events changed.

- **The order of drawing is the order of the file.** A layer is drawn in the order of the `dynamics` section, so the occlusion is the data's: `s12-far-lip`, `s12-convoys`, the five pillars, `s12-searchlight`. The convoy lights ride over the lip and the wall and pass behind the pillars; the searchlight is added over all of them. The whole `sky` layer is drawn under the static objects (the crust, the road), the sphere and the hero, so the light never falls on the playfield - by the order, not by care.
- **The hook:** `s12-shuttle-1` lights both with the shuttle's first line - `intensity` 100 on `s12-convoys` at once and on `s12-searchlight` over 20 ticks. Both stand in the file at 0, and each starts its own clock the tick it is first lit. A death needs nothing new: the re-arm of a screen's events (`TEventDirector.ReArm`) rewinds the targets of their `intensity` actions, and a rewind puts both out, empties the ledge and takes the spot back to its start; the line lights them again. Leaving for screen 11 and coming back rewinds nothing: the traffic has gone on unseen, as every dynamic object does.
- **The convoy** (`TConvoy`, kind `convoy`): one object for the traffic of the ledge, its lanes inside it - the second lane comes in with the second column of the first (`"joins": 2`), so its start is no number of its own. A column is 3-5 lights 3.2 units apart: a warm headlight in front (`tint`, a halo of `size` 3 units, 90%), dim running lights (55% of its size, 30%), a red tail light (`tail`, 75%, 70%), and on 35% of the columns (`flashers`) one running light is an orange flasher (`flasher`), a flash at 1.6 a second at a beat of its own. The gap from head to head narrows from 72 units to 34 over 25 seconds (`gap`, `ramp`) - the second column comes about 9 seconds in, while the shuttle is still typing; half the ledge (237 units, 30 seconds of one column alone) was tried first and left the screen empty for longer than anyone stays on it, each gap off by up to 25% either way (`jitter`). Which column has how many lights and which flashes follows from its number (`SlotRoll` of the placement's seed): the same traffic after every death. The lanes: the near one from (-16, 216.4) to (458, 216) at 8 units a second; the far one from (-16, 214.9) to (458, 215.2) at 6.4, its lights at 80% (`scale`). At the gate end of a lane a light keeps 70% of its size and light (`far`) - the ledge runs into the depth; every light fades over the last 20 units (`fade`) at either end. A column is let go once its tail is past the end of its lane.
- **The searchlight** (`TSearchlight`, kind `searchlight`): a lamp at the gate (462, 216), 9 units at 90%; a cone at 10% - in a vacuum the light is seen where it lands -, 2.5 units across at the lens and as wide as the spot where it lands; a spot that walks a figure of eight about (190, 200), 150 units each way across and 75 up and down, once in 11 seconds, starting a quarter in (`phase` 25) - at the right end, near the gate, going left. The eight covers x 40-340 and y 125-275: the left two thirds of the wall. The spot is a soft oval drawn long along the beam, 90 by 54 units where the beam falls on the middle of the eight (`spot`); it grows with the way from the source and dims as it grows, within 65-130% of its light there; a hot middle at 45% of its size. Cold light (88/94/100) against the warm light of the vault.
- **The pillars light up in their own shape:** the searchlight names them (`"catches"`, the tags of crafts) and draws each once more, added in its light (`TCraft.DrawLit`), as lit as the spot is on the point of the pillar nearest its middle, 60% of its light at most (`catch`). A searchlight that catches stands nailed itself; a caught craft must stand nailed on the searchlight's screens and layer and come before it in the list - the load refuses anything else (`FindCatches`, after the whole section is read). The eight crosses the height of the pillars only near its middle and its ends, so pillars a, c and d catch the light, b and e hardly ever.
- **What moved to be shared:** the lamp of the beacon (`DrawLamp`: spill, halo, core) lights the convoys and the searchlight's lamp; the cone of the beam (`DrawLightCone`, out of `TBeam`) is the searchlight's cone; `DrawGlowTurned` and `PointGlowLevel` joined Render.Glow for the oval spot and its falloff; a craft draws its pieces with an ink of its own (`DrawPieces`, `TPieceInk`: tint, opacity, painted or lit); the glows of Render.Glow hold their level to 0..1 themselves, so no caller clamps it. Which columns come and when is a class of its own, `TConvoySchedule`; the convoy only draws them. Its rolls take every other slot of `SlotRoll`: two slots that differ in the lowest bit alone roll the same.
- **Tests:** `TConvoyTests` and `TSearchlightTests` in `Tests.Levels.Dynamics` - the gap over the ramp, the line and the depth of a lane, the schedule (the first column alone, the second lane joining, the same columns after a rewind, a column let go past the finish, the lights and the flashers of a column), the figure of eight, what the files say and what they refuse, the catches found and refused.
- **Old cards:** dense traffic is about 28 columns, under 140 lights, three glows each - one texture, a few hundred copies a frame; the searchlight is a cone, two ovals and a lamp.
- First guesses for the live run: the spill of the running lights in dense traffic (they may run together into one glowing string), the size and light of the spot, how bright a caught pillar burns.

## Stage 8a as built (3.0.74)
Screen 13 of level 2 stands as data and art; the machine is 8b. Two words of code: the music hands over after a fade-out, and a door may be sealed for good.

- **The music** (3.0.74): what the vocabulary had - `music` with `fade` brings a track in from silence but cuts the one before (`PlayMusic` halts it); the scene cue `music` sets the volume of the track that plays and changes no track, so a scene ending at 0 would leave the next track mute; and a scene fading the track with an event changing it after a delay would remember the new track late - a death in the fade would restart into the old one. What was added: `"fadeOut"` (seconds) on `music` (`FadeOutSeconds`, Levels.Events; `SEventBadFadeOut` below 0). The track that plays fades out (`TSoundBank.HandOverMusic`, `Mix_FadeOutMusic`), the new one waits (`TQueuedTrack`) and starts, with its own fade-in, the tick the mixer is silent (`TSoundBank.Tick`, called by the game every tick in every state, so a fade runs on under the menu). The game remembers the new track at once (`ChangeMusic`), so a restart at any moment plays it. A track already playing is left alone: the restart starts the boss track, the re-armed event asks for it again and nothing happens. `PlayMusic` and `StopMusic` drop a waiting track. Tests: three in `Tests.Levels.Events` - the fade-out read beside the fade-in, none by default, below 0 refused.
- **The boss track:** `boss3.ogg` (the numbering of the music folder: boss2 is the gravel trial). Until it is made, a copy of `boss1b.ogg` stands under that name; the real one replaces the file, the level file does not change. The event `s13-music` (`enterScreen`): `boss3.ogg`, `fadeOut` 2.5, no fade-in - the boss track hits, it does not creep in. (Replaced in 3.0.75 by the Ram's own action, `s13-ram`: the music fades to silence as the Ram's entrance begins, and the track starts when its head locks - see "Stage 8b as built".)
- **The screen:** the 13th entry of `tiles.screens`, row 11 solid wall to wall, the rest air; the respawn point at cell 2, 11 - a cell off the left edge, room to step back from the machine. The left side is the one-way door of 2008. `"sealedDoors": [13]` (3.0.74, `Levels.Defs`): the way on out of 13 never opens - the game ends on this screen, by the blast and the titles, not through a door, so no arrow ever lights there. A sealed door is shut as a shut door is (`DoorShutOn`), takes no event to open it, and the load refuses an event that would (`SLevelDoorSealedOpens`) and a screen both shut and sealed (`SLevelDoorTwice`); three tests in `Tests.Levels.Doors`. Until the blast is built, screen 13 is a dead end. (A first try opened the door on arrival, `s13-door`, and lit the arrow - dropped.)
- **The road** (`tek-road-tile`, level2-objects; `s12-road-tile` gone from the set): one ChatGPT generation of three, a hex tread in gunmetal; the hazard stripe ran edge to edge and broke at every joint, so it is closed at both ends by caps cut from the plate's own side frame, mirrored. 192x192, 32 by 32 units, opaque. Sixteen of it on 13 (`s13-road-1`..`16`, tags by number for the belt of stage 11) and six on 12 in place of the old road, under the crust's lip as before.
- **The city pads** (`city-pad` 192x103, 32 by 17.1 units; `city-pad-long` 384x103, 64 by 17.1; level2-objects): one generation each with real alpha, the lenses of their nozzles measured at 16 (and 47), 15.5 units from the corner for the rigs to come. Placed on 13 as four pads (see the decisions of 2026-10-25 above); each wears a rig of its own kind, `cityPad` and `cityPadLong` - the lamp of the `pad` rig moved to the lens and turned the cold white of the gate (88/94/100, 14 across), the haze and the jet shorter (40 units, 0.25 s), so they cross less of the far ledge.
- **The backdrop** (`screen13`, level2-backdrops; `backgrounds` from screen 13, tint 75/75/80): the generation taken whole, resampled from 1448x1086 to 1440x1080.
- **The far ledge of 13** (dynamics, sky layer, in the order lip, convoys, pillars, searchlight): the lip is `s12-far-lip` again at -208, 212.9 - its gate end on the gate, its rim on 214.7, where the base of the gate stands; the pillars `s13-pillar-b` (block, 39.5, 185.7), `-d` (stump, 115, 192.7), `-e` (the mirrored needle, 184, 172.7), feet a unit under the rim, tint 60/60/64; the convoys as on 12, two lanes from x -16 to 250, at the gate; the searchlight from the gate (254, 213.7), its eight about (256, 170), 236 each way across and 70 up and down - x 20-492, y 100-240, the whole screen; its middle 44 units over the lamp, or the spot would sit on the lamp where the eight crosses -, once in 16 seconds (11 on 12, over a shorter way), catching the three pillars. Lit from the level's load (no `intensity` in the file - 100), not by an event: the traffic has run all the level by the time the hero arrives, and the re-arm of 13's events has no `intensity` to rewind. Nothing of 12 is carried by `"screens"`: the backdrop moved, so the ledge moved too.
- **The End debug key** (decided 2026-10-25, the full way every time): the gravel trial of screen 9 won at once - its triggers spent, its events re-armed, the breakthrough celebrated (`CelebrateBreakthrough`, taken out of `TickGravelAttack`) - so every press plays the false ending, the tunnel music fading in, the arrow, and the walk from 10 to 13 with the hand-over of the music at its end. `TrackBefore` is gone with the old jump to screen 10.
- First guesses for the live run: the fade-out's 2.5 seconds, the road's brightness, the sweep of the searchlight (its middle, reach and period), the convoy lanes' height against the stratum, the lip at 212.9.

## Stage 8b, the art (2026-10-26)
The machine as one sprite set, `bin/sprites/tek-machine.mset`, packed by `tools/machine/build_machine.py` from five ChatGPT generations (not in the repository): the machine whole (squared from a blockout of 4x4 cells, it came out 4 by 3), the same machine battle-worn, one wheel, the mast kit (three sections and the mortar), the heads (hunter, its barrel, sweeper, bunker). The game's TEK machines (shuttle, flying platform, tank, the first boss) were the style references. No code, no version.
- **Density** 6 px a unit, as the tank. The hull 169 by 128 units (1014x768 px), standing on the bottom edge - the tip of the ram.
- **Mirrored when packed:** the generations face right, the set faces left; every point in the descriptions is after the mirror.
- **Layers, back to front:** `well` (the back half of the dark of the mast well), `chassis` (the dark of the arches, fading below the axles), `wheel` twice (radius 20.5, axles at 61.1 and 133.2, y 107.5), `mortar` (rises from behind the rear block), `mast3`, `mast2`, `mast1`, `hull` (clear in the arches and over the back half of the well), `hullDamaged` (blended over it), then the head. The part of the mast and head over the well's middle line (y 14.6) is drawn again over the hull.
- **The worn hull** came back a little smaller and higher than the whole one; its box is mapped onto the whole one's (98.9% of the silhouettes agree after it) and it is cut by the whole one's silhouette.
- **The heads** sit with their socket (bottom centre) on the top collar of `mast3`. `hunterBarrel` turns about its pivot on the hunter's sleeve; `sweeperHeat` is the sweeper's barrels in white, tinted red by code; `bunkerLid` is the lid lifted off the bunker, shut by turning it 52 degrees about its hinge and moving it 5.5, -1.5 units.
- **Points** (hull, units): mast well centre 69.9, 14.6 (opening half-width 16.5); eye 22.2, 54.8; brake lamps 160.9, 43.3 and 51.2; exhausts 164.2, 14.9 and 28.5; mortar pivot 131.5, 23.3. The heads' sockets, muzzles, lens and hinge are in the set's descriptions.
- **Size:** 3.4 MB, most of it the two hulls; every texture within 1024 px.

## Stage 8b as built (3.0.75)
The machine is a unit of the game, as the cannons of arena 2 are, not a monster: `Game.Ram` - `TRam` the rules (no art, a test drives it), `TRamArt` the art, `TRamPainter` the drawing. The game carries out the cue of a tick (`TRamCue`): the sounds, the shake, the boss track; and the wound and the throw of a touch.

- **The action** `ram` (`eaRam`, Levels.Events; `TRamOrders`): `"stop"` (units, the tip of the ram after the drive-in, on the screen), `"file"` (the track of the fight), `"flightFile"` (since 3.0.115, the track from the honeycomb's fall on; the fight's by default), `"fadeOut"` (seconds the music before it takes to fade to silence). The director's cue `Ram` (`TRamStartCue`). Screen 13: the event `s13-ram` (`enterScreen`) - stop 260, `boss3.ogg`, 2.5 - in place of `s13-music`. A `silence` event, as the plan had it, would have been re-armed by a death and faded out the boss track that plays; the Ram knows which entrance it gives, so the music is its own.
- **The entrance** (`TRam.Start`): whole the first time on a level - True, and the game fades the music out to silence (`TSoundBank.FadeOutMusic`) and forgets the track a restart would bring back; from the stop after a death. A new level (`Reset`) sees it whole again; so does the End debug key. Whole: the fade-out and a beat of 16 ticks; then the rumble loop (`PlayLooped`) and the drive from 40 units beyond the right edge at 2.8 a tick; the brake - up to speed and down again by 0.2 a tick a tick, so the stop takes about 20 units -, the tip exactly on its mark; the brake lamps burn, the rumble cuts to the hiss, a jolt of 0.25; a breath of 16 ticks; the eye lights (`THull.Awake`) and the mast rises over 50 ticks (smoothstep) to the whine; the head locks - the cue `Locked`, and the game starts the track unless it already plays (`TSoundBank.Plays`). About 3 s from the edge to the stop.
- **A death** (`Clear`, then the re-armed event): the Ram stands at its stop, eye dark, mast down; the breath, the mast, the lock; the track plays on.
- **The track through a death, everywhere** (decided 2026-10-26): `RestartLevel` no longer starts over the track that already plays (`Plays`); a death brings back another track only when the current one is not it.
- **After the lock** (a first sketch of act 1, to be talked over after the live run): a rest of a second, then legs to marks of its own anywhere the hull fits between the walls (8 units off them in the first build; to the walls since the polish below), a leg 48 units at least, at 1.4 a tick, a rest of 1.5 to 3 s between them (`TXorShift` of its own); every leg with the rumble, every stop with the hiss, the lamps and a jolt of 0.12. Backing right, the wheels turn the other way. The head does not shoot (stage 9).
- **The mast** (`TRamPainter.DrawMast`): its height is how far the socket of the head stands over the middle line of the well (y 14.6): -20 at rest (the head under the opening, out of sight), 48 for the hunter (first guess). Each section's top rises its share of the rise (a third, two thirds, all), the narrowest drawn first; the head on the top collar, the barrel level on its sleeve. Drawn twice: behind the hull, clipped above y 90 of the hull (lower, the sections would show through the open arch over the front wheel), and over it, clipped above the well line (`TSpriteRenderer.ClipAbove` / `ClearClip`, the binding `SDL_RenderSetClipRect`). The sway: a spring the drive's speed-ups and brakes kick (about 4 degrees forward on the brake), over a slow sway of its own (0.6 degrees over 150 ticks); the whole mast turns about the middle of the well.
- **The hull** draws through `THull` (Monsters.Hull), its draw split in two - `DrawUnder` (the arches, the wheels) and `DrawBody` (the hull, the worn copy, the eye) - so the mast goes between them; `Draw` is both. The order: the well, the arches and wheels, the mast behind, the hull and its eye, the mast over, the brake lamps (red glows at the lamp points). The mortar is not drawn yet (stage 12). On the world's channel, after the static objects and before the pads: it drives behind them.
- **The shield over the body** (`TTekShield.CreateOver`, Game.Shield; `TShieldMask`): the body read off the art at load (`TRamArt.Body`: a unit is solid where `hull` or `chassis` is half opaque at its middle, or inside a wheel), two units wider on every side but the floor. The honeycomb of the cannons, its rim share by how deep a cell lies under the outline (a chamfer of two passes; 28 units are the whole rim - the width of a cannon's rim), so the cells glow along the outline, none along the floor. A hero's bullet that meets it flares the cells and is gone (`BulletStruckRam`); the mast and the head let bullets by until the heads are targets.
- **The contact** (`TRam.Contact`, `ResolveRamContact`): the hero's body against the body mask. A touch wounds (the mercy holds as against a bullet) and throws him away from the machine's middle (`THero.Throw`: a jump to that side at a boost of 7 - about 75 units up, over a low pad from the floor - with the controls out of his hands for 14 ticks, or the jump key he does not hold would cut the rise). The machine drives behind the pads: a hero standing on one is out of its way, and stays so in the air while he is over it; he is in its way again once he comes down onto the floor or leaves it. A fall onto it from above is a touch.
- **The sounds** (`tools/sounds/ram.py`): `ramrumble.wav` - a 2 s loop with no seam (every part repeats a whole number of times in it, its noise shaped round the loop): the engine at 31 Hz with its overtones and a 4 Hz throb, the cleats striking the road 13 times a second, the grind of the road; `ramhiss.wav` - a clunk of the brake and the air let out of it, 1.3 s; `ramwhine.wav` - a pump climbing from 150 to 330 Hz over 1.5 s with its strokes over it, and the clank of the lock, 1.75 s.
- **The boss track** `boss3.ogg` is the real one (Vorbis, stereo, 160 kbit/s, as every track of the folder).
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Ram`, 16): the whole entrance waits out the silence, drives in and stops at its mark, the head locks after the breath; the replay from the stop; a new level sees the whole entrance; the legs keep off the walls; the touch on the floor, the hero on a pad, the fall onto it, the side of the throw; the shield takes a bullet, an absent machine nothing; the shield over a body holds it and a margin and nothing under its foot; the action read and refused; the arena of level 2 brings the Ram.
- First guesses for the live run: the drive-in's speed and the brake, the breath, the mast's rise and the hunter's height, the sway, the throw's height and lock, the patrol's speed, marks and rests, how loud the rumble is under the silence, the shield's rim depth on a body this big.

## Stage 8b polish (3.0.75)
After the first live run (decided 2026-10-26). The version stays 3.0.75: polish of the same machine, the version not yet closed.

- **The exhausts smoke** (`TRamPainter`, two `TSmoke` of `ExhaustSmoke` - a body smoke as the monsters' are, made by `TSmoke.CreateLook`): out of the mouths of the two pipes on the stern, at x 167.3 (the mouths' faces, the right edge of the silhouette; the points 164.2 of the set are the openings' middles, inside the pipes), y 14.9 and 28.5. A jet thrown back to the right by its own speed (angle 8, cone 24, 34 units a second, drag 60%), with no lift and no wind - there is no air; puffs 4 times a second, as the rumble throbs. A little at rest (`ExhaustIdle` 30%), full while it drives, 8 ticks between. Drawn before the body, so a machine backing right drives into its own cloud and it goes behind it; driving left it leaves a trail. A new start rewinds it (`TRam.Arrivals`).
- **The eye is the Ram's own** (the hull's eye dozes for good: it fades 15% a tick, made for the flash of a shot, and would smear a fast blink): dark until lit; it breathes slowly at rest and while it drives (0.3 to 0.55 over 2.5 s); it blinks fast only as a warning - now while a head rises (3 ticks a half, a flash against next to nothing), later the change of heads (stage 9) and the brake lurch (stage 14). Its size grows with the square of its level: the breath barely swells it, a blink flashes to 9 units.
- **The lens of the head winks** once the head has locked - a short wink a second over a faint ember, the light that says the gun is armed; out with the machine. Pale, the cold white of the city pads: red on this machine is a warning (the eye's blink, the brake lamps, the sweeper's barrels), and a red light that burns all the time would teach the player to ignore it. The red flare before a burst is stage 9's - bigger, held.
- **No floor by a wall is safe.** The first build stopped 8 units off the walls, and the cell at a wall seemed safe; it was safe by chance, not by geometry - at hero height the stern reaches its widest, and a mark in the last 15 units of the range touched a hero there, about one leg in twenty. Now the patrol goes to the walls, and one leg in three (`WallLegShare`) ends at the far wall.
- **Pinned at a wall, thrown straight up.** A touch throws the hero away from the machine's middle; at a wall that was into the wall, back into the hull, and the throw again every 14 ticks while it stood there - a wound at every end of the mercy. Now the side away from the middle that leaves him less room to the screen's edge than his body's width throws him straight up (`TRam.ThrowSide`, `THero.Throw(0)`): from the floor at a wall he climbs about 77 units and comes down onto the low pad by that wall (`s13-pad-1`, `s13-pad-4`, 72 over the floor), which stands there for it. And a hero the machine threw is out of its way until he lands (`FHeroThrown`): no new throw in the air.
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Ram`, 22): some legs end at a wall, not all; the eye blinks while the head rises and breathes after it, never a flash at rest; the lens is dark until the lock, then winks over an ember, and goes out with the machine; a hero pinned at the right wall and at the left is thrown straight up; a thrown hero is out of its way in the air and in it again on the road.
- First guesses for the live run: the smoke's density at rest and in a drive, its colour against the dark wall; the breath's pace; the lens's wink; how often a leg goes to a wall; that the straight-up throw at a wall lands on the wall pad.

## Stage 9 as built (3.0.76)
The hunter and act 1's two modes, in `Game.Ram` (`TRam` the rules, `TRamPainter` the drawing); the game carries out two more cues.

- **What it sees.** `TRam.Tick(target)` takes the hero (`TRamTarget`: alive, body, standing on a pad); `Tick` with none is the old roam (one leg in three to the far wall) - a dead hero, and the tests of 8b. Where he is and how fast he moves (learnt by 0.3 a tick) - for the lead.
- **Low or high.** Low is feet under `LowLine` (the road less two cells, 288; the low pads' decks stand at 280) and not on a pad. Low for `PressAfterTicks` 10 - the press. It lets go once he has stood on a pad `LeaveOnDeckTicks` 6, or kept over the line `LeaveAboveTicks` 33, or died: a hero hopping on the road is pressed through his jumps.
