- **The press.** A rest ends at once; a leg toward him runs on at the press's pace, one away from him brakes first (`StopAhead`, never past a wall). Then a rev (`rpRevving`, 8 ticks: the rumble, the exhausts full, a jolt of 0.06) and to the wall on his side (`PressWall`; the other one when it already stands there) at 1.9 a tick - the hero runs at 2 - and 2.1 at the last third; a rest of 13 ticks, then it looks again. At the wall the throw goes straight up onto the wall pad (8b polish), and on the pad he is hunted.
- **The hunt.** Legs of 32-96 units at 1.1, rests of 15-40 ticks; a leg goes where the barrel still reaches him (`InSightsFrom`: no lower than its lowest), or failing that the farthest from him; out of its sights it stands no more than 5 ticks.
- **The barrel.** Turns on the sleeve within its half - from straight up to 25 degrees under level on the side the head faces (`AimWithin`) - at 3 degrees a tick toward the hero. Past the vertical by 15 degrees for 10 ticks the head turns: 10 ticks, squashed across its socket (`TurnSquash`) and mirrored at the middle, the barrel with it; never in a burst, and no shot while it turns.
- **The bursts** (`TFirePattern` by the thirds of its lives): the lens burns red (`LensHot`, bigger, brighter as it goes) for 20 / 16 / 14 ticks, the burst is 3 / 3 / 6 shots 5 / 5 / 6 ticks apart, then a rest of 50 / 40 / 45 ticks. The first two thirds hold the burst on a point taken as the lens lights - where he stood, then where he will be if he keeps going (the lead, at most 80 units) - and the barrel keeps on that point while the machine drives; the last third is the hose: the barrel follows him at 2 degrees a tick through the burst. A burst begins once the barrel is within 3 degrees of him and he is in its sights; the first one a second after the lock. A press puts out a lens that burns, lets a burst under way finish and starts none. Every burst about 2.2 s from the last at the first third.
- **The shot.** The cue `Shot` (`ShotFrom` - the muzzle, along the barrel 30.7 from the sleeve; `ShotAngle`): the game fires a monster bullet at `HunterBulletSpeed` 6 (its picture hangs a sprite over its point, so the bullet goes in a sprite lower) with the cannons' `shoot2.wav`. Monster bullets: they wound as theirs do, and the hero's shots can meet them.
- **The eye.** `StrikeEye(from, to, at)` - a bullet's stretch against a circle of 5 units about the lens (`LensPoint`): a life gone, the lens flashes white for 4 ticks (unless it burns red - the warning comes first). Only a hunter up and locked has an eye. The game asks the eye before the shield (`BulletStruckRamEye`): sparks off the lens (`FaceFromCenter`), the armour's ping. Lives: `HunterLives` 100 times the grade's monster lives (`DifficultyMonsterLives`: 1, 1.5, 2), set on every start - a death restarts the fight with a whole hunter.
- **The row of lives** (asked after the first build): `HeadRowCells` 12 cells over the head, four a third, in the thirds' colors - the same thirds the bursts change by. As the head locks the row comes up a cell at a time over `RowBootTicks` 30 to the terminal's key, and the lens burns white meanwhile: this is what has lives. The first burst comes after it. A hit reddens the frame and whitens the cell it emptied into; the row stays while the head is up, and a dead head's row, empty, fades over 20 ticks. The rules in `TRam` (`Row`, `TickRow`, `HeadTopAt`, the cue `RowCell`), the drawing in `Hud.Marks` (`DrawRam`) with the monsters' rows.
- **Wounded.** At the last third the head smokes (`HeadSmokeLook`, thin and slow, no lift).
- **The change of heads.** The last life blows the head up on the next tick (the cue `Blast` at its middle: an `ekMachine` explosion, `platform.wav`, a jolt of 0.5). A driving machine brakes to a stand first; then `rpChanging`: the empty mast sinks over 25 ticks, smoking, a beat of 5, the sweeper rises over 30 to the whine, and the fight goes on - the eye blinks all through. The sweeper stands silent at a mast of 48 until stage 10; bullets pass it.
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Ram`, a fixture of its own, `TRamHunterTests`, 15): the lives by the grade; only the eye takes a hit; the last life blows the head up, the eye blinks through the change, nothing fires and the sweeper rises; a hero low is pressed to the wall on his side and not shot at; one on a low pad is not pressed; the lens burns before the first shot; the bursts are apart; the first shots fly at him; a hero past the vertical turns the head; the last third fires six; no row before the lock, the row up a cell at a time before the first shot with the eye white, the row following the lives, a dead head's row fading.
- First guesses for the live run: the lives, the lens's time, the bursts and the rests, the press's speed and the low line, the hunt's legs, the lead, the eye's radius, the sweeper's height.

## Stage 10a as built (3.0.77)
The sweeper, in `Game.Ram` beside the hunter; the game fires its bullets and asks its barrels.

- **Its lock.** The change of heads ends in `LockSweeper`: `SweeperLives` 140 times the grade's (the share kept from the start), a whole row coming up cell by cell with the terminal's key as the hunter's does, the barrels white meanwhile (`Booting`), the first sweep a second after the lock.
- **The ride** (`TickRide`). The head rides the mast at 1.5 a tick between `SweeperMastLowest` 12 (the barrels' bottom just over the roof) and `SweeperMastHighest` 96 (three cells): the barrels' row from 216 down to 132 on the screen. Idle it follows the hero's row; while the ram presses him it sinks to the bottom - the closed ceiling. A hero past its socket on the other side by 8 units for 10 ticks turns the head, as the hunter turns, never mid-sweep.
- **The sweep** (`TickSweep`, `TSweepPattern` by the thirds): on its row (within 4 units) with the hero in front, the barrels burn red (`sweeperHeat` tinted by code, `HeatAt` from 0.4 up) for 22 / 18 / 16 ticks - the countdown waits until the head is on its row - then a stream level along the row, out of the four muzzles in turn (`ShootBarrel`, 3 ticks apart, `SweeperBulletSpeed` 5; the cue carries `ShotSpeed` now, the hunter's 6). First third: 8 shots, a rest of 55. Second: the fork - 8 shots, then at once (heat 10) a row a cell away, up unless he is falling or up is past the mast (`ForkRow`), rest 50. Last: the ladder - 12 shots while the row rides 1.3 a tick toward the side with more room, rest 50. A press does not hold its fire: the closed ceiling burns while the ram comes.
- **The target** (`StrikeBarrels`, `BarrelsPoint`). The stretch is taken into the head's own units (`ToHead`: the sway, the facing and the squash undone) and walked half a unit at a time: the pack is 17.5 by 12 units of the art (x 0-17.5, y 2-14), the plates under it to y 18. A bullet met under the pack, or one climbing more than 6 degrees into it, is guarded: the nearer plate flares, nothing is lost. Else a life goes and the barrels flash white for 4 ticks. A head squashed under half in a turn is not hit. The game: `BulletStruckRamBarrels` between the eye and the shield - sparks off the pack's middle and the armour's ping for a hit, nothing but the plate's light for a guard.
- **Two more of the same version.** The Duplet's recoil is gone (see stage 3b). A death in the Ram's fight - once its entrance has begun on the level (`TRam.Entered`) - brings the hero back with 10 health, the whole of it with the bonus (`RamRestartHealth`, Game.Moon `RestartLevel`): every death there restarts the whole fight.
- **The plates.** Two strips of two cells at 0.6 of a cell under the barrels (`PlateMiddles` 4.5 and 13 units along, 16 down) - `TTekShield.CreateStrip` (Game.Shield: a short row of small cells, all rim, a box to hold), drawn with the hull's shield, turned with the head's facing.
- **Its death.** The head blows up as the hunter's; the empty mast sinks and stays down (`FKilled`), smoking - the next head comes with its own stage. The machine keeps pressing and roaming.
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Ram`, `TRamSweeperTests`, 11; `TBodyShieldTests` one more for the strip): its lives by the grade; it rides to the hero's row; a hero on the road closes the ceiling at the bottom of the mast, never under the roof; the barrels burn before a sweep; a sweep is level along his row at its own speed; the whole pack takes a hit from above and level, nothing over it; the plates take a bullet from below and a climbing one; the middle third forks a cell away; the last third rides a ladder; it turns to a hero on its other side; the last life leaves the mast empty.
- First guesses for the live run: the lives, the heat, the rests, the ride and the ladder's pace, the plates' cover angle, the bullets' speed, the shot's sound on every bullet of the stream.

## Stage 10b as built (3.0.78)
The bolts of both heads, in `Game.Ram` - the rule in `TRam`, the bolts in `TRamPainter`.

- **The rule** (`ShortLevel`). Nothing while the head on the mast has over half its lives; at half the tank wreck's floor, 0.35 (`ShortFloor`), up to 1 at its last life (`ShortSpan` 0.65) - the share of the lower half gone. No head, no bolts. The same for the hunter and the sweeper.
- **The bolts.** A `TLightning` a head (`HeadShorts`): the tank's `WreckShort` - spans, no strike, a cold blue-grey halo, two strokes, a flash of 0.55, no jolt - its box about the head: 10 by 6 on the hunter, 16 by 6 on the sweeper. The level is the source's intensity: at half about a bolt a second, at the last life about two, longer and thicker. Ticked over the head's middle as it sways and turns (`TickShorts`), drawn first in `DrawLight`, over the head. The head blown up stops making them; the bolts in the air die out. A machine come anew rewinds them with its smoke (`TickArrival`, taken out of `TickExhausts`).
- **The hunter's smoke** at its last third stays as it was.
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Ram`, `TestShortsOutFromHalfItsLives` in `TRamHunterTests` and `TRamSweeperTests`): none over half, some at half, harder at the last life, none from a head blown up.
- First guesses for the live run: the floor and the frequency (the tank's), the boxes, whether the sweeper's bolts read against its red barrels.

## Stage 10c-1 as built (3.0.79)
The sweeper's end, in `Game.Ram`; the game plays its sound.

- **The overload** (`TickKill`, `BeginOverload`). The last life of the sweeper does not blow the head up on the next tick, as the hunter's does: for `OverloadTicks` 20 (about 0.6 s) it overloads. The gun falls silent and the head stands where it is - no ride, no turn, no hit (`StrikeBarrels` takes nothing); the press lets go; a machine that drives brakes to a stand (`StopAhead`), one that revs or rests stays standing. The eye blinks fast, as through the change of heads. `Overloading`, `Overload` (0 to 1).
- **The look** (`TRamPainter`). The head whitens: its art added over itself at up to 0.9 of the overload (`DrawOverload`), the barrels white with it (`DrawHeat`); the shorts at their full, and over them the thick bolts of the overload (`OverloadShortLook`: reach 24, size 1.8, nine a second at the end, three strokes).
- **The blast.** At its end the head bursts as the hunter's does (`KillHead`: the explosion, the empty mast sinking and staying down), but the cue says `Shock`: the jolt is 0.8 (`SweeperBlastJolt`, the hunter's 0.5), and the white flash (`FlashAt`) covers the whole frame at 0.85 and fades over `BlastFlashTicks` 6, with a light of 160 units about the head (`DrawFlash`, last in `DrawLight`). The game plays `sweeperblast.wav` in place of the hunter's `platform.wav`.
- **The sound** (`tools/sounds/sweeperblast.py`, 3.8 s): a thump from 70 down to 32 Hz over a floor of low noise, then two stacks of overtones beating at 0.6 Hz, from 44 Hz sinking to 24 as they fade - the stone's wake going down. A low-pass at 240 Hz over all of it: no crack. *Since 3.0.83 under the tank's death, a low-pass at 700 Hz - see "The wrecks and the blast retuned".*
- **Tests** (`TRamSweeperTests`): the last life overloads before the blast (no fire, the eye blinking, the overload rising, then a shock, a jolt over the hunter's and the white); the machine pressing a hero brakes to a stand through it and starts no new drive; the hunter's blast carries no shock; the old test of the empty mast waits out the overload.
- First guesses for the live run: the overload's length, the jolt, the flash's strength and length, the head's white, the thick bolts, the sound's level against the boss track.

## Stage 10c-2 as built (3.0.80)
The sweeper's shock wave, in `Game.Ram`; the game throws the hero.

- **The rule.** The sweeper's blast starts the wave at the head (`BlastAt`): its front runs out at an even pace to the farthest corner of the screen (`WaveReach`) in `WaveTicks` 33 - about a second - and is gone. `WaveFrontAt` between ticks; none at all after a death (`Clear`) or a new start.
- **The throw** (`WaveStrikes`). Once a wave, the tick the front reaches the middle of the hero's body: he is thrown as a touch of the hull throws (`THero.Throw`), out of the machine's way until he comes down (`FHeroThrown`), with the moment of mercy (`HurtMercyTicks`) but no wound and no pain sound (`TMoonGame.ResolveRamWave`, after the contact). The side (`WaveSide`): away from the hull when he is clear of it; from over it - on the middle pad, in the air over the roof - toward the side with more road.
- **The look** (`TRamPainter`, colourless, as a shock wave in film). The dark band: a soft ring just inside the edge of its own square (`CreateWaveBand`, 256 px, at 0.93 of the half side, 0.05 across), drawn black at 0.6 behind the front (`DrawWave`); the front: a thin white ring of triangles 1.2 units across at 0.85 (`SDL_RenderGeometry`, 96 segments). Both fade as the wave runs out. Drawn in `DrawLight`, under the blast's white.
- **The dust.** Where the front runs along the road - a point each side of the blast, once the front is past the road's line - two dust sources ride with it (`FWaveDust`, `WaveDustLook`: grey, thrown up a little, no air to carry it, settling as it fades); what they put out stays where it rose. Drawn first in `DrawLight`.
- **Tests** (`TRamSweeperTests`, 3 more): the blast sends a wave that runs past every corner in about a second, and a death takes it away; it throws the hero once, away from the hull; from over the hull it throws toward the side with more road.
- First guesses for the live run: the wave's pace, the band's width and darkness, the front's brightness, the dust's amount, whether the throw from the middle pad clears the hull.

## Stage 10c-3 as built (3.0.81)
The pads off, in a unit of their own, `Game.Wrecks` (`TPadWrecks` the rules, `TPadWreckPainter` the drawing); the pad goes to sleep in `Pads.World`; the game knocks them off.

- **The knock** (`TMoonGame.KnockOffPads`). Every pad of the screen that stands (`TPadWorld.Standing`) whose middle the front has reached (`TRam.WaveReaches`) goes to sleep (`TPad.Doze`, the dormant pad of 3.0.62 once more: neither a floor nor a body, not drawn, what hangs on it out) and a wreck is launched in its picture from its middle, away from the blast. The nearest first, as the front runs. It is done after the pads' tick and before the hero's: a hero who stood on the pad falls the same tick (`DeckWithdrawn` sees a deck gone this tick only).
- **The flight** (`TPadWrecks`, the numbers of the second stand): off at 2 a tick away from the blast, at least 40 degrees up; the heading eases at 0.05 a tick to 30 degrees over the level, outward, a thrust of 0.2 along it, the hero's pull of 1/7 and a drag of 0.03 a tick. The picture spins on its own: from 18 degrees a tick, 0.4 faster every tick, no faster than 45, clockwise to the right, the other way to the left. Gone 60 units past the left, the right or the top edge. *Retuned in 3.0.83 - see "The wrecks and the blast retuned".*
- **The look** (`TPadWreckPainter`). The pad's own picture in its placement's tint, turned with the spin (`DrawSized`); under it the fire of its jet - a smoke of heat 1 out of a point 6 under its middle, turned with it, cooling to a grey smoke that stays where it rose, so the spiral leaves a burning trail; over it an orange glow breathing about 0.45, sparks out of the jet, small blue-grey bolts over the deck. A gone wreck puts out nothing more, and what it put out burns out. The fire before the dynamics of the back layer, the light before the Ram's (the wave and the blast's white go over it).
- **No wound.** A wreck touches nothing: the hero passes through it, bullets pass it.
- **A death gives them back**, and not by a scene: the blast is the Ram's, not a scene of the level, so there is no aftermath to put. A death rewinds the pads to the level file (`TPadWorld.Rewind` - a dozed pad wakes, as the file has it awake) and clears the wrecks with the Ram (`FWrecks.Clear` where `FRam.Clear` or `Reset` is); every death restarts the whole fight anyway. ("For the code" above spoke of the aftermath of the blast's scene: a scene's aftermath keeps what it did after a death - the opposite.)
- **The wave's fields** are folded into a record, `TShockWave`, with `WaveReaches` for the pads.
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Wrecks`, 4): away from the blast and up on either side; from any point over the roof the head may burst at, none of the arena's five comes down onto the road, and all are gone within five seconds (the slowest about 3.8 s); the spin grows to no more than its most, the way it flies; a clear. `Tests.Pads.World` 2 more (a dozed pad is gone and not among the standing; a rewind gives it back), `TRamSweeperTests` 1 more (the wave reaches the near before the far).
- First guesses for the live run: the whole flight (speed, climb, drag, spin), the fire's size and life, the glow, the sparks, the bolts.

## Stage 10c-4 as built (3.0.82)
The Ram backs off before the sweeper bursts, in `Game.Ram`; the game sounds the alarm.

- **The retreat** (`TickRetreat`, `BeginRetreat` - the old `BeginOverload` - `Retreating`). The sweeper's last life sets it going (`StrikeBarrels`): the gun is silent, the head stands, nobody is pressed, a machine that drives brakes to a stand. It stands `RetreatStandTicks` 16 (about half a second), then drives to the right wall (`ScreenWidth - RamWidth`) at `RetreatSpeed` 2.5 - from the far wall about 4.5 s. Standing at the wall, the retreat is over and the overload of 10c-1 begins there (`Overloading` is False while it retreats).
- **The warning.** The barrels throb red, `HeatAt` from 0.6 to 1 every `RetreatThrobTicks` 16; at the wall the overload whitens them. The eye blinks fast all the way, as through the overload. The cue `Alarm` every `AlarmEveryTicks` 50 (1.5 s; the hum is 1.2 s) from the first tick; the game plays `padhum.wav` - the warning of an arena rebuild.
- **The touch** stays as it was: a hero in its way is wounded and thrown, straight up at the wall.
- **Spent** (`Spent`: no head, the sweeper the last killed). After its blast the machine stands at the wall for good - no press, no legs - until the bunker comes (stage 14). Before, it roamed with an empty mast.
- **The way out of the hull** (`FClearTicks`, the whole fight, not only its end). Every throw - a touch (`Contact`) or the wave (`WaveStrikes`) - opens a way out: a hero who comes down inside the hull is not touched for `ClearTicks` 60 counted from his landing (from its middle at a run he is out in about 45). Out of the hull, the way closes at once; the time over, a touch is a touch again. Without it a throw from the low pad by the right wall, or a short throw from the middle of a moving hull, landed him inside it - a wound, a throw, another wound - and a hero pinned at a wall with no pad over him was wounded at every end of the mercy.
- **Tests** (`TRamSweeperTests`; the blast now waits for the retreat - `EndLimit` 300 ticks in place of `OverloadLimit` 30). `TestBacksToTheRightWallBeforeTheOverload` in place of `TestTheMachineStandsThroughTheOverload`: a press to the left wall when the last life goes - brakes, stands at least 10 ticks, backs to the right and never forward again, barrels red, the alarm, no fire and nobody pressed; the overload and the blast at the wall. `TestTheWaveLeavesHimInFrontTillHeIsClear` (new): thrown left, down inside the hull no touch, clear of it, back into it a touch. `TestTheLastLifeLeavesTheMastEmpty` - it stands at the right wall after. `TestTheLastLifeOverloadsBeforeTheBlast` skips the retreat. `TRamTests`: `TestThrownHeroIsOutOfItsWayTillHeLands` - down inside, no touch; clear, then back in, a touch; `TestTheWayOutOfTheHullCloses` (new) - unhurt inside at least 45 ticks, touched within 90; `TestThrownAwayFromTheMiddle` steps out between its two touches.
- First guesses for the live run: the stand, the speed, the alarm's period, the red's throb, the length of the way out of the hull.

## The wrecks and the blast retuned (3.0.83)
After the live run of 3.0.82 the wrecks looked false, without a plain reason why. Measured on a model of `TPadWrecks`: every wreck left at the same 2 a tick and then sped up on its thrust - a tow, not a blast; the kick did not care how near the head the pad stood; all spun at 18 degrees a tick from the first tick and faster up to 45, the same rate on all, the side the only difference; and all eased to one heading of 30 degrees and one top speed, so in about 45 ticks they flew in formation. At 45 a tick the pad's picture, alike every half turn, shows four positions a half turn - a propeller. Since the retreat the head bursts at a known spot, so it was the same parade every time. The sound: `sweeperblast.wav` lay 96% under 100 Hz under a low-pass of 240 - on laptop speakers and most headphones nothing but the shake; and the sweeper played it in place of the hunter's blast, so there was no bang at all.

- **The kick** (`Launch` takes the reach of the wave, `FRam.WaveReach`, from `KnockOffPads`): from `WreckKickNear` 7 next to the blast down to `WreckKickFar` 3.5 where the wave dies, by the distance; at least 40 degrees up as before. The thrust is 0.15 (was 0.2), weaker than the drag takes off a near kick: the wreck slows down, then the jet holds about 4.9 a tick.
- **Each its own** (`WreckVariety`: a steady hash of the wreck's turn and a salt - the same blast throws the same wrecks, no two alike): a climb of 20 to 40 degrees (`WreckClimbLeast`, `WreckClimbSpan`; at 45 the slowest stays on the screen about five seconds), a spin of 5 to 14 degrees a tick at a full kick and half of it where the wave dies (`WreckSpinLeast`, `WreckSpinSpan`, `WreckSpinFarShare`), a spin-up of up to 0.3 a tick (`WreckSpinGainMost`), one in five spinning against the others (`WreckCounterSpinShare` 0.2); no faster than `WreckSpinMost` 24. The record keeps `Climb` and `SpinGain`; the spin keeps its own sign, not the side's.
- **On the model** (the same formulas over every blast over the roof): no pad lower than about 290 (the road at 352), the last off the screen in 156 ticks, under the test's 165.
- **The sound.** On `Cue.Shock` the game (`TickRam`) plays the tank's death - `bottle.wav` and `platform.wav`, as `monsters.json` gives it - and `sweeperblast.wav` under it, its drone after the bang; the hunter keeps `platform.wav` alone. `sweeperblast.py`: the low-pass up to 700 Hz so the drone's overtones come through (the ear rebuilds the low pitch from them), a body of noise 90-450 Hz dying in 0.2 s for the blow, a soft clip (`BlastDrive` 3) over the whole; nothing over 2 kHz, still no crack. Over 150 Hz in its first half second it is now about as loud as the hunter's blast (0.14 of full scale against 0.15; it was 0.03).
- **Tests** (`Tests.Game.Wrecks`, 7): `TestSpinsFasterUpToItsMost` replaced by `TestSpinsNoSlowerUpToItsMost` (all five of the arena: never slower, never past 24); new `TestNearerKickedHarder`, `TestKickDiesDown` (slower after 30 ticks than at the kick), `TestNoTwoSpinAlike`. The flight and the road tests as they were, given the reach.
- After the live run: better; a little strange still, and left so - it is a game.

## Stage 10d as built (3.0.84)
The shuttle's medkits on the thirds, in a unit of their own, `Game.Capsules` (`TShuttleCapsules` the rules, `TCapsulePainter` the drawing); the Ram tells the thirds; the game picks the mark and puts the medkit into the field.

- **The thirds** (`TRam.TickThirds`, cue `Third`). As either head's lives pass into their next third - the thirds its fire goes by (`Tier`) - the cue says so, once a third and one a tick: two a head, four over the hunter and the sweeper. The last life is a blast, not a third. The count (`FThirds`) starts again with each head (`Arm`, `LockSweeper`), so a death, which restarts the fight, brings them all back.
- **Where** (`TMoonGame.CallTheShuttle`). The hunter's onto the middle of the deck of the pad, of those the `ram` action names in `medkitPads`, that stands farther from the hero (`TryPickMedkitPad`) - on level 2 `s13-pad-2` and `s13-pad-3`, the high pads at the sides, in the gun's sights. The sweeper's onto the road, `MedkitWallGap` 16 units from the wall away from the Ram's middle (`MedkitWallMark`), under its closed ceiling. No pad named, or none standing - the road.
- **The capsule** (`TShuttleCapsules`). Out of sight over the top of the screen, it falls straight onto its mark in `FallTicks` 33 - a second from any height - on an ease-out: fast at first, braking at the end; the jet lights from 0.4 of the way. Landed (cue `Landed`: the game plays `padclack.wav`), its shell parts over `OpenTicks` 27; at `MedkitOutTicks` 5 the cue's `Opened` gives the mark and the game stands a medkit on it (`SpawnStanding` of `medkit`, its feet on the mark) - a pickup like any other, taken as any other. Then the shell is gone.
- **The look** (`TCapsulePainter`, drawn in code, with the pickups on the monsters' shake). A hull of two discs (`DiscShade`: dark steel under a lighter one), a green cross on its side, a green beacon on top blinking every 8 ticks, under it the jet - an orange glow with a white core, growing as it brakes. Opening: two halves slide out 9 units and lean 35 degrees as they fade, a green breath where the medkit comes out.
- **A death takes them away**: `FCapsules.Clear` wherever the wrecks are cleared; the medkits already down go with the field, which a restart makes anew.
- **The data**: the `ram` action takes `"medkitPads"`, a list of pad tags (`TRamOrders.MedkitPads`; anything else - `SEventBadRamPads`).
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.Capsules` (7: onto its mark and never past it, down within two seconds; braking at the end; lands once and opens once; the medkit on the mark after the landing; the shell gone after; two land each on its own; a clear takes them away). `TRamHunterTests` and `TRamSweeperTests`: `TestEachThirdCallsTheShuttle` (one call a third, none for the last life). `TRamLevelTests`: `TestMedkitPadsAreRead`, two refusals more in `TestBadRamRaises`, and the pads the arena of level 2 names stand on it.
- First guesses for the live run: the fall's length, the jet, the opening of the shell, the clack, the gap at the wall.

## Stage 15a as built (3.0.85)
The charge of the form by the first two heads (decided 2026-10-30: it must grow little by little with the fight, tied to the damage and never read as tied to it), in a unit of its own, `Game.StoneTank` (`TStoneTank`); the gauge takes it straight, with no orbs (`THeroOrbs.Charge`).

- **The tank.** Every hit on a head (`BulletStruckRamEye`; `BulletStruckRamBarrels`, a plate's catch not counted) puts its share of the head's lives into a line of `LateTicks` 50 slots (`Wound`); a wound reaches the tank only when its slot comes round - about 1.5 s after the hit.
- **The pulses.** The gauge drinks from the tank on a clock of its own: every 8 to 30 ticks (`PulseGapLeast`, `PulseGapSpan`, rolled anew each time) a percent, now and then two (`DoublePulseShare` 0.3, when the tank holds them). No even beat follows a burst, and every rise flashes the gauge, as a rise always does.
- **The share.** A head's whole lives pour in `HeadShare` 20% - a little short of its quarter; a trickle of about 0.1% a second (`TricklePerTick`) runs from the first lock, so the gauge lives between the bursts.
- **The ceilings** (`Ceilings`): 30% while the hunter lives, 55% after it. What stands at the ceiling is spilt, not kept: it must not flood the gauge the moment the next head lifts it.
- **A head's death** (`HeadDown` on the cue `Blast`): what is left from the gauge up to that head's ceiling is poured late, as any wound, in place of all that was still on its way - nothing of one head spills over into the next head's quarter - and the next ceiling stands from then on. The gauge goes on growing a few seconds after the blast. The sweeper's death pours up to 55%: the bunker's 95% and 100% are stage 15b.
- **The start and the end.** The tank starts as the first head locks (cue `Locked`), and a death clears it with the Ram, the capsules and the wrecks; the gauge itself goes back to the charge the screen began with (`THeroOrbs.Rewind`), as ever.
- **On a model** (100 hits on the hunter over 40 s, 140 on the sweeper over the next 60): the gauge goes 5, 10, 15, 22, 28 - 31 after the hunter's death - then about 4% every ten seconds to 53, and 55 after the sweeper's.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.StoneTank` (9: nothing before the fight; a wound reaches the gauge late; uneven pulses of one or two; never past the hunter's ceiling; a head's death pours up to its ceiling, late; nothing spills into the next quarter; the second ceiling; the trickle with no hit; a clear stops it). `Tests.Game.HeroOrbs`: the direct charge rises at once, stops at full counting the orbs on their way, and gives a corpse nothing.
- First guesses for the live run: the share of the lives, the lag, the gaps of the pulses, the trickle.

## Stage 11a as built (3.0.86)
The road of the chase, in a unit of its own, `Game.Belt` (`TBelt`): only the road's motion; the Ram tells when to start, the game carries the world and slides the road.

- **The start** (`TRam.TickSpent`, cue `Chase` with `Rumble`). After the sweeper's blast the empty mast sinks; the spent machine then stands at the right wall for `ChangeRiseTicks` 30 + `RowBootTicks` 30 - the time the bunker will take to rise and light its row - and `ChaseBreathTicks` 25 (0.75 s); then it drives off, once. About 3.5 s from the blast. With the bunker (stage 14) the breath counts from its row lit, and the bunker's death stops the belt; until then the belt runs until a death.
- **The belt** (`TBelt`). `Start` to `BeltCruise` 1 unit a tick (the road's 33 a second of `chase-backdrop.md`), `Stop` to a standstill, each over `BeltRampTicks` 66 (2 s) by a smooth step from the speed it finds: no jerk, a stop in the middle of the climb included. `Speed` - what the road went left this tick; `Travel` and `TravelAt(alpha)` - how far it has run.
- **The road.** The 16 tiles of screen 13's road carry `"belt": true` (`TLevelObject.Belt`); `Render.Objects` draws them left by the travel, in fractions of a unit, wrapped round the screen's width (`BeltX`), a tile over the right edge once more on the left. Standing, the travel is 0 - the screen as it was.
- **The world on it** (`TMoonGame.TickBelt`, before `TickRam`, standing with the world in the burst's inhale). The hero rides at `-Speed` on the floor and in the air (`THero.RideBelt`: `CarryX`, the wall asked at every step; on a deck, the deck carries him); an idle hero drifts to the left wall, where the screen's edge holds him. The live pickups ride too (`TMonsterField.CarryLoot`, `TMonster.RideBelt`: the road has no walls) and are gone once wholly past the left edge. Bullets, wrecks and the blast's smoke do not ride.
- **The machine.** It stays at the wall; its wheels turn by the belt (`TRamPainter.Tick(beltShift)`), the exhausts work as in a drive, and the smoke of the exhausts and of the empty mast drifts off with the road (`TSmoke.Drift`).
- **A death** clears the belt with the capsules and the tank: the road stands at its place again.
- **Debug key `I`** - a point of health (`CureHero`, up to 10), to have something to spend tuning the long fight.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.Belt` (`TBeltTests` 9: stands before the start; cruise at the end of its ramp; down to a standstill; no jerk up or down; a stop in the climb smooth; a second start changes nothing; the travel the sum of the speeds; between ticks between them; a clear; `TBeltRoadTests` 4: `BeltX`). `TRamSweeperTests.TestDrivesOffIntoTheChaseOnce` (once, with the rumble, at the wall, mast down, 3-4 s after the blast); no chase after the hunter.
- First guesses for the live run: the cruise, the ramp, the breath before the drive-off, the smoke on the belt.

## Stage 13 as built (3.0.87)
The pads of the chase, in a unit of their own, `Pads.Drift` (`TPadDrift`, the dispatcher): pads of the screen's pool sent in from beyond the right edge as the road runs; `Pads.World` carries them and lets them go.

**Over "Decided 2026-10-31, the drifting pads"**: no chunk of three on a beat of 180 units. Ladders on a beat read as a metronome in the live run: too many pads, nothing to look for, and the city behind covered. So a random structure - climbs of random shape among lone pads, with a guard against a long wait - retuned denser after its own run.

- **The pool** (data). Screen 13 of `level2.json` holds 12 pads with `"drift": true` - 8 of 32 units (`s13-drift-step-01`..`08`, `city-pad`) and 4 of 64 (`s13-drift-high-01`..`04`, `city-pad-long`), bullets pass, a bob of 1. A pad that drifts takes no x or y (`SPadDriftPlaced`) and does nothing else - no path, plunge, shelf, group, carousel, stray or scene's sleep (`CheckPadDrift`, `SLevelPadDriftBusy`); a pool of a screen under `MostAdrift` pads or a pad of it neither 32 nor 64 wide raises at load.
- **The pad adrift** (`Pads.World`). Asleep in the pool until `SetAdrift(x, deck)` sends it - awake, there at once. Every tick on the hero's screen it goes left by the road's way, and its riders with it; wholly past the left edge it is let go (`LetGoPastLeft`), asleep, back in the pool. A ram only rocks it.
- **The groups.** Along the road, an empty stretch of `GroupGapLeast` 48 - `GroupGapMost` 112 after each. A group is a lone pad - any width, any deck from 120 to 288: a step off the road or a pad nobody can reach - or a climb: the foot at deck 272-288 (the plain jump from the road, 352), then a pad a step of 48-80 higher each, 16-40 across edge to edge, until one stands at 144 or higher, where a shot down reaches the hatch. A climb rises toward the machine or away from it (`AwayShare` 0.5); a climb away is the same climb read from the top. A long pad is 0.3 of the dice (`LongShare`), a climb 0.6 of the groups (`ClimbShare`). A lone pad looks like the foot of a climb until the next pad comes in.
- **The guard.** No climb ended for `GuardUnits` 198 of road (6 s at cruise): the next group is a climb - nothing to tell it by. The chase opens with one.
- **Room on the screen.** A group comes only when all of it fits under `MostAdrift` 10 pads at once, and comes in at the right edge; a climb is never cut. A width the pool has run out of is made up with the other.
- **The game** (`TMoonGame`). The road ticks at the head of the tick, before the pads (`BeltWay`; 0 in the burst's inhale - the road stands with the world), so the pads of the chase ride it and carry what stands on them; `FDrift.Tick(screen, FBelt.Travel)` while the belt runs; `FDrift.Clear` beside every `FBelt.Clear` - a death, a door, a new level.
- **The hero on them.** A deck a wall pulls from under him leaves a walking hero too, not only a standing one (`RideDeck` -> `LoseDeck`). `CarryX` never takes him left of `LeftDoorStop` 2, the line the 2008 left door puts him back on after the tick: the deck is judged under the feet he keeps - otherwise the pad he stood on rode off the screen at the left edge unseen, and he hung in the air (seen in the live run).
- **Tests**: `Tests.Pads.Drift` (`TPadDriftTests` 16: nothing before the road runs; the chase opens with a climb beyond the right edge; every group a lone pad or a passable climb; a climb never far behind the last; never more pads than the screen takes; no two across each other; the dice give every number; the pads ride the road; a still road, still pads; a pad adrift carries its rider; past the left edge let go and sent again; a clear puts the pool to sleep; a short pool raises; a pad of another width raises; a pad that drifts takes no place; and does nothing else).
- First guesses for the live run: the shares, the gaps, the guard.

## Stage 13b as built (3.0.87)
The medkits of the chase's climbs, in the same version: 3.0.87 was not yet closed by a tag.

- **Which climb** (`Pads.Drift`). One climb in `MedkitClimbs` 3 carries a medkit; which of the three, the dice say as each round of three begins. Every climb counts - the one the chase opens with and those the guard brings too; `Clear` starts the round anew. One in three is a first guess for the live run.
- **Which pad.** The middle pad by height: of the pads between the foot and the top - a climb has three or four - the one whose deck is nearest halfway between the foot's and the top's; of two as near, the lower (`PutMedkitMidway`). The same pad whichever way the climb rises.
- **The mark** (`TDriftCue`). `Tick` returns where the medkits stand from this tick: the middle of the deck of a pad just sent, beyond the right edge, on the screen. The dispatcher knows no monsters; a pad the pool cannot give is no deck, and no medkit.
- **The medkit** (`TMoonGame.StandMedkitOn`). `SpawnStanding` of `medkit`, its feet on the mark - a pickup like any other, taken as any other; the shuttle's capsules stand theirs through it too. It rides the pad as any body on a deck (`RideDeck`).
- **Gone past the left edge** (`TMonster.RideBelt`, a fix of 11a). `CarryX` stops a body at the screen's edge, so a medkit on a pad hung there while the pad rode out, and dropped onto the road as the pad went from under it. `RideBelt` now gives a body on a deck the rest of the deck's way (`MotionX` less what `RideDeck` carried, `FCarriedX`); the medkit rides its pad out and is gone once wholly past the edge, as one on the road. It runs after the field's tick of the same tick.
- **A death** takes them away with the field, which a restart makes anew; the round starts anew with `FDrift.Clear`.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Pads.Drift` - `TPadDriftTests` 5 more (a medkit only on the middle pad of a climb; one climb in three carries one; the dice pick the climb of the three; the medkit stands on the pad sent with it; a clear starts the three anew), `TDriftLootTests` 2 (a medkit rides its pad out past the left edge and is gone; one on the road rides out and is gone).

## Stage 11b-1 as built (3.0.88)
The far plane of the chase (`chase-backdrop.md`), in three units of its own: the data (`Levels.FarPlane`), the motion (`Game.FarPlane`) and the drawing (`Render.FarPlane`). The numbers: `bin/chase-backdrop.json`, moved from `tools/chase/layout.json` - one source for the stand and the game.

- **The data.** Level 2 names the screen and the layout in a section of its own (`"farPlane": screen 13, layout chase-backdrop.json`); the pictures are the set named as the layout, `chase-backdrop`, which joined the level's `objectSets` (a set the level does not list, or a screen past the level's, raises at load). Layers back to front: the ridge, the city (the crater floor, the domes, the monorail, the outskirts), the tunnel (screen 13's own backdrop as an element, `existing`, and the cliff over it) and the near ground. A band may start at an x of its layer and fade in there (`from`, `featherLeft` - unused now), fade its top in (`fadeTop`) and keep less of its colour in the tunnel (`shadeInTunnel`); an element may fade its left end in (`featherLeft`), be shaded (`shade`), tint the sun (`tintsSun`) and be drawn mirrored (`mirror`).
- **The motion** (`TFarPlane`), by the stand's rules tick for tick: the tunnel at its peak and the city at a share of it until the tunnel's last element is 16 past the left edge, then both ease to cruise over 3 s; the city brakes within the last screen of its strip, to a floor, never past its end; the ridge at a share of the city, the ground at its own speed. Every speed times the belt's share - the road's ramp starts the far plane, its stop stands it. Ticks with the belt (and stands with it in the burst's inhale); a death, a door or a new level puts it back at the start of the chase.
- **The drawing** (`TFarPlanePainter`), in place of the screen's backdrop on screen 13: the black sky, the sun (a disc and two glows added, fitted to the stand's gradient, tinted while a dome stands before it), the layers - every picture a strip of triangles whose corners carry the fades and the shade, so the shared cache's colour mods are not touched. While screen 13's backdrop covers the whole screen nothing behind the tunnel's layer is drawn: the arena before the chase costs the cliff's end and the near hills.
- **The cliff over the wall** is drawn by code from the arena's first frame (its left 64 units fading into the wall), not baked into `screen13`: the same pixels, nothing to regenerate.
- **The far ledge rides with the wall.** The sky layer of screen 13 - the lip, the pillars, the convoys, the searchlight - is drawn from minus the tunnel's offset, in fractions of a unit (`TDynamicScreenRenderer.Draw` takes a `TSdlFPoint`), so it rides with the wall. Drawn after the cliff, as over a backdrop the cliff were baked into.
- **No haze on screen 13** (`HazeBackdrop`): the hazes bend nothing there.
- **The numbers after the live runs** ("Decided 2026-10-31, the far plane"): the wall 6 a second, the city 3 (0.5 of the wall while the cliff is in sight), the ridge 0.35 of the city, the ground 14 and 30% of its colour in the tunnel; the city's six elements mirrored; the domes at a shade of 0.7.
- **The stand** (`tools/chase`) plays the same: the ground's layer in front of the cliff, its shade in the tunnel, the mirror; the layout's path in the three tools.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.FarPlane` (23, over a layout of their own - the stand's first tuning, so tuning the shipped one breaks none): the ride against the stand's timeline, cruise, the strip never ending in sight, the ridge and the ground, a stopped belt, between ticks, a clear; the sun's tint; a band's shade; level 2 reading its far plane and the layouts that raise.

## Stage 11b-2 as built (3.0.89)
The fangs of the chase ("Decided 2026-10-31, the fangs"), in a unit of their own, `Game.Fangs`: the rules (`TFangRow`) and the drawing (`TFangPainter`), as the wrecks and the capsules.

- **The data** (`Levels.FarPlane`): the section `fangs` of the layout - the kit, each picture with its size at the scale of 1 and whether it may stand tall; `scale`, `gap`, `lowTop`, `tallTop` as ranges, the least first; `tallShare`, `tintInTunnel`, `tint`, `tintJitter`. The speed is the `fangs` of the speeds, a motion of its own (`fmFangs`), not a layer. A layout without the section has no fangs; an empty kit, a picture without a size, a range the wrong way or a scale of zero raise.
- **The motion** (`TFarPlane`): the fangs' layer runs at its speed times the belt's share, as the near ground - no ease, no brake.
- **The row** (`TFangRow`): one after another along the layer, each sent at the right edge and let go past the left one; every number the dice's (`RollDiceSeed` on every clear) - which picture, the scale, the top, mirrored or not, a little off the tint, the road to the next. A tall one only of a kind that may be and a screen of road after the last tall one. A fang never floats: too short to reach under the bottom edge from its top, it is made bigger as far as the scale lets it, then sunk.
- **The drawing** (`TFangPainter`): after the far ledge (`dlSky`), before the shuttle (`dlAloft`) and the play layer; it stands while the world shakes, as the far plane. The kit is the level's own objects (`level2-objects:s11-fang-*`), whose tint screen 11 sets on every draw, so the painter sets it on every draw too, and mirrors by the flip.
- **The numbers**: 20 a second; scale 0.5-0.8; 60-240 units of road between two, a fang about every 11 s, two or three on the screen; low tops 250-310, tall 140-200, one in four; 35/33/37 in the tunnel to 55/52/56 outside, 4 either way. At the scale of 0.8 the tall kinds reach a top of about 186-200: the tall ones come out near the low end of their corridor - more scale lets them rise.
- **The clear**: beside the belt's and the far plane's, wherever they are cleared.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.Fangs` (24, over a kit and numbers of their own): the first at the right edge, none shown while the road stands, each in at the right edge and out past the left, the road between two, tall ones of the right kinds and never two on the screen, the corridors of the tops, no fang floating, the scale, the same dice the same row, a clear, no fangs without the section, the layer's speed; the tint in the tunnel, outside and off by the jitter; level 2 rides fangs and the layouts that raise.

## The city's frames as built (3.0.90)
The frames and the depth of the city ("Decided 2026-11-01, the city"), in the game as on the stand - the stand (`tools/chase/stand.template.html`) is the reference, number for number.

- **The data** (`Levels.FarPlane`): the layout's `camera` (the horizon, the base line, the focal length) and `frames` (each `x`, `w`, `h`, `frequency`, `rib`, `tintsSun`; a frequency of 3 and a rib of 2 when absent, as the stand has them); an element's `groundY`, where its depth is read when it hangs in the air (`DepthY`). A camera whose base line is not below its horizon or has no focal length, a frame without a size, a frequency under 1 or a rib of 0, and frames without a camera raise. A layout without a camera keeps the city flat.
- **The depth** (`TFarPlaneDef.ElementLeft`): a building of the city comes to the middle of the screen when its layer has ridden its centre there, and drifts from it k times as fast, k = (feet - horizon) / (base line - horizon), 0.05 at least - the stand's `cityLeft`. Its width is the layout's already; only its place moves. The other layers ride flat.
- **The sun** turns under a frame marked `tintsSun` (`SunCover`) as it did under the elements; the elements keep the key. The colour of the turn is `TFarPlaneSun.ColorAt`, the sun's and the glare's.
- **The geometry** (`TDomeFrames`, `Render.DomeFrames`, no SDL): an icosahedron cut `frequency` times along each side, a vertex at the top, cut at the equator, the equator added as the ring (96 segments, 1.6 times a rib) - built once a frequency, frames of one frequency sharing it. Seen through the camera for the city's ride: the ribs split by the depth of their middle into the far half and the near half, each far to near; each with the side of its rim toward the sun and its light, 0.12 to 1 by the sun over 150 units times 0.35 to 1 as it faces it. A frame off the screen gives nothing.
- **The drawing** (`TDomeFramePainter`): strips of triangles with no texture (`SDL_RenderGeometry`), the colour and the alpha in the vertices. A rib dark (26, 23, 21) with round ends, its rim a strip of its own, 0.3 of the rib wide and 0.35 of it toward the sun; a shadow black at 0.45, 1.4 times its rib, cut at the foot of the floor band. Every edge fades out over one pixel of the window (`SDL_RenderGetScale`, new in `Sdl2.Core`) - the smoothing a canvas gives a line. The glare is two point glows fitted to the stand's gradient. No shader. A strip with no texture takes the renderer's draw blend, which the sky leaves at none: the painter sets it.
- **The order** in the city's layer (`TFarPlanePainter.DrawLayer`): the floor band, the shadows, the far half, the buildings, the near half, the glare; then the tunnel and the near ground as before.
- **The shadows** show little, as on the stand: `chase-backdrop.md`, "The shadows as they are".
- **Tests**: `Tests.Game.FarPlane` 13 more - the sun under a frame; the depth (on the base line with its layer, halfway at half the speed, the lower the faster, on the horizon still riding, every centre to the middle, a hanging thing by what it stands on, flat without a camera, the other layers flat); level 2 reading its camera and frames, and the camera, frame and frames that raise. `Tests.Render.DomeFrames` 15, over a far plane of their own: none without frames; a frame on the base line, its top over its centre, as far left as right, riding with the city; the halves, far to near, the near ribs thicker; a finer frame more ribs; none off the screen; a low frame's shadows on the floor, none of a tall frame, none under a sun below the horizon; the rim toward the sun and brightest by it.

## The long shadows as built (3.0.91)
The frames' shadows cut at the eye rather than dropped whole, drawn as soft strokes and laid on over the near hills (`chase-backdrop.md`, "The shadows") - the stand first, the game number for number.

- **The cut** (`TDomeFrames.CutAtEye`, the stand's `cutAtEye`): both ends of a rib dropped onto the floor; the part in front of the plane at `NearestShare` of the focal length is kept, as the shares of the way along the rib, cut `EyeCutMargin` (0.001 unit) in front of it - cut on it, the arithmetic could put the end behind it and `Project` drop it. Both ends past it - no shadow.
- **The stroke** (`AddShadow`, the stand's `shadowStroke`): `ShadowSamples` (16) points between the shares, each a point of the rib dropped onto the floor. The light runs `Y / ToSun.Y` from there to the floor; the penumbra spreads by the sun's disc over the focal length (`FSunSpread`) times that run. The dark is 1.4 times the rib wide (`ShadowWidening`): the plateau's half width is the nearness times half of it less the spread, the whole's the nearness times half of it and the spread, the peak the dark over the larger of the dark and twice the spread (`TShadowSample`).
- **The drawing** (`TDomeFramePainter.DrawShadows`, `AddStroke`; the stand's `renderShadows` in WebGL, since a canvas cannot shade a triangle by its corners): four lines across every sample - clear, the peak, the peak, clear - joined sample to sample, black at 0.45 times the peak in the vertices, the edge a pixel past the plateau at the least.
- **Twice, clipped** (`SDL_RenderSetClipRect`; `TFarPlanePainter.DrawFrameShadows`): on the city's band, under the buildings; on the layer of motion `fmGround` after its band, from its top to the bottom of the screen. The frames' view is taken once a frame (`FFrameView`).
- **The stand**: the switch "shadows over the near hills", on by default; the game always draws them.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Render.DomeFrames` 18 - the shadows of a low and of a tall frame between the horizon and the eye (in place of the low frame's on the floor and the tall frame's none: the design changed, not a bug), a shadow running to the eye, soft at its edges, fully dark at its rib's foot, fading toward the eye.

## The city's life as built (3.0.92)
The lights of the city, the TEK emblem, its paint and the frames' beacons (`chase-backdrop.md`, "The lights" to "The beacons"), in the game tick for tick as on the stand - the stand is the reference, key for key.

- **The data** (`Levels.FarPlane`): `cityLights` (the looks of window, neon and strip, the signs' `flicker`, the `windows`, the lights of each piece - a kind, a box in shares of its picture, a colour), `emblem`, `beacons`, a frame's `beaconsAbove`, a placed building's `paint`. The shares the dice are compared with are Double. Raise: a light of an unknown kind, a box not of four numbers or below zero, a range of whole numbers out of order or under its least (a span of no ticks would never end), a paint without width, an emblem without parts, a halo to each, a width and an aspect; lamps or frame beacons without `beacons`; an emblem without `cityLights`.
- **The clock** (`Levels.CityLife`, no SDL): the stand's functions of the same names, the dice lowbias32 to the bit (`Mix`; `MulLow32` multiplies in halves, so the checked builds never overflow). Two traps of the 32-bit compiler, closed: its FPU counts in extended precision, so a product is kept in a Double before `Floor` (`RollInt`, `NodeKey`) - a hair under a whole number in extended is that number in the stand's doubles; and `Round` goes to the even where the stand's `Math.round` goes half up (`NodeKey` floors half up). The tick is `TFarPlane.Clock` - the stand's tick, counted from the start of its run, whose road first moves on its second tick (the first moving tick of the game is the clock's 2); it stands with the far plane in the burst's inhale, which the stand has not.
- **The beacons** (`TDomeFrames`): every end of an edge above the equator once, above `beaconsAbove` of the frame's height - 16 on the main dome, 6 on the satellite - seeded by the frame's place in the list and the node's x and z to a thousandth, not by the order of the nodes; projected with their frame, the far ones (behind its centre by more than the noise of the arithmetic: the top, at 0, is near as on the stand) drawn after the far half, the near ones after the near half.
- **The drawing** (`TFarPlanePainter.DrawLife`, after each building of the city that stood on the screen, seeded by its place in the city's layer): the paint (`tek-paint`, the building's shade, its `alpha`; a mirror moves it, never turns it), the lights (a lamp blinks as a beacon in its own colour; a sign a point glow, a strip and a window a beam glow - `gsBeam` 128 - a halo grown by `haloPad` at the building's nearness, then a core), the emblem on its building's tip (the metal at its shade, then each part's glow and tube added in its colour, `tek-N-halo` and `tek-N`, set to add and filtered). A beacon: a halo at half its level, a core.
- **The set**: `tek-emblem`, `tek-1..6`, `tek-1..6-halo` and `tek-paint`, put into `chase-backdrop.mset` by `tools/chase/make_emblem.py`. `pack_set.py` writes the set anew from its folder and drops them: after a repack, make_emblem runs again, or the level does not load.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Levels.CityLife` 18 - the hash and the seeds against the stand's numbers, and a sign, a window, the emblem's frame and small triangle and a beacon tick by tick against tables the stand's own functions made under node, over numbers of the tests' own (every tick a level changes on, the level there, the sum over the run); `Tests.Game.FarPlane` 14 - the clock, level 2's city life read, the refusals; `Tests.Render.DomeFrames` 4 - none without a share, the stand's nodes (its seeds), far and near, riding with the city.

## The train as built (3.0.93)
The train on the monorail (`chase-backdrop.md`, "The train"): one train shuttling between the two stations, on the stand first, then in the game tick for tick - the stand is the reference, key for key.

- **Decided**: one train, there is one beam and no loop; at a stop the nose goes in near the box's far wall and a car of three stands out on the beam, so the train is seen standing at the platform (of three ways - that, the train cut to two cars to vanish into the station, a stop outside the mouth - the first).
- **The data** (`Levels.FarPlane`): `train` (`TCityTrain`: the piece, the station's piece, `mouth`, `w`, `y`, `shade`, `inside`, `cruise`, `accelTicks`, `dwellTicks`); `CityElements` finds the stations. Raise: no width, no cruise, a push of less than a tick, a dwell out of order or under a tick, not two stations in the city; and at the painter's making (`MonorailLine`), stations at two depths or stops with no run between them.
- **The motion** (`Levels.Monorail`, no SDL): the stand's `monorailLine`, `trainRun`, `runPlace`, `trainLeft`, `trainShown` under Pascal names. The line is the train's depth with the city at rest, so the mouths keep their places as the city rides; a run pushes up and down over `accelTicks` and rolls between; the dwells are dice of `Levels.CityLife` (seed `$7A1`, the first dwell before the clock at key 1, the legs' at 100 on).
- **The time** (`TFarPlane.ClockAt`): the clock between ticks as the offsets lie - the tick before the last one plus the frame's share, 0 before the road; the stand's `Now * 33` unrounded. The lights keep their whole tick.
- **The drawing** (`TFarPlanePainter.DrawTrain`): in the city's layer before the first element in front of the line, its picture's quad and source cut between the mouths, then its lights (the piece's `cityLights`, seeded one past the city's elements) cut to the same span (`DrawLight` takes a `TScreenSpan` now, `GlowWithin` cuts a beam and keeps a point whole while its centre is in).
- **The stand**: cruise, push ticks, inside and feet among the looks, written back by the download; the run's seconds in the corner. The first numbers: cruise 16, a push of 132 ticks, dwells of 300 to 600 - a run of about 30 s.
- **Tests**: `Tests.Levels.Monorail` 19 - the run, the ticks the train leaves and reaches its stops, where it stands and the sum against tables the stand's own functions made under node, over a train and stops of the tests' own; a short run, either end, never past cruise, within the stops, the dwells, between ticks; the line's stops, mouths and depth, the order of the stations, the refusals, the cut. `Tests.Game.FarPlane` 5 - the clock between ticks, level 2's train, the refusals.

## Stage 14a as built (3.0.94)
The bunker and phase 1, with the bunker's half of the charge (15b), in one version: the third head of `Game.Ram`, its shells in a unit of their own, `Game.Shells` (`TBunkerShells` the rules, `TShellPainter` the drawing), and the game carrying out both.

- **The rise.** After the sweeper's blast the empty mast sinks and the bunker (`rhBunker`) rises in its place over `ChangeRiseTicks` 30, low on the mast (`BunkerMast` 12: its top about y 204 with the Ram at the right wall, under the chase's high pads), to the whine. `LockBunker`: `BunkerLives` 150 times the grade's share, the row from 0, the hatch shut. The breath before the chase counts from the row lit: a rest of `RowBootTicks` 30 + `ChaseBreathTicks` 25, then the cue `Chase` (`TickChaseStart`, in place of `TickSpent`). From the sweeper's blast on the machine stands at the right wall for good (`AtTheWall`, in place of `Spent`): no press, no legs.
- **The turn.** It turns to a hero on its other side as the sweeper does (`TickBunkerTurn`: `SweeperTurnPast` 8 for `TurnHoldTicks` 10), only while its hatch is shut. Its art faces right; the game draws it mirrored to face left, the head's points kept in the others' left-facing frame (`HeadShapes`: 28.2 by 34.3, socket 17.9, 34.1).
- **The breath** (`TBunkerHatch`, `TickHatch`): shut and aiming, `HatchAimTicks` 50 (the first after the row lit, `FirstBurstTicks` 33), while a hero lives and the row is up; the lob (cue `Lob`: out of the next of its two muzzles at the hero's feet - the middle of his body's width, his feet's line - with a small jolt); `HatchFiredTicks` 10; the column (cue `Vent`, the brake's hiss), `HatchVentTicks` 16; open and cooling, `HatchWindowTicks` 40; shut again. The lid (`LidAt`) is thrown open in 3 ticks and shuts in 6; drawn turned about its hinge (`LidHinge` 9.6, 11.6 on the art) by 52 degrees and moved 5.5, -1.5 as it shuts (`TRamPainter.DrawLid`).
- **The column** (`ColumnHolds`): 6 units either side of the hatch and 80 up, while it vents; the game wounds the hero in it (`ResolveRamColumn`). Drawn as a smoke of fire thrown straight up (`ColumnLook`) and four glows standing in it.
- **The hit** (`StrikeBunker`, "Decided 2026-11-02, the bunker's top"). A bullet's stretch that comes down across the top of the dome (`HatchX` 17.4, `HatchY` 12, `HatchHalfWidth` 8.5, measured on the art) at `HatchFallLeast` 15 degrees or steeper takes a life, the lid shut or open; shallower, the honeycomb flares there. Under that line the honeycomb (`FHeadShield`, an ellipse of 15 by 18 about the dome's middle) takes the bullet: only the part of the stretch under the line is asked, so a bullet over the top flies on. The shield is open on top in the picture too (`TTekShield.OpenAbove`). `HatchPoint` - the middle of the top. `TRamPainter.DrawTop`: a pale band 22 by 7 on the top, breathing 0.35-0.75 over 40 ticks, white while the row comes up and when struck; drawn once a frame, after the lens.
- **The thirds** call the shuttle as the others' do: the bunker's medkits go onto the road `MedkitAhead` 64 ahead of the hero, short of the Ram (`MedkitAheadMark`). The capsules ride the belt now (`TShuttleCapsules.Ride`, before their tick: the medkit stands where its capsule has been carried). All three thirds play phase 1 in this stage.
- **The death.** Its last life blows it up as the hunter's does - no overload, no wave; the empty mast sinks and stays down, the machine stands at the wall, the road runs on.
- **The shells** (`Game.Shells`). `Lob(from, target)`: of the arcs that come down on the target in 18 to 60 ticks, the one whose top stands nearest `LobRise` 40 over the higher end; `ShellGravity` 0.25; the middle aimed a radius (`ShellRadius` 3.5) over the target. The arc is aimed in the world: every tick the road's way is added to the shell as to the decks, so a hero who keeps standing gets it and one who runs leaves it behind. The ring (`Predict`, every tick): the first deck under what is left of the arc, else the road - a pad knocked off from under it sends the ring on down. A shell that comes down onto a deck bursts (cue `Bursts`, which deck); one rising through a deck does not. One that misses every deck lands on the road (`Landed`) and rolls left `RollSpeed` 1.6 a tick faster than the road; rolling, it is spent on a body it touches (`Strikes`); past the left edge it is gone. `ShellSlots` 8.
- **The game** (`Game.Moon`). `TickShells(way)` after `KnockOffPads` and before the hero's tick (not in the burst's inhale): the decks are the pads standing on the screen; a burst dozes its pad and launches a wreck from the burst (`ShellBurstReach` 48), a barrel's explosion, `bottle.wav`, a jolt of 0.15 - whoever stood on it falls this tick with no wound (decided: the ring warned; the price is the height lost). `ResolveShellContact` - a rolling shell spent on the hero: the explosion and a wound. `BulletStruckRamBunker` before the hull's shield. The lob plays `granade.wav`. The rings are drawn after the pads in front, the shells with the monsters' bullets. `FShells.Clear` beside every clear of the capsules.
- **The wrecks** (`TPadWrecks.Launch`): a new wreck takes the place of one gone, so a long chase does not pile them up; each has its `Turn`, and the painter starts a slot's fire, sparks and bolts anew when the turn changes.
- **The charge (15b)** (`Game.StoneTank`): `Ceilings` 30, 55, 95, 100; the share of a head's whole lives `HeadShares` 20, 20, 36 - the bunker's a little short of the way from 55 to 95; the last head's death pours up to full, not to its own ceiling.
- **Debug key `O`**: a third of the lives of the head on the mast at once (`TRam.WoundThird`), and a third of its share into the tank - three heads stand between the start and the chase.
- **Tests**: the new `TRamBunkerTests` 12 (its lives by the grade; nothing lobbed while the row comes up; the chase a breath after the row lit, once; the breath - the shell, a beat, the column about half a second, the window about a second, shut again; the shell at the hero's feet; hit from above shut or open; a shot down from a pad at 25, 40 and 60 degrees goes in, stretch by stretch; shallow or from the side the honeycomb; the column burns only over the hatch and only while it blows; the turn; the thirds call the shuttle; its last life blows it up with no wave and it stands); `TRamSweeperTests`: `TestTheBunkerRisesAtTheWall` in place of the empty mast, and the drive-off with the bunker up. The new `Tests.Game.Shells` 13 (down on its target; the arc climbs and stays on the screen; on a sliding road a standing hero gets it; the ring where it comes down; a burst on the deck it comes down on, none on one it rises through; the ring down to the road once the deck is gone; rolls left faster than the road; spent on a body only rolling; gone past the left edge; as many as its slots; a clear). `Tests.Game.StoneTank` 3 (the bunker's ceiling; its lives bring most of its half; its death fills the gauge), `Tests.Game.Capsules` 1 (rides the road), `Tests.Game.Wrecks` 1 (a new one in a gone one's place). And the tests of the monorail (3.0.93) made to build: a loop's tick cast to `Integer`, the two refusals caught with `try`/`except` in place of `Assert.WillRaise`.
- First guesses for the live run: the lives, the breath's ticks, the arc's rise, the roll, the column, the top band, the medkit ahead.

## Stage 14b as built (3.0.96)
Phase 2 of the bunker, the fall back ("Decided 2026-11-01, the bunker in three phases", "Decided 2026-11-03, phase 2").

- **The phase** (`BunkerSecondPhase`): the bunker up, the chase begun, its lives at two thirds or less - both later thirds until 14c.
- **The fall back** (`TFallBack`, `TickFallBack`, from the rest at the wall): the first `FallBackFirstTicks` 66 into the phase, then every `FallBackEveryLeastTicks` 200 to `FallBackEveryMostTicks` 264 (`NextFallBackWait`); it begins only with the hatch shut, so a breath under way is waited out. The warning: the eye blinks fast for `FallBackWarnTicks` 16 (`TickEye`), the machine still at the wall. The lunge to its ram at `FallBackX` 256 at `LungeSpeed` 3, a stop with `LungeStopJolt` 0.2 (`Arrive`); a stand of `FallBackStandTicks` 33; the drive back at `ReturnSpeed` 1.2; at the wall the next wait. A touch on the way is the contact rule as ever.
- **The silence** (`HoldsFire`): from the warning till the drive back begins no new lob (`TickHatch`); on the way back it aims again. `FallingBack` (the lunge, the stand, the way back) is the napalm's pause for stage 12.
- **The throw over the hull** (`ThrowsOverTheHull`, `ResolveRamThrowOver` before `ResolveRamContact`): falling back, a hero on the road (not in the air, not on a pad) whose middle is right of the stern is thrown to the left, out of the machine's way till he comes down, with a wound (`HurtByRam`, shared with the touch). The throw is `THero.Fling`: the throw's jump (`BeginThrow`, which `Throw` now goes through) at `FlingBoost` 10 - about 150 units up, the hull is 128 - the controls out of his hands for `FlingLockTicks` 30, the whole rise, and a way of its own across, `FlingWay` 1 a tick, on top of his own until he lands. With the road under him he crosses about 330 units: from behind the stern at the middle he comes down well left of the bow.
- **The shells in pairs** (`Lob`): out of both muzzles at once (`BunkerMuzzle`), the first at his feet, the second (cue `SecondLob`) at `TRamTarget.SecondAt`, which the game fills from `SecondShellMark` (`Game.Shells`) over `ShellDecks`: the middle of the pad under his feet (`UnderFeetSlop` 1); else of the nearest pad a plain jump up (`ClimbLeast` 16 to `ClimbMost` 92 over his feet) whose middle has ridden past the middle of the screen; else the road `RoadAhead` 64 ahead of him, never past the right edge. The lob plays once. In `TickShells` two bursts on one pad in one tick knock it off once (`Knocked`): one wreck, both explosions.
- **The shells wound**: `TBunkerShells.Strikes` asks the shells in the air as well as the rolling ones - caught in flight, a shell is spent on him (`ResolveShellContact`: the explosion, a wound). Every burst on a deck (`TickShells`) and on him adds a `TBlast` of `ShellBlast` - the tank's numbers in `monsters.json` (radius 80), no shards - to the game's waves: it wounds whoever it reaches through `StrikeHero`, under the mercy, and shoves the monsters and the medkits near it as any blast does.
- **The death away from the wall**: `KillHead` drops the fall back; after the change of heads `TickChange` drives the machine back to the wall at `ReturnSpeed` if it stands away from it.
- **Tests**: `TRamBunkerTests` 9 more (no fall back in the first phase; the warning about half a second at the wall; to the middle and never nearer the left edge, the stand, the slower way back; the next a few seconds after; silent from the warning till the way back, then firing again; the shells in pairs, the second where the game says; the throw over the hull from the road behind, once while he flies, and out of its way over the hull; none from a pad or the air, none in front of it; dying away from the wall it goes back). The new `TSecondShellMarkTests` 5 in `Tests.Game.Shells` (the pad he stands on, on the right half too; the nearest pad past the middle a jump up; not one still on the right half; not one too high; the road ahead, never past the right edge). `TBunkerShellTests`: `TestInTheAirItIsSpentOnABody` in place of `TestInTheAirItTouchesNobody`. The fling is the hero's and one wreck a pad is the game's, beyond the tests' reach: checked live.
- First guesses for the live run: the gap between falls back, the warning, the lunge's speed and the stand, the way back, the fling's height and way, the middle as the line a pad is struck past, the climb's reach, how far ahead the road shell lands, the blast's radius.

## Stage 14c as built (3.0.97)
Phase 3 of the bunker, the hunt from behind ("Decided 2026-11-01, the bunker in three phases", "Decided 2026-11-03, phase 3").

- **The phases** (`BunkerSecondPhase` now `Tier` 1 only, `BunkerThirdPhase` `Tier` 2): the last third is the hunt (`TBunkerHunt`: `htCalling`, `htPassing`, `htHunting`; `TickHunt` from the rest `AtTheWall`, before `TickFallBack`). `Hunts` - for stage 12: no napalm in it, the mortar folded.
- **The change** (`BeginHunt`): where it stands - at the wall or at the middle, a fall back under way dropped - the cue `Alarm` (the rebuild's hum) and `Rows`; silent (`HoldsFire`) and blinking (`TickEye`) till the game tells it the rows are in (`RowsArrived`), then `HuntPassWarnTicks` 16 more.
- **The rows** (`Pads.Drift`): `BeginRows` - from now on three rows at `RowDecks` 272, 192, 120 (the low one a plain jump from the road, the middle one clear of the bunker's top, the top one the firing height), the gaps `RowGapLeast` 4 to `RowGapMost` 12, narrower than his body (16); no more climbs. The first stretch, from the left edge to `RowsBeyond` 128 past the right one, is sent at once and flies in (`TPad.SetAdriftComing`: from `RowsComeFrom` 560 right of its place over `RowsComeTicks` 120, eased out as a square, so it ends at the road's pace; its place rides the road all the while; the jets hard); a slot within `RowsApart` 32 of a pad already riding is left to it. The cue's `RowsIn` the tick the flight ends; then each row is sent at the right edge as the road runs (`SendRow`); a pool run dry is a hole. The pool of screen 13 grows to 52 (`s13-drift-step-01..38`, `s13-drift-high-01..14`).
- **The pass**: to the left edge at `LungeSpeed` 3, silent; `Arrive` ends it with `LungeStopJolt`. A hero on a pad is not touched (`Contact` is out in the pass and the hunt). One on the road is shoved before the bow (`Shoves` - the bow's depth into his body, at most `ShoveDeepest` 12; the game's `ResolveRamShove`, `THero.Shove`) till he is pinned - the bow nearer the left edge than his body and `PinRoom` 12 - or run into deeper (one who came down inside the hull): then `ThrowsOverTheHull` throws him to the right with a wound. The throw's way of its own is now the Ram's (`TOverTheHull`, `THero.Fling(side, way)`): `HuntFlingWay` 3 a tick against the road's 1 (about 190 units by the time he comes down past the roof), `FallBackFlingWay` 1 for phase 2.
- **The hunt** (`TickHuntDrive`, from the rest): not of the world, the road does not carry it; its hatch toward `HuntTrail` 24 behind his middle (the mast's `WellX` off its left edge), closing `HuntGain` 0.15 of the gap a tick, at most `HuntBehindSpeed` 1.5 (his run along a row against the road gains 1), up and down by `Brake`; the rumble back on. A hero on the road it touches is rammed (`ThrowsOverTheHull`): thrown forward to the right with a wound - back to the left at the right wall, with no room past the stern (`HuntThrowSide`) - and it stands `RamStandTicks` 50 while he flies.
- **The shells** in pairs again (`Lob`): the first at his feet, the second at `AheadShellMark` (`Game.Shells`): the middle of the nearest deck ahead of his feet along his row, else the road ahead. The game picks the mark by `Hunts` (`TickRam`).
- **Its death** ends the hunt (`KillHead`); the machine drives back to the wall (14b), the rows ride on.
- **Tests**: `TRamBunkerTests` 10 more (no hunt in the middle third; the change - the alarm with the rows, called once, no fall back; standing silent and blinking till the rows are in; the pass a warning after, to the left edge, silent, only to the left; a hero on a pad lets it pass; shoved to the left edge and thrown over to the right, farther than the road carries back; the hatch a step behind him, standing and running, no faster than 1.6; a ram on the road forward, once while he flies, and it stands; at the right wall back to the left; the shells in pairs); the two phase-2 throw tests to the new signature. The new `TAheadShellMarkTests` 3 in `Tests.Game.Shells` (the nearest pad ahead along his row; not under him, behind him or on another row; none - the road ahead). `TPadDriftTests` 4 more (the rows fly in from beyond the right edge; in once at their flight's end, covering the screen, at the road's pace; only the rows after, as dense; a riding pad keeps its slot). The shove, the fling and the flying in are the game's and the pads': checked live.
- First guesses for the live run: the rows' decks and gaps, the flight's length and distance, the warning before the pass, the shove's reach and the pin, the fling's way, the hatch's step behind him, its speed and gain, the stand after a ram, the shells in pairs.

## Stage 14c reworked (3.0.98)
Phase 3 again, as "Decided 2026-11-04, phase 3 reworked" says; "Stage 14c as built (3.0.97)" above is the first build, kept for its numbers.

- **The steps** (`TBunkerHunt`): `htCalling` (the change, as in 3.0.97), `htPassing`, `htTurning`, `htEntering`, `htHunting`. `Hunt` is public for the tests. `HoldsFire` from the change till it is in again.
- **The wave** (`Pads.Drift`). `BeginRows` lays the three rows (`RowDecks` `ClimbFootHighest` 272, 192, `WayUpDeck` 144 - the decks of a climb; the gaps 4 to 12, from the left edge to `RowsBeyond` 128 past the right one, flying in over `RowsComeTicks` 120, each row from its own side, `RowsComeFrom`: the foot 560 right of its place, the middle 720 left of it - the whole stretch off the screen - the top 200 above) and keeps its pads (`FWave`); `TPad.SetAdriftComing` takes the offset as a point and eases both ways (`ComeShare`), and `LetGoPastLeft` spares a pad still flying in; `SendRow` and the row cursors are gone. Till the cue `RowsIn` nothing else is sent; then the groups come again, the first `GroupGapLeast`..`GroupGapMost` past the wave's end on the road, the guard counting from it. `AdriftOn` leaves the wave's pads out, so the groups do not wait for it to ride off; a pad of it that is let go drops out of it. From the wave on the groups are denser (`FDense`): `HuntGuardUnits` 132, `HuntClimbShare` 0.7.
- **The pass** at `LungeSpeed` 3 to `-RamWidth - PassBeyond` (24 - the brake's way from that pace, so it stops unseen). `Arrive` there has no hiss, no jolt, no lamps, and the rumble runs on; it begins the turn (`BeginTurn`).
- **The throw in the pass**: `ThrowsOverTheHull` from the pass on - any touch on the road (`Overlaps`, not while he is out of its way): to the right, `HuntFlingWay` 3, and it stands `RamStandTicks` 50. `Shoves`, `BowAt`, `RamsOnTheRoad`, `HuntThrowSide`, `ShoveDeepest`, `PinRoom`, `THero.Shove` and `ResolveRamShove` are gone.
- **The turn** (`htTurning`): `HuntTurnTicks` 50 out there, standing; the hull faces right from its first tick (`FHullFacing`, `HullMirrored`). The eye blinks as a warning (`TickEye`); `PeekLevel` rises over the last `HuntPeekTicks` 16, and the painter throws the eye's red on the left edge (`DrawPeek`, `PeekSize` 40, at the eye's height).
- **The drive in** (`htEntering`, `TickHuntDrive`): never back, at most `LungeSpeed`, toward the hunt's mark but at least the left edge; within `EnterSlop` 1 of it the hunt begins.
- **The hunt** (`htHunting`, `TickHuntDrive`): the mark (`HuntMark`) puts the bow `HuntTrail` 24 behind his middle, within the screen; it closes `HuntGain` 0.15 of the gap a tick, at most `HuntBehindSpeed` 1.5, up and down by `Brake`. It backs only when he is behind its stern and not on a pad; otherwise it holds rather than goes back. After a ram it stands. The bow holds within `HuntLine`: `HuntMiddleLine` (the middle of the screen), and through a surge `HuntFarLine` (40 short of the right edge); past its line it goes back to it, at the lunge's pace. The surge (`THuntSurge`, `TickSurge`): every `HuntSurgeEveryLeastTicks` 200 to `HuntSurgeEveryMostTicks` 300 (`NextSurgeWait`), the eye's warning `HuntSurgeWarnTicks` 16 (`SurgeWarns`, `TickEye`), then `HuntSurgeTicks` 75 at the lunge's pace - long enough to get from the middle to near the right edge - and back. The bow never comes within his body of the right wall: the throw is always to the right.
- **The mirror.** `HullSpot(left, point)` - a point of the hull's art on the screen, mirrored about the hull's middle when turned; `WellAt(left)` - the well's middle. `MastPivot` and `HeadPoint` take the well's X now, so every head point follows the mirror; the heads keep their own facing. The body a touch meets is read mirrored (`SolidAt`, under `Overlaps` and `SpanAcross`); the body's middle, the shield's, is the hull's middle either way. The painter draws the well, the chassis, the wheels' places, the hull and the mast sections mirrored (`THullStand.Mirrored`), the eye and the lamps at their mirrored spots, and keeps a second pair of exhaust smokes thrown the other way (`TExhaustPair`, `FExhausts[mirrored]`); the pair of the other way stops puffing and clears. A start (`Place`) faces it left again.
- **Its death** in the hunt (after the run, "Decided 2026-11-04, after the run of 3.0.98"): `KillHead` leaves the hunt on for the bunker - the mast sinks where it stands (a pass under way runs on off the screen and the mast sinks out there), then `TickChange` hands it back to the hunt (`BeginRest`); one that had not begun - the bunker killed before its last third, the debug key - begins it there (`BeginHunt`). With no head nothing is lobbed and nothing is struck (`StrikeBunker`); the touch on the road is still a ram. Till the henshin, which is to stop it.
- **The shell's blast** (`ShellBlast`, `Game.Moon`): radius 40 in place of 80.
- **The hunt's shells**: `Lob` pairs in the second phase only; the hatch aims `HuntAimTicks` 35 in the hunt (`TickHatch`). `TRamCue.LobForRoad` - in the hunt and he is not on a pad; then `TickRam` aims at `TBunkerShells.RoadMark` - the nearest road point to his X, to either side by 8 up to 64, 16 off the edges, whose whole arc (`Aim`, `ReachesRoad`, the same solve as `Lob`) meets no deck first; none clear - the road under him. `TickRam` gives `SecondShellMark` only - `AheadShellMark` is kept in `Game.Shells`, unused.
- **The open top**: `OpenTop` - the hatch's point `OpenRowsDepth` 5.1 lower (a row of `Game.Shield` cells, 1.5 * `HexSize`); `StrikeBunker` takes the line there, `OpenAbove` strips the honeycomb down to it. `HatchPoint` stays the hatch, for the art and the column.
- **The roll** (`Game.Shells`): `TShell.RollWay` - the sign of the arc's way across as it lands; rolling, `RollSpeed` that way on top of the road; gone past either edge.
